# Fabric API mapping ledger

NeoForge 26.3's transfer API is the specification for parts: Fabric transfer maps almost entirely onto its transactions, component-carrying resources and index-based contents. This ledger maps transfer, registries and data if Fabric mods become port targets; event counterparts belong on individual native hooks, except the review notes under [Event counterparts: client lifecycle and ticks](#event-counterparts-client-lifecycle-and-ticks-1014), which record why a reviewed hook has no `fabric` key. It also records the amount conversion: 81,000 droplets per bucket versus 1,000 mB; conversions refuse fractional mB, never round.

This is a porting ledger, not a promise of binary or source compatibility. **Covered** means the named use has a usable counterpart, not that a whole Fabric module is identical. **Planned** means an explicitly identified design or tracker item, not shipped code. **Gap** means the particular semantics have no established equivalent. Multiple statuses on a row distinguish the individual claims.

Reference: Fabric API 26.3 branch at [`2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`](https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/tree/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222) (Apache-2.0); fork main `be1fa0d1ec89d07998e8ea7c72137ce483904d8c` (snapshot, not a current-status guarantee). NeoForge names below refer to its public 26.3 API, not its implementation: [`ResourceHandler`](https://aldak.netlify.app/javadoc/26.3.x/net/neoforged/neoforge/transfer/ResourceHandler.html), [`Transaction`](https://aldak.netlify.app/javadoc/26.3.x/net/neoforged/neoforge/transfer/transaction/Transaction.html), [`TransactionContext`](https://aldak.netlify.app/javadoc/26.3.x/net/neoforged/neoforge/transfer/transaction/TransactionContext.html), and [`SnapshotJournal`](https://aldak.netlify.app/javadoc/26.3.x/net/neoforged/neoforge/transfer/transaction/SnapshotJournal.html). The fork's parts contract is #41 §7. Paths below are relative to the repository.

## Transfer: `fabric-transfer-api-v1`

| Fabric API | Purpose | NeoForge counterpart | Fork counterpart and status | Material differences |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Storage<T>` | Insert/extract a resource and inspect a storage; capability/support queries and a version hint. | `ResourceHandler<T>` | **Covered in part:** `ResourceHandler<R>` (`crates/horizon-mod-vocab/src/content/resource.rs`), as `ResourceHandler<ItemResource>` for `ITEMS` (`crates/horizon-mod-vocab/src/item/capability.rs`) and `ITEM_ITEMS` (`crates/horizon-mod-vocab/src/block/item_place.rs`), and as `ResourceHandler<FluidResource>` for `FLUIDS` (`crates/horizon-mod-vocab/src/block/fluid_capability.rs`). | Fabric is generic, returns accepted/extracted `long` amounts and requires transaction contexts; NeoForge's `ResourceHandler` also uses `TransactionContext`. Fork handlers take a `&Transaction` and answer moved `u32` amounts, as NeoForge's do, for items and fluids alike; support and version hints have no direct equivalent. No generic Fabric adapter is implied. |
| `StorageView<T>`, `iterator()`, `nonEmptyIterator()`, `nonEmptyViews()` | Inspect each resource, amount and capacity and extract from a view; nonempty iteration filters blank/zero views. | `ResourceHandler<T>` indexed resource and capacity access | **Covered in part:** `size`, `resource`, `amount` and `capacity` by index on `ResourceHandler` (`resource.rs`); **gap:** Fabric view/iterator contract and underlying-view identity. | Fabric may visit a resource more than once; extraction during iteration must be visible, insertions need not be; a changed iterator may become invalid at outer transaction end. Index traversal and `Option` filtering are an approximation, not those guarantees. Fork capacity asks about a resource at an index; it is not directly a blank view's estimated bound. |
| `Transaction`, `TransactionContext`, nested `openNested`, commit and abort | Tentative, composable changes across participants; closing without commit aborts, inner commit remains subject to outer abort. | `Transaction`, `TransactionContext`, `SnapshotJournal` | **Covered:** `host.transaction(|host, tx| …)` and `&Transaction` (`crates/horizon-mod-vocab/src/item/transaction.rs`, #41 §7 T1–T3): nested in the open transaction, an `Ok` commits into the outer one and an `Err` aborts, and the game journals the block entities, cauldrons, players' and entities' inventories and item entities a write touches. Every item and fluid write takes `tx`; there is no `simulate`. | A mod's own storage joins by keeping its state where the game journals it, or by being a test host's whole state; there is no public `SnapshotJournal` participant trait yet. Fabric's outer-close callbacks have no counterpart: the game tells the world of changes at the root commit. |
| `FluidVariant`, `ItemVariant` | Resource identity and components separated from the transferred quantity. | `FluidResource`, `ItemResource` | **Covered:** `FluidResource` (`fluid_capability.rs`) and `ItemResource` (`resource.rs`), an id and its components without an amount; `FluidStack` and `ItemStack` are the forms with an amount (`to_stack`, `of`). | There is no blank variant: an empty index answers no resource. Retain component identity when adapting, do not compare only IDs. |
| `FluidConstants` | Amounts in droplets: 81,000 per bucket. | Fluid quantities in millibuckets | **Covered in part:** `FluidStack.amount` is `u32` mB, 1,000 per bucket (`fluid_capability.rs`). | 81 droplets = 1 mB. Arbitrary droplet amounts, including one droplet, cannot be represented exactly as integer mB; Fabric `long` range also exceeds `u32`. Reject amounts that are not whole mB, never round; silent rounding changes recipes and can create or destroy fluid. |
| `FluidStorage.SIDED` | Lookup a block storage by position and optional direction; null means unrestricted. | `Capabilities.Fluid.BLOCK` | **Covered in part:** `LevelRef::capability(host, &FLUIDS, pos, side)` and handler calls with `Option<Direction>` (`capability.rs`, `fluid_capability.rs`); mod registration in `parts/`. | Direction is passed to each handler method, not to lookup. The default covers mod tanks and cauldrons; a block capability key is not a Fabric lookup object and does not imply identical provider composition, invalidation or client behavior. |
| `FluidStorage.ITEM` | Query item fluid storage using a container context; Fabric provides buckets and water potions and supports combined providers. | `Capabilities.Fluid.ITEM`, `ItemAccess` | **Covered in part:** `ITEM_FLUIDS` (`fluid_capability.rs`, #271), a `ResourceHandler<FluidResource>` for the item at an `ItemPlace`, whose writes exchange one item; the game answers it for vanilla's buckets, one tank filled and drained whole. **Gap:** water potions, which Fabric answers and the game doesn't; and Fabric's combined providers, which merge every non-null storage where a first-answer-wins lookup can't. | Providers are asked in order, the first answer winning: those registered `before_game`, the game's own, then the others, each in registration order (`parts/`). Fabric's combined providers compose all non-null storages, so that lookup alone does not reproduce that composition. Exact droplet granularity remains a gap. |
| `ContainerItemContext` | Main slot, overflow and additional slots, exchange items transactionally; contexts include hand, cursor, single slot, creative and constant. | `ItemAccess`, particularly `exchange` | **Covered in part:** `ItemPlace`, `PlaceProvider`, `ItemPlace::{insert, extract, exchange}`, `ItemPlace::capability` (`item_place.rs`); **planned:** use with `ITEM_FLUIDS`. | `ItemPlace` locates a stack and is NeoForge's `ItemAccess` over it; hand overflow may drop and a block slot can refuse if no room. This is not Fabric's general transactional main/overflow/additional-slot API or its creative/constant context. Validate the current item before use. |
| `StorageUtil.move` | Filter resource variants and move up to a `long` total, using nested transactions, with optional enclosing transaction. | `ResourceHandlerUtil.move` | **Covered:** `mod_api::move_resources(host, &from, &to, filter, max, tx)` (`resource.rs`), for items and fluids: a filter over resources, several resources in one call, each move all or nothing in a transaction nested in the caller's `tx`, and a move the target takes only part of tried again with that part. | A handler answering more than it was asked has its move undone and gives `Overanswered`, naming the mod; returned with `?`, it aborts the caller's whole transaction, so nothing is lost or duplicated. Fabric returns the amount and leaves misbehaving storages to the caller. |
| Team Reborn Energy `EnergyStorage` (separate project, not Fabric API) | Electric energy storage and transfer on Fabric. | `Capabilities.Energy.BLOCK` / `ITEM`, `EnergyHandler` | **Covered in part:** `ENERGY` (`crates/horizon-mod-vocab/src/item/energy_capability.rs`), an `EnergyHandler` written in a transaction, answered by the game for a mod block's registrar-declared `block_energy` buffer as `SimpleEnergyHandler`, and `move_energy` as `EnergyHandlerUtil.move`; `ITEM_ENERGY`, the same handler for the item at an `ItemPlace`, answered only by mods. | Energy is not `fabric-transfer-api-v1`; verify Team Reborn's own version, units and transaction semantics before implementing an adapter. Neither a unit conversion nor transactional parity follows from an energy capability name. |

### The NeoForge floor

The counterpart for most transfer operations is NeoForge-shaped `ResourceHandler<R>` with index-based contents, component-carrying `ItemResource` and `FluidResource`, and nested `host.transaction` (#41 §7 T1–T3). Fabric-specific differences remain in view iteration, `ContainerItemContext` overflow/additional slots and creative/constant contexts, droplets, and `long` amounts.

### Design recommendations for item fluids and energy

1. Keep a place-backed item exchange whose split/overflow is written in the same transaction as the rest of the move; document the different creative, overflow and water-potion behavior rather than calling it Fabric parity. Allow a deliberate provider composition strategy if several mods need to expose one item.
2. Preserve resource components end-to-end for handlers that support them; the game's buckets and a mod block's saved tanks refuse a fluid with components, and a cauldron discards them. Refuse droplets not divisible by 81, never round; address amounts exceeding `u32` before promising lossless Fabric ports.
3. Item fluid and energy handlers take the same `&Transaction` as item and fluid handlers, so one transaction spans them all; keep it that way, including for item exchange overflow.
4. For energy, fork amounts are `u32` and the game's buffer saves an `i32`, with no unit of its own; inspect the chosen Team Reborn release before claiming compatibility. Provide view and filtering adapters only with clearly stated iteration and identity limits.

## Registries and data

Event counterparts belong on individual hooks; the exceptions are the review notes under [Event counterparts: client lifecycle and ticks](#event-counterparts-client-lifecycle-and-ticks-1014). These rows concern registry and data contracts; a partial counterpart does not imply whole-module compatibility.

| Fabric module and key API | Fork mapping and status | Porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `fabric-registry-sync-v0`: `FabricRegistryBuilder`, `RegistryEntryAddedCallback`, `RegistryIdRemapCallback`, `DynamicRegistries`, `DynamicRegistrySetupCallback` | **Covered in part:** code-declared blocks/items and synced component/particle types (#27 Not started: partly done code-side registration). **Planned:** block-entity type network IDs/builders and broader entity types, same #27 lines. **Gap:** generic custom synced registries, remap callbacks and arbitrary dynamic-registry codec/sync contract are not established by those slices. | Static code registration, datapack registries and a server-to-client remap are different operations. Do not infer full registry sync from matching IDs in one process. |
| `fabric-model-loading-api-v1`: `ModelLoadingPlugin`, `ModelModifier`, `BlockStateResolver`, `ExtraModelKey`, unbaked/baked wrappers | **Covered in part:** standalone models baked with block models (#27 Not started: model changes at bake time; Done portion). **Planned:** bake-time model changes / `ModelData` (#27 same line, design horizon-rust/horizon#6303). **Gap:** arbitrary Fabric plugin, resolver, wrapper and item/block model replacement contracts. | A standalone model registration does not mean a mod can intercept and replace any baked model. |
| `fabric-resource-conditions-api-v1`: condition registration and resource condition evaluation | **Covered in part:** `neoforge:conditions` in recipe loaders (`docs/modding/src/how-to/native-mods.md`, Conditional data). **Gap:** Fabric condition schema/custom registration and conditions on tags, loot and advancements; current unsupported conditions fail closed. | Syntax and evaluated resource domains are not interchangeable. |
| `fabric-api-lookup-api-v1`: `BlockApiLookup`, `ItemApiLookup`, `EntityApiLookup` | **Covered in part:** `CapabilityKey`, `ItemCapabilityKey`, `LevelRef::capability`/`ItemPlace::capability` (`mod_api/capability.rs`, `item_place.rs`, `parts/`). **Gap:** general Fabric lookup provider/fallback/cache composition and entity lookup contract. | — |
| `fabric-biome-api-v1`: `BiomeModifications`, `BiomeSelectors` | **Gap:** programmatic biome modification phases and selection contract; data-driven biomes alone do not provide these callbacks. | — |
| `fabric-content-registries-v0`: `FlammableBlockRegistry`, `OxidizableBlocksRegistry`, villager and fluid registries | **Covered in part:** `fire_odds` block part (#41 §2). **Gap:** registry-specific mod additions and their complete reload/order semantics. | — |
| `fabric-convention-tags-v2`: `ConventionalItemTags`, `ConventionalBlockTags`, fluid/entity tags | **Covered in part:** data-pack tag loading and game tag queries; **gap:** automatic equivalence of Fabric's conventional tag namespace, contents and tooling. | — |
| `fabric-data-attachment-api-v1`: `AttachmentRegistry`, `AttachmentType`, sync predicates | **Covered in part:** typed `AttachmentType` and holder data (#41 §1, attachment codecs, save/sync). **Gap:** generic Fabric registry, predicates and lifecycle parity for all holders must be verified separately. | — |
| `fabric-data-generation-api-v1`: `FabricDataGenerator`, recipe/loot/language providers | **Gap:** Fabric's provider framework and generator integration. Existing data files or developer tools are not this API. | — |
| `fabric-object-builder-api-v1`: `FabricBlockEntityTypeBuilder`, `FabricDefaultAttributeRegistry`, `FabricEntityType` | **Covered in part:** code-side block/item declarations (#27 registration line). **Planned:** block-entity builder/network IDs and broader mod entities (#27 Not started: registration and mod entity types). **Gap:** remaining builders and default-attribute registration parity. | — |
| `fabric-recipe-api-v1`: `CustomIngredient`, `CustomIngredientSerializer`, `RecipeSynchronization` | **Covered in part:** `native_ingredient`, `native_ingredient_test` and recipe synchronization (`docs/modding/src/how-to/native-mods.md`, custom ingredients). **Gap:** all Fabric ingredient serializers, synchronization rules and custom recipe forms are not implied. | — |
| `fabric-serialization-api-v1`: `MoreCodecs`, `FabricValueInput`, `FabricValueOutput` | **Gap:** Fabric's codec/value-input extensions; existing Rust serialization and attachment codecs are different APIs. | — |
| `fabric-tag-api-v1`: `FabricTagFile`, `ClientTags` | **Covered in part:** vanilla datapack tags and the `tags_updated` notice on both sides, with the server's block, item and fluid tags readable on a client. **Gap:** Fabric's tag-file extensions and the rest of `ClientTags`' semantics. | — |
| `fabric-dimensions-v1`: `DimensionEvents.MODIFY_ATTRIBUTES` | **Gap:** no equivalent dimension-attribute modification contract established; this event is not a level lifecycle event. | — |

## Event counterparts: client lifecycle and ticks (#1014)

Checked against Fabric API 26.3 at `2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`
(Apache-2.0, including the headers of the files below). **Covered** here means a
verified event moment, logical side, listener inputs and non-cancelling contract;
it does not expose Java objects or promise the whole Fabric API. The client object
passed by Fabric is represented by the generated `ClientHost`, not an extra event
field. Both client tick events have no other payload, return `void`, and iterate
all listeners; Horizon uses `contract = "listen"` and no cancellation output.

Scope: every hook in `client_setup.rs`, `client_stopping.rs`, `client_stopped.rs`,
`client_tick_pre.rs`, `client_tick_post.rs`, `client_player_logging_in.rs` and
`client_player_logging_out.rs`, plus the unmapped server lifecycle declarations
`world_lifecycle`, `server_about_to_start`, `server_starting`, `server_started` and
`server_stopped` in `base.rs`. The four existing Fabric mappings in `base.rs` and
its unrelated hook families are unchanged and are not certified by this review.
Other client-lifecycle neighbours (`client_player_clone`,
`client_resources_reloaded` and `game_shutting_down`) are outside the chosen files
and keep their existing metadata. Paths in the Horizon column are under
`crates/horizon-client/src/`.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `client_setup` | **Gap:** `ClientLifecycleEvents.CLIENT_STARTED` | [Client lifecycle API][cl-api] lines 28–37, 51–54 and [client mixin][cl-mixin] lines 49–53 fire in `Minecraft.run` just after `gameThread` is assigned, while the splash is displayed. Horizon fires it in `start_game` (`game.rs`) after initial resources have applied. Both are client-only notices with client context, but the resource-readiness moment differs. No key. |
| `client_stopping` | **Gap:** `ClientLifecycleEvents.CLIENT_STOPPING` | [Client lifecycle API][cl-api] lines 39–49, 56–59 explicitly precedes integrated-server stopping; [client mixin][cl-mixin] lines 44–47 injects in `exitWorldAndClose` after logging. Horizon's `Game::shut_down` (`game.rs`) calls `save_for_exit` first, then notifies with client context. Same side and non-cancellation, different ordering. No key; corrected the hook comment's former counterpart claim. |
| `client_stopped` | **Gap:** none | [Client lifecycle API][cl-api] lines 24–60 defines only started/stopping, no completion event. Horizon's `run_event_loop` (`game.rs`) notifies after its event loop and shutdown services. `CLIENT_STOPPING` is earlier, not an alias for this client-only notice. |
| `client_tick_pre` | **Covered:** `ClientTickEvents.START_CLIENT_TICK` | [Client tick API][ct-api] lines 29–36, 67–70 passes only the client and returns `void`; [client mixin][cl-mixin] lines 34–37 injects at `Minecraft.tick` HEAD without cancellation. Horizon fires it at the start of each step of the client tick loop in `Game::handle_window_event_inner` (`game.rs`), outside the title/pause guard; `Game::native_client_tick_pre` (`game/native_hooks/`) forwards without extra payload. Generated client host supplies the client context. |
| `client_tick_post` | **Covered:** `ClientTickEvents.END_CLIENT_TICK` | [Client tick API][ct-api] lines 38–45, 72–75 and [client mixin][cl-mixin] lines 39–42: client-only, client context only, `void`, at each successful `Minecraft.tick` RETURN. Horizon fires it at the end of each step of the same loop in `Game::handle_window_event_inner`, after the tick and client UI/audio work, outside the title/pause guard; errors return before it. `Game::native_client_tick_post` (`game/native_hooks/`) forwards without extra payload. |
| `client_player_logging_in` | **Gap:** `ClientPlayConnectionEvents.INIT` / `JOIN` | [Connection API][connection-api] lines 31–52, 68–76 passes `(listener, client)` for INIT and `(listener, sender, client)` for JOIN, both `void`. [Play mixin][play-mixin] lines 46–57 and [play addon][play-addon] lines 52–67 fire INIT after packet-listener construction and JOIN after `handleLogin` returns, before initial channel registration. Horizon notifies from the login packet's arm of `Game::handle_packet` (`game/network/remote/mod.rs`) after level/player login setup; `Game::native_client_player_logging_in` (`game/native_hooks/`) supplies entity ID and UUID plus client host, not a packet listener and sender. INIT is also earlier. No key, even though JOIN is a similar login-completion notice. |
| `client_player_logging_out` | **Gap:** `ClientPlayConnectionEvents.DISCONNECT` | [Connection API][connection-api] lines 54–63, 78–81 passes `(listener, client)`, returns `void` and forbids packet sends. [Connection mixin][connection-mixin] lines 89–101 reaches the addon at channel inactivity or before `PacketListener.onDisconnect`; [network addon][network-addon] lines 158–162 deduplicates and invokes before ending the session, then [play addon][play-addon] lines 96–99 fires DISCONNECT. Horizon's `Game::client_logging_out`, called first in `Game::leave_server` (`game/network/remote/mod.rs`), notifies before game teardown with optional player ID/UUID (`Game::native_client_player_logging_out`), not a packet listener. The transport boundary and arguments are not established as equivalent. No key. |
| `world_lifecycle` | **Gap:** none for the complete contract | [Server lifecycle API][sl-api] lines 31–78, 128–146 has separate non-cancelling start/stop events taking a server, not Horizon's lifecycle context, phase and error text (`Game::native_world_lifecycle` in `game/native_hooks/`). In particular it does not provide the `WorldOpenFailed` / `WorldCloseFailed` error notices of this hook. No key for a partial phase overlap. |
| `server_about_to_start` | **Gap:** `ServerLifecycleEvents.SERVER_STARTING` | [Server mixin][server-mixin] lines 56–59 fires before `initServer`; [server lifecycle API][sl-api] lines 31–40, 128–131 supplies the server, returns `void`, before player list/levels load. Horizon's `Game::run_dedicated_inner` (`game/interface/dedicated/mod.rs`) already loads server lists before this `loadLevel` boundary; `Game::integrated_open` (`game/world/integrated.rs`) likewise places it at world opening, after the mods' server configs are read. Same logical server side, later moment. No key. |
| `server_starting` | **Gap:** `ServerLifecycleEvents.SERVER_STARTING` | Same Fabric evidence as above. Horizon fires immediately after `server_about_to_start` (in `Game::run_dedicated_inner` and `Game::integrated_open`), not before `initServer`. `Game::native_server_starting` (`game/native_hooks/`) passes a `dedicated` flag and server host, with no veto. The matching name is not evidence of the matching point. No key. |
| `server_started` | **Gap:** `ServerLifecycleEvents.SERVER_STARTED` (not proven) | [Server lifecycle API][sl-api] lines 42–51, 133–136 is a server-only `void` notice with server context, after levels load and before ticking; [server mixin][server-mixin] lines 61–65 places it immediately before the first `buildServerStatus`. Horizon's `Game::run_integrated_inner` (`game/world/integrated.rs`, right after `integrated_open`) and `Game::run_dedicated_inner` (`game/interface/dedicated/mod.rs`, after the initial save) call `Game::native_lifecycle_world_ready` (`game/modding/native_lifecycle.rs`) to mark world-open readiness, forwarding server host and `dedicated` (`Game::native_server_started`). These establish the same broad phase, but Horizon has no `buildServerStatus` call at its world-ready point, so the orderings cannot be shown equal. No key. |
| `server_stopped` | **Gap:** `ServerLifecycleEvents.SERVER_STOPPED` | [Server lifecycle API][sl-api] lines 67–78, 143–146 supplies the server and returns `void`; [server mixin][server-mixin] lines 72–75 injects at `stopServer` TAIL. Horizon's `Game::shutdown_started_server` (`game/world/server_lifecycle/mod.rs`) also notifies after teardown errors or listener panics; a thrown exception does not reach a Fabric TAIL injection. The successful shutdown phase resembles it, but failure coverage differs. No key. |

`ClientTickEvents.START_LEVEL_TICK` and `END_LEVEL_TICK` are not aliases for the
client tick hooks. They pass a `ClientLevel`, not a client ([client tick API][ct-api]
lines 47–65, 77–85), and fire at `ClientLevel.tickEntities` HEAD and `ClientLevel.tick`
RETURN ([client level mixin][level-mixin] lines 30–38). They require a level and do
not wrap the whole client tick in menus or while paused. No additional key is added.

The host argument is generated by `host_param` in `crates/horizon-mod-api-gen/src/host_param.rs`;
empty hook declarations do not mean the listener lacks client context. This change
adds metadata only: no dispatch, tick-time work, adapter, alias or gameplay changes.

## Event counterparts: effects, interaction, damage and chat (#1014)

Checked against the same Fabric API 26.3 revision,
`2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Fabric line numbers below are at that
revision; Horizon code is cited by function name, under `crates/horizon-client/src/`.

Scope: every hook in `mob_effect_events.rs`, `right_click_item.rs`,
`use_item_on_block.rs`, `living_damage_post.rs`, `living_death.rs`, `client_chat.rs`,
`client_chat_received.rs` and `server_chat.rs`. None of them is **Covered**: each
Fabric event below shares the broad moment, but at least one of firing point,
listener inputs or cancel behaviour differs, so no `fabric` key is added. The most
common difference is the result shape. Fabric's `ALLOW_*` events can only veto (any
listener returning `false` wins), and its interaction callbacks treat `PASS` as "go
on". Several Horizon hooks can also force an outcome or edit the payload, and a
Horizon cancel that answers `Pass` still skips vanilla.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `mob_effect_applicable` | **Gap:** `ServerMobEffectEvents.ALLOW_ADD` | [Effect mixin][effect-mixin] lines 51–62 wrap `LivingEntity.canBeAffected` on the server, the same check Horizon's `Game::can_be_affected_resolved` (`game/items/potions.rs`, behind `Game::can_be_affected`) wraps. But [effect events][effect-api] lines 54–62 and 165–175 only veto: `true` defers to vanilla, so a listener cannot let in an effect vanilla's tag check refuses. Horizon's `replace` contract lets the first answer `Allow` override that check. Fabric's listener also gets the whole `MobEffectInstance` and an `EffectEventContext`; Horizon gives the effect's `EffectKey` only. No key. |
| `mob_effect_expired` | **Gap:** none | Fabric has no expiry veto. [Effect events][effect-api] lines 92–110 say `ALLOW_EARLY_REMOVE` is not called on expiry, and [effect mixin][effect-mixin] lines 175–189 fire only the non-cancelling `BEFORE_REMOVE` in `tickEffects`. Horizon's hook, fired when an effect counts down (`Game::tick_mob_effects` and `Game::effect_expired` in `game/items/potions.rs`), can cancel and keep the effect. No key. |
| `mob_effect_remove` | **Gap:** `ServerMobEffectEvents.ALLOW_EARLY_REMOVE` | Both veto an early removal on the server: [effect mixin][effect-mixin] lines 134–154 wrap `removeEffect`, and lines 107–132 ask per effect in `removeAllEffects`. In `removeAllEffects` Fabric clears the map first, asks each listener, then puts refused effects back. Horizon's `Game::remove_player_effects` (`game/items/potions.rs`) and `Game::living_remove_all_effects` (`game/modding/living_effects.rs`) ask before each removal with the effect still in place. Fabric passes the `MobEffectInstance` and `EffectEventContext`; Horizon passes the effect's `EffectKey` and amplifier. No key. |
| `right_click_item` | **Gap:** `UseItemCallback` | [Server interaction mixin][interaction-server] lines 96–105 fire at `ServerPlayerGameMode.useItem` HEAD, before vanilla's spectator and cooldown checks. [Client interaction mixin][interaction-client] lines 117–131 fire before the first packet in `MultiPlayerGameMode.useItem`. Horizon's `Game::right_click_item` (`game/native_hooks/`) fires immediately before `Item.use`, and only for an air click (not when a block or entity is aimed). [Use item callback][use-item-api] lines 32–34 treat `PASS` as "go on"; a Horizon cancel answering `Pass` still skips the item's Part and vanilla. No key. |
| `use_item_on_block` | **Gap:** `UseBlockCallback` | [Server interaction mixin][interaction-server] lines 85–94 fire once at `ServerPlayerGameMode.useItemOn` HEAD; [client interaction mixin][interaction-client] lines 98–115 fire once before the client's prediction. Horizon's `Game::use_item_on_block` (`game/native_hooks/`) fires once per chosen phase (item before block, block, item after block), after the phase is picked. Its cancel answers `cancellation_result`, initially `TryWithEmptyHand`, where [use block callback][use-block-api] lines 33–35 make `PASS` go on. The single entry point is closer to `right_click_block`, which was not part of this review. No key. |
| `living_damage_post` | **Gap:** `ServerLivingEntityEvents.AFTER_DAMAGE` | [Living mixin][living-mixin] lines 87–92 fire at `hurtServer` TAIL, skipped when the entity is dead or dying, and also after a hit a shield blocked. [Living events][living-api] lines 46–60 and 124–137 pass raw `baseDamageTaken`, `damageTaken` and `blocked`. Horizon's `damage_post` (`game/native_hooks/`, called from the hurt code in `mobs.rs` and `health.rs`) fires at the end of `hurtServer` once per hit that passed the cooldown, as NeoForge's Post does. Lethal and blocked hits are included (health damage 0 when none was taken), and it passes the damage container. No key. |
| `living_death` | **Gap:** `ServerLivingEntityEvents.ALLOW_DEATH` | [Living mixin][living-mixin] lines 75–78 redirect the fatal `isDeadOrDying` check inside `hurtServer`, before the totem of undying ([living events][living-api] lines 140–150), and only for deaths from damage. A veto leaves health at 0, and the entity dies next tick unless the listener heals it (lines 62–74). Horizon fires at the start of `LivingEntity.die` (`Game::mob_death_canceled` in `game/mobs/creatures/mod.rs`, `Game::guest_die` in `game/network/lan_actions/respawn.rs`), after the totem. Version 2 carries the complete accepted `HurtSource` and optional killing weapon; cancellation leaves listener-written health untouched and suppresses loot, with an unhealed body removed after 20 ticks without redispatch. No key. |

| `client_chat` | **Gap:** `ClientSendMessageEvents.ALLOW_CHAT` / `MODIFY_CHAT` | [Client send mixin][send-mixin] lines 36–45 fire at `ClientPacketListener.sendChat` HEAD, for any caller. Horizon's `Game::send_chat_from_screen` (`game/network/remote/mod.rs`) fires in `ChatScreen.handleChatInput`, for typed chat only. Fabric splits the veto and the edit into two events ([client send events][send-api] lines 37–45 and 75–81), each listener seeing only the text; Horizon's one hook does both and adds `original_message`. No key. |
| `client_chat_received` | **Gap:** `ClientReceiveMessageEvents.ALLOW_CHAT` / `ALLOW_GAME` / `MODIFY_GAME` | [Chat listener mixin][receive-mixin] lines 41–68 veto player and disguised chat in `ChatListener.showMessageToPlayer` (twice when a filtered copy exists); lines 70–90 veto and edit system and overlay messages at `handleSystemMessage` / `handleOverlay` HEAD. [Client receive events][receive-api] lines 47–91 have no edit for player chat. Horizon's `Game::chat_received` (`game/network/remote/chat_received.rs`) is one hook for all of them, and it can replace a player chat line. No key. |
| `server_chat` | **Gap:** `ServerMessageEvents.ALLOW_CHAT_MESSAGE` | [Player list mixin][player-list-mixin] lines 44–52 veto at `PlayerList.broadcastChatMessage` HEAD with the decorated `PlayerChatMessage`. Lines 64–87 also ask it for command messages (`/say`, `/me`) a player sent. Horizon fires it from the `ChatSubmitted` arm of `Game::lan_process_events` (`game/network/lan/mod.rs`) for typed chat only, with the raw text, an editable `message` and `replies` to the sender. Fabric's listener can veto but not edit. No key. |

## Event counterparts: server ticks, levels and chunks (#1014)

Checked against the same Fabric API 26.3 revision,
`2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Fabric line numbers below are at that
revision; Horizon code is cited by function name, under `crates/horizon-client/src/`
unless the file says otherwise.

Scope: `base.rs`'s `server_stopping`, `server_tick`, `level_tick_pre`,
`level_tick_post`, `level_load`, `level_unload`, `chunk_load` and `chunk_unload`.
Four of them carried a `fabric` key on main and are re-verified here like the new
ones: `server_stopping` and `server_tick` still match and keep their keys;
`level_tick_pre` and `level_tick_post` do not, and their keys are removed.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `server_stopping` | **Covered:** `ServerLifecycleEvents.SERVER_STOPPING` | [Server lifecycle API][sl-api] lines 53–65 declare a non-cancelling event that passes only the server, and say it fires before the network channel closes, before any player disconnects, with all levels still present. [Server mixin][server-mixin] lines 67–70 inject at `MinecraftServer.stopServer` HEAD. Horizon's `Game::shutdown_started_server` (`game/world/server_lifecycle/mod.rs`) calls `Game::native_server_stopping` before the failure notices, the final save and player removal, and `Game::run_server_lifecycle` reaches it whenever startup began, including a failed init or tick — the same coverage as vanilla's `runServer` finally reaching `stopServer`. The generated server host stands in for Fabric's `MinecraftServer`, and `dedicated` is the fact `MinecraftServer.isDedicatedServer()` gives a Fabric listener. Side, moment, the worlds-loaded/players-connected state and the non-cancelling contract match. Key kept. |
| `server_tick` | **Covered:** `ServerTickEvents.START_SERVER_TICK` and `ServerTickEvents.END_SERVER_TICK` | [Server tick API][st-api] lines 37–57 and 84–93 declare two non-cancelling events that pass only the server and are not invoked while a dedicated server is paused (lines 28–31, 40, 51). [Server mixin][server-mixin] lines 77–79 inject before `tickChildren` in `tickServer`, lines 82–84 at its TAIL. Horizon's `Game::tick_server` (`game/world/levels.rs`) computes `running` from pause and an agent hold and calls `Game::native_lifecycle_server_tick_start` before `levels_to_tick`, then `native_lifecycle_server_tick_end` after the last level and `Game::close_idle_levels`; `Game::native_server_tick` (`game/native_hooks/base.rs`) forwards the phase and context. A paused or held pass fires nothing, as Fabric documents; a frozen tick without a requested step fires with `world_running: false`, the state Fabric's listener reads from the tick rate manager. Both events' moments, side, no-cancel contract and server context match; the phase names separate the two moments in the one hook, and the remaining context fields are server-derivable. Keys kept. |
| `level_tick_pre` | **Gap:** `ServerTickEvents.START_LEVEL_TICK` | [Server tick API][st-api] lines 59–68 and 94–98 pass only the level; [server level mixin][server-level-mixin] lines 34–36 inject inside `ServerLevel.tick`, after `handlingTick` is set and before the level's own work. Horizon calls `Game::native_level_tick_pre` from `Game::tick_server` before `Game::update_world` and the pass's tick. For the first level that pass is `Game::tick` (`game.rs`), which runs the tick's shared parts (the autosaves, `Game::tick_functions`, `Game::advance_clock`) after the hook and before `Game::tick_world`; Horizon's own comment in `Game::tick` places vanilla's `commandFunctions` in `tickChildren` before any level ticks, so a Fabric listener has passed the function phase where a Horizon one has not. Horizon also fires only for `levels_to_tick` — levels selected by player simulation, forced chunks, container viewers, beds, game tests, pre-generation or portal arrivals/tickets, with the Overworld as the fallback when none qualify — while Fabric's event wraps every `ServerLevel.tick`; `docs/server-levels.md` records that vanilla ticks every level and Horizon parks the empty Nether and End. Firing point and firing count differ. Key removed. |
| `level_tick_post` | **Gap:** `ServerTickEvents.END_LEVEL_TICK` | [Server tick API][st-api] lines 70–77 and 98–101 pass only the level; [server level mixin][server-level-mixin] lines 39–41 inject at `ServerLevel.tick` TAIL, skipped when the tick throws. Horizon calls `Game::native_level_tick_post` only after a successful pass, which matches that rule, but only for `levels_to_tick`: a level Horizon parks gets no per-level tick and therefore no event, while `docs/server-levels.md` records every vanilla level being ticked. The set of levels notified differs, so the moments are not the same. Key removed. |
| `level_load` | **Gap:** `ServerLevelEvents.LOAD` | [Server level API][server-level-api] lines 27–36 and 51–53 declare `(MinecraftServer, ServerLevel)` "just after a level is loaded by a Minecraft server"; [server mixin][server-mixin] lines 87–93 fire it from the `Map.put` in `createLevels`, for each level the server creates at startup. Horizon's `Game::native_level_opened` (`game/world/levels.rs`) fires from `Game::finish_open_world` (`game.rs`) for the dimension the world opens in, and from `Game::enter_level` the first time any other dimension is entered; the Nether and End open when someone first travels there, not with the world. Only the opening level has a comparable moment; the count and the time for the other levels differ. The dimension ID and generated server host identify the level context, which the mapping rule permits in place of Fabric's server/level context. No key. |
| `level_unload` | **Gap:** `ServerLevelEvents.UNLOAD` | [Server level API][server-level-api] lines 38–48 and 56–58 declare `(MinecraftServer, ServerLevel)` "before a level is unloaded by a Minecraft server", typically after the server started shutting down; [server mixin][server-mixin] lines 95–98 inject at the `ServerLevel.close()` call inside `stopServer`, before the close runs. Horizon's `Game::native_level_closed` fires after `Game::save_level` and the chunk unload hooks in `Game::close_idle_levels`, and after teardown in `Game::native_levels_closing`; it also fires when a non-Overworld level closes for being empty after 300 idle ticks, a close vanilla's `stopServer` path does not have. The before-close and after-close points differ. Fabric also explicitly permits dynamic level mods to invoke UNLOAD (API lines 41–42), so an idle close alone is not a categorical mismatch; Horizon still notifies after teardown. No key. |
| `chunk_load` | **Gap:** `ServerChunkEvents.CHUNK_LOAD` | [Server chunk API][server-chunk-api] lines 34–47 and 96–98 declare `(ServerLevel, LevelChunk, boolean generated)`: "when a chunk is loaded into a ServerLevel", already in the level, and not for chunks that become accessible without being unloaded. [Chunk status mixin][chunk-status-mixin] lines 41–48 fire it at the TAIL of the full-status task, once per chunk as it becomes a `LevelChunk`, with `generated` false for a chunk read from the save. Horizon queues `(x, z, new)` in `Streaming::receive_generated` (`crates/horizon-client-render/src/streaming.rs`) as each chunk enters the level, and `Game::native_chunks_loaded` (`game/native_hooks/shared.rs`) drains the queue at the end of the streaming commit, from `Game::update_world` (`game.rs`) or `Game::close_idle_levels`, so all chunks of one commit are reported together after the commit. The listener also gets the dimension ID and the chunk's coordinates, not the `LevelChunk`. Firing point and argument shape differ. No key. |
| `chunk_unload` | **Gap:** `ServerChunkEvents.CHUNK_UNLOAD` | [Server chunk API][server-chunk-api] lines 63–76 and 106–108 pass `(ServerLevel, LevelChunk)` while the chunk is still present in the level; [chunk map mixin][chunk-map-mixin] lines 47–50 inject at the `ChunkMap.save(chunk)` call in `scheduleUnload`, before that save runs. Horizon's `Game::native_chunks_unloaded` (`game/native_hooks/shared.rs`) fires after the save: `Game::update_world` calls `save_later` for the modified unloaded chunks before it, `Game::close_idle_levels` calls `Game::save_level` first, and `Game::native_chunk_unload`'s own comment says changes made in the hook are not saved. A Fabric listener's edits can therefore reach the save and a Horizon one's cannot. The firing point differs, and the listener gets the dimension ID and coordinates instead of the chunk. No key. |

Fabric's level-tick and level listeners receive the `ServerLevel` context. Under
the mapping rule, Horizon's generated `ServerHost` may stand in for server/level
context; a dimension ID is not by itself an argument mismatch. Fabric's chunk
listeners additionally receive a `LevelChunk` data object. Coordinates and the
`new` flag do not replace that data object, so the chunk rows retain a separate
argument gap as well as their firing-point gaps.

Horizon's `Game::tick` comment and `docs/server-levels.md` are Horizon's own
statements of the vanilla ordering and level set used above; no decompiled 26.3 sources
were read for this review. The change is metadata only: no dispatch, tick-time work,
adapter, alias or gameplay change.

[server-chunk-api]: https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/blob/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222/fabric-lifecycle-events-v1/src/main/java/net/fabricmc/fabric/api/event/lifecycle/v1/ServerChunkEvents.java

## Event counterparts: player connection (#1014)

Checked against the same Fabric API 26.3 revision,
`2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Fabric line numbers below are at that
revision; Horizon code is cited by function name, under `crates/horizon-client/src/`
unless the file says otherwise.

Scope: `base.rs`'s `player_login`, `player_load_from_file`, `player_logged_in`,
`player_logged_out`, `player_save_to_file`, `player_client_loaded_world` and
`player_kicked`, and the one hook in `client_information_updated.rs`,
`client_information_updated`. All eight record a gap; no keys are added.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `player_logged_in` | **Gap:** `ServerPlayerEvents.JOIN` | [Server player events][sp-api] lines 52–57 declare a non-cancelling notice that passes the `ServerPlayer` and is "called on the server thread after the player has fully been loaded into the world", singleplayer worlds included; [server player list mixin][sp-mixin] lines 46–49 inject at `PlayerList.placeNewPlayer` RETURN. Horizon's `Game::native_player_logged_in` fires from the `HostEvent::Joined` arm of `Game::lan_process_events` (`game/network/lan/mod.rs`) after `Game::lan_guest_joined` has called `Hub::place_new_player`, the port of `placeNewPlayer` including its join line, and only for a placed guest; `Game::arrival_placed` (`game/interface/dedicated/transfer.rs`) fires it for a held arrival once that player is placed. Once per join, on the server thread, after the player is in the world and listed and before the next tick, with no cancel on either side. Fabric passes the `ServerPlayer` entity; Horizon passes only UUID and name. The generated host may replace server/level context, but not an entity data argument. Arguments differ. No key. |
| `player_logged_out` | **Gap:** `ServerPlayerEvents.LEAVE` | [Server player events][sp-api] lines 64–69 say "called on the server thread before the player has been saved and removed from the world", and [server player list mixin][sp-mixin] lines 51–54 fire it at `PlayerList.remove` HEAD, so vanilla's stat award, save and removal all follow it. Horizon's leaving path, the `HostEvent::Left` arm of `Game::lan_process_events`, calls `Game::save_guest` — which writes the player's file — and drops the player from the hub's play set before `Game::native_player_logged_out`; only the stop path, `Game::native_players_leaving`, runs before `Game::save_leaving_guests` and so fires before a save. One Horizon hook therefore reports two different points, and the ordinary leave is after the save that Fabric's listener precedes: a Fabric listener's edits can reach the player file, a Horizon one's cannot. No key. |
| `player_login` | **Gap:** none; nearest `ServerLoginConnectionEvents.INIT` / `QUERY_START` / `DISCONNECT` | [Server login connection events][slc-api] lines 31–35, 42–49 and 56–60 are void notices in the LOGIN state with no cancellation return value; [server play connection events][splay-api] lines 31–35 (INIT) is a void notice in the PLAY state, where the player already exists. These callbacks do not expose Horizon's cancel-and-reason contract. Horizon's `Game::native_player_login`, from the `HostEvent::Joined` arm, fires after the connection's own checks (name, whitelist, bans, a full server, the proxy's forwarding) and after `Hub::admit` has given the player a place, and its `cancel` disconnects them with a reason and stops the join. No equivalent cancellable login event is proven. No key. |
| `player_load_from_file` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API has no player-data load event; [Server player events][sp-api] lines 52–57 fire only once the data has been read and the player placed. Horizon's `Game::native_player_load_from_file` fires inside `Game::lan_guest_joined`, right after `playerdata::load` or `playerdata::load_owner` applied the player's `.dat` and before placement, and hands the listener the world's player-data directory. No key. |
| `player_save_to_file` | **Gap:** none; nearest `ServerLifecycleEvents.BEFORE_SAVE` / `AFTER_SAVE` | [Server lifecycle API][sl-api] lines 111–122 are the whole-server save notices at `MinecraftServer.saveAllChunks` HEAD and TAIL ([server mixin][server-mixin] lines 114–121): they pass the server and the `flush` and `force` flags, fire once per save pass whether or not a given player's file is written, and do not report the asynchronous per-player writes. Horizon's `Game::native_player_save_to_file` fires once per written player file — from `Game::save_guest` when a player leaves, from `Game::finish_player_autosaves` when an autosave's write completes, from `Game::save_game_metadata` for the integrated owner, and from `Game::place_arrival` for a held arrival — and passes the player-data directory. No key. |
| `player_client_loaded_world` | **Gap:** none | Fabric has no notice for the client's world-loaded acknowledgement; the nearest, `ServerPlayConnectionEvents.JOIN` ([server play connection events][splay-api] lines 42–46), fires while the player is still being placed and only once per join ([network player list mixin][network-player-list] lines 33–35, before the abilities packet is constructed). Horizon's `Game::native_player_client_loaded_world` fires from the `HostEvent::PlayerLoaded` arm of `Game::lan_process_events`, which `Hub::drain_packets` raises for the guest's `PlayerLoaded` packet after joining, respawning or changing dimension. No key. |
| `player_kicked` | **Gap:** none; nearest `ServerPlayConnectionEvents.DISCONNECT` | [Server play connection events][splay-api] lines 53–57 declare a void notice for "the disconnection of the server game packet listener"; [connection mixin][conn-mixin] lines 89–99 fire it from `Connection.handleDisconnection` and `channelInactive`, before the listener's own `onDisconnect`, for every disconnect a connection can have, and pass the listener and the server, not a reason. Horizon's `Game::native_player_kicked` fires from the `HostEvent::Kicked` arm of `Game::lan_process_events`, which `Hub::kick` queues after it disconnected that guest with a reason, and only for a kick. Different scope, point and arguments. No key. |
| `client_information_updated` | **Gap:** none | No Fabric API 26.3 event covers the play-state `ClientInformation` packet; no API package in the pinned tree declares one. Horizon's `Game::native_client_information_updated` (`game/native_hooks/client_information_updated.rs`) fires from the `HostEvent::ClientInformation` arm of `Game::lan_process_events`, which `Hub::drain_packets` raises after the packet's language, view distance, chat, skin parts, main hand, filtering, listing and particles were applied, with the values before and after, and from `Game::announce_login` once for the information the client joined with, from the defaults, just before `player_logged_in`. No key. |

`Game::lan_guest_joined` and `Hub::place_new_player` are the port of
`PlayerList.placeNewPlayer`, and the save ordering above is Fabric's own javadoc
statement about `PlayerList.remove`; no decompiled 26.3 sources are present in
`.local/` to confirm vanilla's ordering independently. The change is metadata
only: no dispatch, tick-time work, adapter, alias or gameplay change.

## Event counterparts: loot and drops (#1014)

Checked against the same Fabric API 26.3 revision,
`2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Fabric line numbers below are at that
revision; Horizon code is cited by function name (under
`crates/horizon-client/src/`, or `crates/horizon-world/src/` for the loot
engine) and never by line number.

Scope: `base.rs`'s `loot_table_load`, `loot_drops`, `loot_function` and
`loot_condition`, and the hooks in `block_drops.rs` (`block_drops`),
`living_drops.rs` (`living_drops`, `get_equipment_drop_chance`) and
`living_experience_drop.rs` (`living_experience_drop`). This is the loot part of
`base.rs`, after the server connection, chunk, level, entity load, player
connection, and effects/interaction/damage/chat parts in the earlier sections.
All eight record a gap; none of these reviewed hooks carries a key and none is added.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `loot_table_load` | **Gap:** `LootTableEvents.REPLACE` and `LootTableEvents.MODIFY` | [Loot table events][lt-api] lines 44–94 declare two separate events at one load site: `REPLACE` returns a replacement `LootTable` (or null) and stops the iteration for that table, while `MODIFY` hands every listener a `LootTable.Builder` and returns void; both receive `(ResourceKey<LootTable>, table/builder, LootTableSource, HolderLookup.Provider)`. [Loot util][lt-util] lines 57–70 runs `REPLACE` and then `MODIFY`, and `determineSource` (the same file, below line 70) says whether the file was `VANILLA`, `MOD`, `DATA_PACK` or `REPLACED`. [Resource load task mixin][lt-load-task] lines 49–72 fires them per table resource immediately after that resource is deserialised and before the table enters the registry; [all-loaded mixin][lt-reload-mixin] lines 48–58 fires `ALL_LOADED` after the whole registry load. Horizon has one hook, `loot_table_load` (`collect`, `out = "table"`), applied by `LootData::apply_native_table_loads` in `horizon-world`'s `loot.rs` after `LootData::load` has read every file: it loops the loaded snapshot in id order and calls `native_loot_table_load` for each table, from `Game::load_server_loot` at world open and from `Game::reload_world_tables` on `/reload`. One Horizon invocation both edits the JSON and may replace the table, so it does the two Fabric events' jobs in one moment instead of Fabric's split replace-then-modify pair, and its `DataLoadContext` (`phase.rs`: load origin, registry/fluid lookups, settings and mods) has no per-table `LootTableSource`, so a Fabric listener can ask `source.isBuiltin()` or distinguish an external data-pack source where a Horizon one cannot. The per-table point also runs after the load has built the table map rather than inside the deserialisation call. No key. |
| `loot_drops` | **Gap:** `LootTableEvents.MODIFY_DROPS` | [Loot table events][lt-api] lines 107–115 and 156–164 declare a void callback with `(Holder<LootTable>, LootContext, List<ItemStack>)`; [loot table mixin][lt-table-mixin] lines 47–60 wrap the consumer overload of `LootTable.getRandomItemsRaw`, collect its stacks, invoke the event after that call returns, then forward the edited list to the original consumer. Horizon's `Game::native_loot_drops` (`game/native_hooks/base.rs`) also edits stacks, a `Vec<ItemStack>`, with no item-entity positions, motion or pickup delay. Its `LootDrops` context supplies a table id, dimension, origin, block or entity, player, tool, explosion and `used`, rather than Fabric's table holder and full `LootContext`. It fires at selected gameplay sites: `Game::native_block_loot`, `Game::native_use_drops`, `Game::drop_death_loot` and `Game::drop_leash`. The death call is after table loot, ordinary equipment and some extras were combined, but before an enderman's carried block and a copper golem's preserved equipment are appended; player inventory is handled separately by `Game::drop_death_items`, not this hook. Use and player-associated leash drops can fire it without a table roll. It does not wrap the loot engine's individual rolls, so chest/fishing rolls and nested table evaluation do not each get this Horizon callback. Both callbacks modify stacks without a cancellation return, but firing point, coverage and context arguments differ. No key. |
| `loot_function` | **Gap:** none | Fabric's whole loot API is the table-level set above ([Loot table events][lt-api] lines 44–115); it has no event that dispatches a custom loot function kind at roll time (in Java such a function is a registered codec, not an event). Horizon's `loot_function` (`collect`, `out = "stack"`) fires inside the roll when a table names a function `type` outside `minecraft:` (`horizon_world::loot::native_invoke_loot_function`), for every table the item engine rolls, and may change the stack. No Fabric event covers that moment. No key. |
| `loot_condition` | **Gap:** none | Same boundary: Fabric exposes no event for a custom loot condition kind, only table replacement and modification. Horizon's `loot_condition` (`collect`, `out = "holds"`) fires per unclaimed condition kind during a roll (`horizon_world::loot::native_invoke_loot_condition`) and the answer decides whether the entry holds. No key. |
| `block_drops` | **Gap:** none; nearest `PlayerBlockBreakEvents.BEFORE` / `AFTER` | [Block break events][pbb-api] lines 44–77 declare `BEFORE` (server only, returns false to cancel the break), `AFTER` (server only, void) and `CANCELED` (void); interfaces lines 86–128 pass `(Level, Player, BlockPos, BlockState, BlockEntity)` and no drops. [Server game mode mixin][pbb-mixin] lines 107–121 fire them at `Block.playerWillDestroy` and `Block.destroy` inside `ServerPlayerGameMode.destroyBlock`, for player breaks only. Horizon's `block_drops` (`cancel`, `out = "cancel"`) fires in `GameLevel::block_drops_ready` (`game/blocks/spill.rs`) after `Block.dropResources` has built the item entities and the experience, before anything spawns; it edits `Vec<ItemDrop>` and `experience`, cancels the spawn, and also covers mob breaks, explosions and level-side breaks. The moment (drop list built vs the break call), the coverage and the arguments all differ. No key. |
| `living_drops` | **Gap:** none; nearest `ServerLivingEntityEvents.AFTER_DEATH` | [Server living entity events][sliving-api] lines 88–90 and 153–160 declare `AFTER_DEATH` as a void `(LivingEntity, DamageSource)` notice; [living entity mixin][sliving-mixin] lines 70–73 fire it in `LivingEntity.die` at the `broadcastEntityEvent` call (and [server player mixin][loot-player-death-mixin] lines 76–78 injects at `ServerPlayer.die` TAIL). It is a death notice, carries no drop list, no position and no cancel. Horizon's `living_drops` (`cancel`, `out = "cancel"`) fires from `Game::drop_death_items` after the entity's whole drop list was collected and each item entity was built, passes the list with positions, motion and pickup delay, plus the killing blow and `recently_hit`, and can cancel the drops; an armor stand fires it with an empty list. Fabric has no drop-list event. Point and arguments differ. No key. |
| `get_equipment_drop_chance` | **Gap:** none; nearest `ServerEntityEvents.EQUIPMENT_CHANGE` | [Server entity events][sentity-api] lines 79–84 and 106–108 declare `EQUIPMENT_CHANGE` as a void `(LivingEntity, EquipmentSlot, previous, next)` notice; its javadoc at lines 73–77 describes equipment changes during `LivingEntity.tick` and on level join. [Equipment change mixin][loot-equipment-mixin] lines 36–38 fires at `Map.put` inside `LivingEntity.collectEquipmentChanges`. It reports a completed change, not a death drop chance. Horizon's hook fires in `Game::drop_death_loot` just before each equipment slot's death-drop roll, with the killing blow, `recently_hit`, the stack, the original chance, `preserved` and an editable `chance`; the roll is still drawn whatever the chance. No Fabric event reports that moment. No key. |
| `living_experience_drop` | **Gap:** none; nearest `ServerLivingEntityEvents.AFTER_DEATH` | Fabric has no experience-drop event; the nearest is the same `AFTER_DEATH` notice at [Server living entity events][sliving-api] lines 88–90 and 153–160, which passes no amount and cannot cancel. Horizon's `living_experience_drop` (`cancel`, `out = "cancel"`) fires from `Game::living_experience_drop` in `Game::drop_death_loot` after the vanilla conditions, with the entity, kind, attacking player, `original` and an editable `amount`; a sculk catalyst that takes the experience skips it. No key. |

The Fabric and Horizon points above come from the Fabric mixins/javadoc and
Horizon's own call order; no decompiled 26.3 sources are present in `.local/` to
check vanilla ordering independently. The change is metadata only: no dispatch,
tick-time work, adapter, alias or gameplay change.

## Event counterparts: item models, tints and tooltips (#1014)

Checked against the same Fabric API 26.3 revision,
`2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Fabric line numbers below are at that
revision; Horizon code is cited by function name, under `crates/horizon-client/src/`
unless the file says otherwise.

Scope: `item_tooltip.rs` and the seven item model and item tint hooks —
`item_model_property`, `item_model_range_property`, `item_model_select_property`,
`item_tint_source`, `register_item_tint_sources`, `register_select_item_model_property`
and `register_range_select_item_model_property`. `register_block_tint_sources` is a
block tint hook and is not part of this review. All eight hooks record a gap; no keys
are added.

In the pinned tree, `fabric-item-api-v1`'s client API declares one event that covers
this area, `ItemTooltipCallback.EVENT`; `fabric-rendering-v1`'s client API has no item
model or item tint event, and its only tint API is the static
`BlockColorRegistry.register` for blocks. The item model and tint hooks therefore
share one reason: Fabric API 26.3 declares no event for the moment they describe.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `item_tooltip` | **Gap:** `ItemTooltipCallback.EVENT` | [Item tooltip API][item-tooltip-api] lines 29–46 declare an array-backed event whose javadoc says it fires "after the game has appended all base tooltip lines to the list", passing the stack, its `Item.TooltipContext`, its `TooltipFlag` and the mutable line list; [item tooltip mixin][item-tooltip-mixin] line 38 injects at `ItemStack.getTooltipLines` `RETURN` ordinal 1, the return that has lines (the first return is the no-tooltip one). Horizon's `Game::stack_tooltip_lines` (`game/blocks/stations.rs`) is the port of that method and calls `Game::native_item_tooltip` at its end when its `shown` flag is set, which is the same "a tooltip the stack does not hide" guard; the client side, point after the base lines, and non-cancelling mutable-list behaviour align. Full argument parity is not proven: Fabric passes `Item.TooltipContext`, whereas Horizon passes local player ids, `TooltipDisplay` and advanced/creative flags, with no explicit tooltip-context argument. `ClientHost` cannot be assumed to supply every context operation. The firing coverage does not: Fabric's injection fires at every qualifying `getTooltipLines` call, and vanilla calls that method outside tooltip drawing — its `SessionSearchTrees` builds the search index from every stack's tooltip lines, which the repo's own oracles record as "SessionSearchTrees.getTooltipLines" (`VanillaCreativeTabsExporter.tooltipLines`) and "SessionSearchTrees.updateRecipes" (`VanillaRecipeBookViewOracle`, whose search tree calls `getTooltipLines`). Horizon's hook fires only where the tooltip is drawn (`Game::draw_stack_tooltip_with`, `Game::stack_tooltip`), never for a crossbow's loaded projectile, and Horizon's search reads export strings instead. A Fabric listener's added lines therefore reach the search index and a Horizon listener's do not, so the moments are not the same. No key. |
| `item_model_property` | **Gap:** none; NeoForge counterpart `RegisterConditionalItemModelPropertyEvent` | No Fabric API 26.3 event names an item model property: `fabric-item-api-v1`'s client API declares only `ItemTooltipCallback`, and `fabric-rendering-v1`'s client API has no model property event. The nearest tint API is the static [block color registry][block-color-registry] lines 47–59, registration methods for blocks, not an event and not item models. Horizon's `Env::item_model_property` (`game/interface/item_draw.rs`, the `ItemEnv` implementation the item model resolver in `horizon-assets` asks) answers a namespaced `minecraft:condition` property each time the model picks a branch, with the stack being resolved. No event, so no key. |
| `item_model_range_property` | **Gap:** none; NeoForge counterpart `RegisterRangeSelectItemModelPropertyEvent` | The same search applies: no Fabric API 26.3 event covers a model property, and the static [block color registry][block-color-registry] is the only tint registration in `fabric-rendering-v1`'s client API. Horizon's `Env::item_model_range_property` answers the number for a namespaced `minecraft:range_dispatch` each time it picks a branch. No key. |
| `item_model_select_property` | **Gap:** none; NeoForge counterpart `RegisterSelectItemModelPropertyEvent` | The same search applies: no Fabric API 26.3 event covers a model property. Horizon's `Env::item_model_select_property` answers the string for a namespaced `minecraft:select` each time it picks a case. No key. |
| `item_tint_source` | **Gap:** none; NeoForge counterpart `RegisterColorHandlersEvent.ItemTintSources` | Fabric has no event for an item tint source; [block color registry][block-color-registry] lines 47–59 register block `BlockTintSource`s and factories at init, a static call, and nothing in `fabric-item-api-v1` or `fabric-rendering-v1` takes an item `tints` entry. Horizon's `Env::item_tint_source` answers the ARGB colour of one `tints` entry per stack each time the item resolves — in hand, in a screen and on the ground — and gets the entry's JSON object. Different mechanism, moment and arguments; no key. |
| `register_item_tint_sources` | **Gap:** none; NeoForge counterpart `RegisterColorHandlersEvent.ItemTintSources` | Fabric's nearest call is the same static block registration, which takes the tint layers and the blocks, not a list of item tint source ids collected once before resources load. Horizon's `register_item_tint_sources` (`game/modding/mods/mod.rs`) collects the ids through `Game::native_register_item_tint_sources` at start, before resources load; a `minecraft:model`'s `tints` entry then colours through `item_tint_source`. No event, so no key. |
| `register_select_item_model_property` | **Gap:** none; NeoForge counterpart `RegisterSelectItemModelPropertyEvent` | Fabric API 26.3 declares no event for registering item model properties, and its item model surface has no such registry; the only registration API in reach is the static block tint registry above. Horizon's `register_select_item_model_properties` (`game/modding/mods/mod.rs`) collects the ids through `Game::native_register_select_item_model_property` once at start, before resources load. No key. |
| `register_range_select_item_model_property` | **Gap:** none; NeoForge counterpart `RegisterRangeSelectItemModelPropertyEvent` | As above: no Fabric API 26.3 event and no item model property registry. Horizon's `register_range_select_item_model_properties` (`game/modding/mods/mod.rs`) collects the ids through `Game::native_register_range_select_item_model_property` once at start, before resources load. No key. |

The `item_tooltip` hook remains a gap: the search callers recorded in the repo's
oracles demonstrate different firing coverage, and full tooltip-context argument
parity is not proven. A key requires evidence for all four parts of the contract;
matching the drawing path alone is insufficient.

The change is metadata only: no dispatch, tick-time work, adapter, alias or gameplay
change.

[item-tooltip-api]: https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/blob/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222/fabric-item-api-v1/src/client/java/net/fabricmc/fabric/api/client/item/v1/ItemTooltipCallback.java
[item-tooltip-mixin]: https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/blob/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222/fabric-item-api-v1/src/client/java/net/fabricmc/fabric/mixin/item/client/ItemStackMixin.java
[block-color-registry]: https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/blob/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222/fabric-rendering-v1/src/client/java/net/fabricmc/fabric/api/client/rendering/v1/BlockColorRegistry.java

## Event counterparts: entity lifecycle and equipment (#1014)

Checked against the same Fabric API 26.3 revision,
`2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Fabric line numbers below are at that
revision; Horizon code is cited by function name (under `crates/horizon-client/src/`)
and never by line number.

Scope: `base.rs`'s `entities_load`, `entities_unload`, `entity_join_level`,
`entity_leave_level`, `player_start_tracking`, `player_stop_tracking`,
`living_equipment_change` and `living_equipment_listened`. This is the entity load,
tracking and equipment part of `base.rs`, after the server connection, chunk, level,
player connection, effects/interaction/damage/chat and loot parts in the earlier
sections. All eight record a gap; none of these reviewed hooks carries a key and none
is added.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `entities_load` | **Gap:** none; nearest `ServerEntityEvents.ENTITY_LOAD` | [Server entity events][sentity-api] lines 35–47 declare `ENTITY_LOAD` as a void `(Entity, ServerLevel)` notice whose javadoc says the entity is already in the level; lines 85–88 are its listener interface. [Server entity callbacks mixin][sentity-callbacks-mixin] lines 38–41 inject at `ServerLevel$EntityCallbacks.onTrackingStart` TAIL, so it fires once per entity as that entity starts being tracked by the level. Horizon's `Game::native_entities_load` is called by `Game::spawn_loaded_entities` (`game/world/persist.rs`) once per saved chunk, after the chunk's entity records were read and their entities spawned, with the dimension, the chunk's x/z and the record count: one aggregate notice per chunk, not one per entity, and it reports records that produced no entity too. Granularity and arguments differ. No key. |
| `entities_unload` | **Gap:** none; nearest `ServerEntityEvents.ENTITY_UNLOAD` | [Server entity events][sentity-api] lines 62–71 declare `ENTITY_UNLOAD` as a void `(Entity, ServerLevel)` notice "called before the entity is removed from the level"; lines 100–103 are its listener interface. [Server entity callbacks mixin][sentity-callbacks-mixin] lines 43–46 inject at `onTrackingEnd` HEAD, so the entity is still in the level. Horizon's `Game::native_entities_unload` is called by `Game::unload_entities` (`game/world/persist.rs`) after `Save::save_entities_later` attempted to queue the chunk's records for saving (the notice also fires if queueing fails), once per chunk with the dimension, chunk x/z and count. Aggregate vs per entity, and after the save was queued rather than before the entity leaves the level. No key. |
| `entity_join_level` | **Gap:** `ServerEntityEvents.ALLOW_LOAD` | [Server entity events][sentity-api] lines 49–60 declare `ALLOW_LOAD` as "called right before an Entity is loaded into a ServerLevel", cancellable, with `(Entity, ServerLevel, EntitySpawnReason, boolean isLoadedFromDisk)`; any listener returning `false` wins and `true` defers to vanilla (lines 52–59, 90–98). [Entity load mixin][entity-load-mixin] lines 33–41 inject at `PersistentEntitySectionManager.addEntity` HEAD, before the entity enters the level. The veto shape matches: Horizon's `canceled` keeps the entity out and nothing else can force it in. Horizon's `Game::native_entity_join_level` is fired by `Game::native_joins_flush` (`game/modding/native_entities.rs`) after the entity was inserted, once per entity in creation order, on the next check (after the blocks, vehicles, projectiles, items and mobs, or at the tick's end) with the dimension, ID, kind, position and `loaded_from_disk`; setting `canceled` takes it back out. Fabric refuses the insert and Horizon removes after the insert and tells later; Fabric also passes the `Entity` and the spawn reason, Horizon passes an ID and a kind and has no spawn reason, and Horizon does not tell displays, markers or interactions, the entities it keeps without simulating. Firing point, coverage and arguments differ. No key. |
| `entity_leave_level` | **Gap:** `ServerEntityEvents.ENTITY_UNLOAD` | Same Fabric event as `entities_unload`: [Server entity events][sentity-api] lines 62–71 with the listener interface at lines 100–103, fired at [server entity callbacks mixin][sentity-callbacks-mixin] lines 43–46, `onTrackingEnd` HEAD, while the entity is still in the level. Horizon's `Game::native_entity_leave_level` is called by `Game::native_entity_left` and `Game::native_leaves_flush` (`game/modding/native_entities.rs`) after the entity is gone from the level's lists, when the game next notices it (the action that removed it, or the tick), with the dimension, ID and kind, and only for entities `entity_join_level` announced. Before vs after removal, per entity vs IDs, and the coverage of unsimulated kinds differ. No key. |
| `player_start_tracking` | **Gap:** `EntityTrackingEvents.START_TRACKING` | [Tracking events][tracking-api] lines 30–39 declare `START_TRACKING` as a void `(Entity trackedEntity, ServerPlayer player)` notice after the player started tracking; lines 51–60 are its interface, and lines 31–33 say it runs after the entity's spawn packet was sent. [Server entity mixin][tracking-mixin] lines 38–41 inject at `ServerEntity.addPairing` TAIL. Horizon's tracker pairs after the tick in `Entities::pair_after_tick` (`crates/horizon-client/src/lan/entity.rs`, the port of `ServerEntity` pairing) and queues the events, and `Game::native_player_start_tracking` tells mods from `Game::run_native_tracking` (`game/network/lan_entities.rs`), bundled per player, with the player's UUID and name and the entity's ID, UUID and kind. Delivery is queued, but `Game::lan_flush_tracking` (`game/network/lan/mod.rs`) can drain it in the same operation after pairing; a later tick is not a universal difference. IDs and names do not replace Fabric's `Entity` and `ServerPlayer` arguments. No key. |
| `player_stop_tracking` | **Gap:** `EntityTrackingEvents.STOP_TRACKING` | [Tracking events][tracking-api] lines 41–49 declare `STOP_TRACKING` as a void `(Entity, ServerPlayer)` notice "called before a player stops tracking an entity", while the entity still exists (lines 42–43); lines 62–71 are its interface. [Server entity mixin][tracking-mixin] lines 43–46 inject at `ServerEntity.removePairing` HEAD. Horizon's hook comment places its own notice at the end of `removePairing`, and the stop is queued by the same per-tick tracker pass; `Game::native_player_stop_tracking` tells mods when the queue is drained, including by `Game::lan_flush_tracking` in the same operation (a leaving player's after `native_player_logged_out`) with the same UUID/name/ID/kind fields. Before vs after the pairing and the entity and player objects vs their IDs differ. No key. |
| `living_equipment_change` | **Gap:** `ServerEntityEvents.EQUIPMENT_CHANGE` | [Server entity events][sentity-api] lines 73–83 declare `EQUIPMENT_CHANGE` as a void `(LivingEntity, EquipmentSlot, ItemStack previous, ItemStack next)` notice called during `LivingEntity.tick()` when a slot changed or was mutated, also when the entity joins the level, decided by `ItemStack.matches`; lines 105–108 are its interface. [Equipment mixin][equipment-mixin] lines 36–38 inject at the `Map.put` in `LivingEntity.collectEquipmentChanges`. Horizon's `Game::native_living_equipment_change` is fired from `Game::native_mobs_equipment` and `Game::native_players_equipment` (`game/modding/native_mobs.rs`) and from `Game::armor_stand_location_effects` (`game/items/enchant_effects.rs`) once per changed slot, with the entity's ID and kind, the slot name and the `from`/`to` stacks. Mobs are checked after their tick; players are checked near the start of `Game::tick_guests` (`game/network/lan_actions/tick.rs`), before its per-player simulation loop. The mob and player passes ask `living_equipment_listened`; armor stands bypass that gate. Fabric hands the `LivingEntity` itself, calls it inside each entity's own tick and has no opt-in gate; arguments and the point in the tick differ. No key. |
| `living_equipment_listened` | **Gap:** none | Fabric has no opt-in gate for equipment listening: `ServerEntityEvents.EQUIPMENT_CHANGE` is invoked directly for every registered listener ([Server entity events][sentity-api] lines 79–83). Horizon's `collect` hook (`out = "listened"`) is asked separately by `Game::native_mobs_equipment` and `Game::native_players_equipment` each tick so the game reads every mob's and player's equipment only when some mod listens. Horizon-only; no Fabric event covers it. No key. |

The Fabric points above come from the Fabric mixins and javadoc, and the Horizon
points from Horizon's own call order; no decompiled 26.3 sources are present in
`.local/` for an independent vanilla check. The change is metadata only: no
dispatch, tick-time work, adapter, alias or gameplay change.
