## Event counterparts: commands (#1014)

Checked against the same Fabric API 26.3 revision,
`2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Fabric line numbers below are at that
revision; Horizon code is cited by function name (under `crates/horizon-client/src/`
unless the file says otherwise) and never by line number.

Scope: `base.rs`'s `register_commands`, `register_client_commands`, `command`,
`player_command`, `run_mod_command`, `player_commands_sent` and `tab_complete`.
This is the commands part of `base.rs`, after the server connection, chunk, level,
player connection, effects/interaction/damage/chat, loot, item/tooltip and entity
lifecycle parts in the earlier sections. All seven record a gap; no key is added.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `register_commands` | **Gap:** `CommandRegistrationCallback.EVENT` | [Command api][cmd-api] lines 28–38 are the javadoc "Callback for when a server registers all commands", lines 40–45 the array-backed event and lines 47–54 the `register(dispatcher, buildContext, selection)` listener. [Commands mixin][cmd-mixin] lines 33–49 inject into `Commands.<init>` at the `CommandDispatcher.setConsumer` call (annotation line 46) and invoke the event with the constructor's dispatcher, build context and selection (line 48); the javadoc at lines 39–45 places that before ambiguities are calculated. Horizon's `Game::native_register_commands` is called by `Game::register_native_commands` (`game/modding/native_server.rs`) from `Game::tick_lan` (`game/network/lan/mod.rs`) once a hub exists, and again after `Game::native_refresh_commands` asked. The moment (a server building its command tree) and the side match, and neither side can cancel, but what the listener gets differs: Fabric hands over the Brigadier dispatcher, `CommandBuildContext` and `CommandSelection`, so a listener can add any argument type, redirect, `requires` predicate or child of a vanilla node, and can branch on integrated/dedicated, while Horizon hands a vector that the listener fills with `Command` usages (`crates/horizon-mod-vocab/src/lib.rs`) in Horizon's own grammar — its built-in argument kinds, argument types a mod registered with `Registrar::register_command_argument`, and an operator level — which `command_tree` merges itself and which drops a name vanilla has. Fabric asks once per `Commands` instance; Horizon asks once per world opened to players and again on a refresh. Arguments and firing count differ. No key. |
| `register_client_commands` | **Gap:** `ClientCommandRegistrationCallback.EVENT` | [Client command api][client-cmd-api] lines 33–38 declare the event and lines 40–46 its `register(dispatcher, buildContext)` listener. [Client command mixin][client-cmd-mixin] lines 76–82 fire it at `ClientPacketListener.handleLogin` RETURN into a fresh `CommandDispatcher`, and lines 85–91 at `handleCommands` RETURN only copy the already-registered nodes into the vanilla dispatcher through `ClientCommandInternals.addCommands` ([client command internals][client-cmd-impl] lines 229–233 and `copyChildren` 245–276), so the event is not asked again when the server's tree arrives. Horizon's `Game::native_register_client_commands` is asked by `Game::chat_tree_for` (`game/interface/chat.rs`) each time the chat box's base tree is built: at connection start, again when the server's tree arrives (`game/network/remote/mod.rs`) and for the local tree. It pushes `Command` usages, a command with an operator level is dropped, and a name the base tree has keeps that tree's command. Side and the non-cancelling registration gesture match, but the listener gets usages in Horizon's grammar rather than the Brigadier dispatcher and build context, and the ask count differs (the server's tree arrival asks again). No key. |
| `command` | **Gap:** `ClientSendMessageEvents.ALLOW_COMMAND` | [Client send api][send-api] lines 47–57 declare `ALLOW_COMMAND` as the event "whenever the player executes a command including client commands", without the slash, to block the message; lines 58–66 stop at the first `false`, and lines 84–98, 112–124 and 135–143 are the separate `MODIFY_COMMAND`, `COMMAND` and `COMMAND_CANCELED` events. [Client send mixin][send-mixin] lines 30–35 set priority 800 so its lines 47–56 run at `ClientPacketListener.sendCommand` HEAD before the command module's own mixin. The client-command execution is that other mixin, [client command mixin][client-cmd-mixin] lines 105–110 at `sendCommand` HEAD and lines 98–103 at `sendUnattendedCommand` HEAD, through `ClientCommandInternals.executeCommand` ([client command internals][client-cmd-impl] lines 82–119), which answers "send to the server" only for an unknown command or a parse exception (lines 165–172). Horizon's `Game::native_command` is asked by `Game::client_mod_command` (`game/native_hooks/shared.rs`) from `Game::handle_chat_input` and `Game::click_command` (`game/interface/chat.rs`) and from the control socket's `command` (`game/runtime/agent/mod.rs`); it is the only way a client command runs, and `ran` both stops vanilla and says the mod ran it. A clicked command goes through `sendUnattendedCommand`, where Fabric executes client commands but fires no `ALLOW_COMMAND` (the send-message mixin hooks only `sendCommand`), while Horizon asks `command` for the click too; and the veto Fabric splits out cannot answer "ran", so a blocked command runs nothing unless its listener acted. Firing coverage and the answer shape differ. No key. |
| `player_command` | **Gap:** none; nearest `ServerMessageEvents.ALLOW_COMMAND_MESSAGE` | [Server message api][smsg-api] lines 71–90 declare `ALLOW_COMMAND_MESSAGE` and lines 118–131 `COMMAND_MESSAGE` for a command's chat message, "such as one from `/me` and `/say`"; [server message mixin][player-list-mixin] lines 64–87 fire them at `PlayerList.broadcastChatMessage(PlayerChatMessage, CommandSourceStack, ChatType.Bound)` HEAD. That is the feedback a chat-carrying command broadcasts, not the command running, and it fires for no other command. Fabric declares no server-side command-execution event: `CommandRegistrationCallback` ([Command api][cmd-api] lines 28–54) only registers commands. Horizon's `Game::native_player_command` is called by `Game::guest_command` (`game/network/lan_actions/commands.rs`) before the hub dispatches the line, for every command the player sends including one they may not run, and by `Game::run_quiet_line` (`game/network/lan_quiet.rs`) for quiet-batch lines that pass its guest-command availability, guest lookup and unsigned-message-argument guards; `canceled` stops the command and `replies` answer it. No Fabric event covers that moment. No key. |
| `run_mod_command` | **Gap:** none | Fabric's mod commands are Brigadier nodes added at `CommandRegistrationCallback` ([Command api][cmd-api] lines 40–54, [Commands mixin][cmd-mixin] lines 46–48) and executed by Brigadier; no API package in the pinned tree declares an execution event, and `ServerMessageEvents.ALLOW_COMMAND_MESSAGE` ([Server message api][smsg-api] lines 78–90) is the message a command broadcasts. Horizon's `Game::native_run_mod_command` is called by `Game::run_native_command` (`game/modding/native_server.rs`), the executor of the `native_mod_command` node, after the permission check and argument resolution, from a guest (`Game::perform_native_command`), the console (`Game::perform_native_console_command`), a function or `execute`; `ran` says a mod handled it, and otherwise vanilla answers the unknown command. No Fabric event covers executing one registered command. No key. |
| `player_commands_sent` | **Gap:** none | No Fabric API 26.3 event filters the command tree a server sends to one player. `CommandRegistrationCallback` builds the server-wide tree once ([Command api][cmd-api] lines 28–45, [Commands mixin][cmd-mixin] lines 46–48); the only per-source filter is `ClientCommandInternals.copyChildren` ([client command internals][client-cmd-impl] lines 245–276), which copies *client* commands into the client's own completion tree by `canUse`, not the server tree a player receives. Horizon's `Game::native_player_commands_sent` is called by `Game::native_filter_commands` (`game/modding/native_server.rs`) for a joining player (no state swapped in) and for a placed player (state swapped in), and by `Game::resend_commands_filtered`; the answer is stored as the guest's hidden root names and the tree the hub sends omits them. No key. |
| `tab_complete` | **Gap:** none | Fabric declares no suggestion or tab-completion event: the event index built from this tree lists none, and the command module's only suggestion-side mixin, [client suggestion mixin][client-suggestion-mixin] lines 34–78, implements the client command source and marks the provider attended, no event. Horizon's `Game::native_tab_complete` is called by `Game::guest_suggestions` (`game/network/lan_actions/commands.rs`) after the server computed vanilla's suggestions for a guest's request, with the guest's players, ops, bans and permission swapped into the registries, and hands the player UUID and name, the command text, the byte range and the mutable suggestions. No key. |

The Fabric points above come from Fabric's event declarations, mixins and javadoc,
and the Horizon points from Horizon's own call order; no decompiled 26.3 sources are
present in `.local/` for an independent vanilla check of where `Commands` is built or
how a command is dispatched. The change is metadata only: no dispatch, tick-time
work, adapter, alias or gameplay change.

[cmd-api]: https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/blob/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222/fabric-command-api-v2/src/main/java/net/fabricmc/fabric/api/command/v2/CommandRegistrationCallback.java
[cmd-mixin]: https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/blob/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222/fabric-command-api-v2/src/main/java/net/fabricmc/fabric/mixin/command/CommandsMixin.java
[client-cmd-api]: https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/blob/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222/fabric-command-api-v2/src/client/java/net/fabricmc/fabric/api/client/command/v2/ClientCommandRegistrationCallback.java
[client-cmd-mixin]: https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/blob/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222/fabric-command-api-v2/src/client/java/net/fabricmc/fabric/mixin/command/client/ClientPacketListenerMixin.java
[client-cmd-impl]: https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/blob/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222/fabric-command-api-v2/src/client/java/net/fabricmc/fabric/impl/command/client/ClientCommandInternals.java
[smsg-api]: https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/blob/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222/fabric-message-api-v1/src/main/java/net/fabricmc/fabric/api/message/v1/ServerMessageEvents.java
[client-suggestion-mixin]: https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/blob/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222/fabric-command-api-v2/src/client/java/net/fabricmc/fabric/mixin/command/client/ClientSuggestionProviderMixin.java

## Event counterparts: block and item interaction (#1014)

Checked against the same Fabric API 26.3 revision,
`2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Fabric line numbers below are at that
revision; Horizon code is cited by function name (under `crates/horizon-client/src/`
unless the file says otherwise) and never by line number.

Scope: `base.rs`'s `use_on`, `use_item`, `right_click_block`, `left_click_block`,
`break_block` and `attack_entity`, plus `block_use_item_on.rs`'s `use_item_on` and
`block_use_without_item.rs`'s `use_without_item`. This is the block and item
interaction part of `base.rs`, after the server connection, chunk, level, player
connection, effects/interaction/damage/chat, loot, item/tooltip, entity lifecycle
and command parts in the earlier sections. All eight record a gap; no key is added.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `use_item_on` | **Gap:** `BlockEvents.USE_ITEM_ON` | [Block events][block-events] lines 38-53 declare `USE_ITEM_ON` for a call to `BlockState.useItemOn(ItemStack, Level, Player, InteractionHand, BlockHitResult)`, and lines 72-86 are the listener that receives the stack, state, level, pos, player, hand and hit result and returns a nullable `InteractionResult`. [Block state mixin][block-state-mixin] lines 41-48 inject at `BlockStateBase.useItemOn` HEAD and return a non-null result in the method's place; a `null` answer falls on to vanilla. Horizon's `Game::native_use_item_on` is called by `Game::use_hand` after the `use_item_on_block` phase declined to answer and before the block-specific handlers, and only on the server; it hands the state and a `BlockClick` (side, dimension, pos, hit, hand, stack, player UUID and entity id) and an `answer` that replaces the block's result, with `Pass` going on to the item's `useOn` and `TryWithEmptyHand` trying `useWithoutItem`. The nullable replace answer matches. Fabric receives a Player object while Horizon supplies player UUID and entity id in the click; the Host only substitutes for level context, not player data. The mixin has no side guard, unlike Horizon's server-only declaration. Actual client prediction call coverage is not established by this mixin alone. Arguments differ and full side coverage is not proven, so no key. |
| `use_without_item` | **Gap:** `BlockEvents.USE_WITHOUT_ITEM` | [Block events][block-events] lines 55-69 declare `USE_WITHOUT_ITEM` for a call to `BlockState.useWithoutItem(Level, Player, BlockHitResult)` and lines 90-102 are its listener (state, level, pos, player, hit result; nullable `InteractionResult`). [Block state mixin][block-state-mixin] lines 50-57 inject at `useWithoutItem` HEAD and return a non-null answer in its place. Horizon's `Game::native_use_without_item` is called by `Game::use_hand` only in the main hand, only after the block's `useItemOn` phase asked for the empty-hand try (`TRY_WITH_EMPTY_HAND`), and only on the server, with the same `BlockClick` and answer. Fabric injects into every `useWithoutItem` invocation without a side or hand guard; this does not prove that vanilla calls it for the off hand. Its listener has no hand or stack argument, unlike `BlockClick`, and takes a Player object instead of player UUID/entity id. The replace answer matches, but arguments differ and full client call coverage is not proven. No key. |
| `use_on` | **Gap:** `ItemEvents.USE_ON` | [Item events][item-events] lines 36-51 declare `USE_ON` for the `Item.useOn(UseOnContext)` call inside `ItemStack.useOn`, with the listener at lines 70-80 taking the `UseOnContext` and returning a nullable `InteractionResult`. [Item stack mixin][item-stack-mixin] lines 47-55 wrap that call and return the listener's non-null result instead of the item's own `useOn`. Horizon's `Game::native_use_on`, called by `Game::use_hand` where its port of `ItemStack.useOn`/`Item.useOn` starts, hands a `UseOnCall` (stack, pos, face, hand, hit, player UUID and entity id) and a `claimed` flag; claiming swings the hand and runs neither `useOn` nor `use`, so the listener cannot answer a result. Fabric's answer can be any `InteractionResult` (`PASS` is also an answer that replaces vanilla, not a deferral), and the common wrapper has no side guard. Answer shape differs; exact client call coverage is not independently established. No key. |
| `use_item` | **Gap:** `ItemEvents.USE` | [Item events][item-events] lines 53-68 declare `USE` for `Item.use(Level, Player, InteractionHand)` inside `ItemStack.use`, and lines 82-93 are the listener (level, player, hand; nullable `InteractionResult`). [Item stack mixin][item-stack-mixin] lines 36-45 wrap the `Item.use` call and return a non-null answer in its place. Horizon's `Game::native_use_item` is called by `Game::use_hand` where `Item.use` starts, after the spectator check and after the item's use cooldown gate, when not handling a guest `UseItemOn` packet, including after a block-targeted click falls through, and a claim swings and takes the use without an answer. Fabric wraps the `Item.use` invocation inside `ItemStack.use`; neither its javadoc nor this wrapper establishes spectator/cooldown guard order. The common wrapper has no side guard, and its listener chooses a nullable result instead of claiming the use. Answer shape differs; exact guard and client call coverage remain unproven. `UseItemCallback` is the nearer Fabric event for `base.rs`'s own right-click hook and was reviewed with `right_click_item` in the earlier section. No key. |
| `right_click_block` | **Gap:** `UseBlockCallback.EVENT` | [Use block api][useblock-api] lines 28-52 declare the callback, fired before the spectator check, where `PASS` continues and `SUCCESS`, `CONSUME` or `FAIL` replace the click. It fires at the `useItemOn` entry on the server ([server player game mode mixin][spgm-mixin] lines 85-94) and on the client ([client game mode mixin][mpgm-mixin] lines 98-115, which repeats the spectator check before the event). Horizon's `Game::native_right_click_block`, fired by `Game::right_click_block` from `Game::use_hand` (and for a spectator's block click in `Game::use_item_as_player`), fires once per hand with a `BlockClick`, two tri-state forces (`use_block`, `use_item`) and a `cancellation_result` that replaces the click, then `canceled`. Fabric's single `InteractionResult` cannot force the block's or the item's branch on or off while letting the click continue. Fabric `PASS` defers to the remaining block processing, whereas Horizon can cancel with `Pass` to skip block and item-on-block processing and continue to item use. Fabric's client injection skips spectators; its server injection is at HEAD. No additional swing difference is claimed. The already-reviewed `use_item_on_block` is the other Horizon hook at the same vanilla entry. No key. |
| `left_click_block` | **Gap:** `AttackBlockCallback.EVENT` | [Attack block api][attackblock-api] lines 29-62 declare the callback with separate client and server meanings for each `InteractionResult`; on the server any value but `PASS` cancels. The server injection repeats the interaction-range and world-bounds checks itself and then fires at `handleBlockBreakAction` HEAD ([server player game mode mixin][spgm-mixin] lines 59-83), without proving the placement of later vanilla spawn-protection or `mayInteract` guards; it sends a block update on a cancel; the client fires from `startDestroyBlock` and, in creative, `continueDestroyBlock`, so a held dig keeps firing ([client game mode mixin][mpgm-mixin] lines 66-91). Horizon's `Game::native_left_click_block` is called by `Game::lan_guest_packet` for a `START_DESTROY_BLOCK` action after `may_reach_block`, the spawn-protection check and the world-border check, once per dig start, with pos, face and state, and a cancel is a boolean with no result to answer. Side, coverage and arguments differ (Fabric receives player and hand, Horizon receives state); the server return values themselves implement a veto just like Horizon's boolean. The injection location is proven, but later vanilla guard order is not independently established. No key. |
| `break_block` | **Gap:** `PlayerBlockBreakEvents.BEFORE` | [Break events][pbb-api] lines 37-54 declare `BEFORE` as server-only ("Only called on the server"), with the listener at lines 86-99 taking level, player, pos, state and a nullable `BlockEntity` and returning `false` to cancel. [Server player game mode mixin][spgm-mixin] lines 107-116 inject at the `Block.playerWillDestroy` INVOKE inside `destroyBlock`; the mixin alone does not prove the order of preceding guards. A `false` listener return fires `CANCELED` and returns `false` from `destroyBlock`; `AFTER` is a separate notice at lines 118-121. Horizon's `Game::native_break_block` is called by `Game::break_block_as_player` after `debug_stick_can_destroy`, `block_action_restricted`, the held item's `can_destroy_block` and the game-master check, before its `playerWillDestroy` port, with pos and state, and `canceled` keeps the block. Both veto before `playerWillDestroy` on the server; exact preceding vanilla guard order is not independently established. Arguments and full contract differ: Fabric passes Player and nullable `BlockEntity`, whereas Horizon uses the acting-player service, has no block-entity argument, and additionally lets the listener replace `exp_to_drop`. The separate Fabric `CANCELED`/`AFTER` events are context, not themselves a mismatch of `BEFORE`. No key. |
| `attack_entity` | **Gap:** `AttackEntityCallback.EVENT` | [Attack entity api][attackentity-api] lines 31-55 declare the callback (fired before the spectator check), with the listener taking player, level, hand, the `Entity` and a nullable `EntityHitResult`; `PASS` continues and any other result cancels, with `SUCCESS` sending the attack packet on the client. On the server it fires at `Player.attack` HEAD for `ServerPlayer` only ([player mixin][player-mixin] lines 34-42, always with a `null` hit result); on the client it fires before the attack packet is sent ([client game mode mixin][mpgm-mixin] lines 133-144). Horizon's `Game::native_attack_entity` is called by `Game::guest_attack` after the world-border and `within_attack_range` checks, before `Player.attack`, and passes the entity's id, its kind and its position, with a boolean cancel. The entity object, hand and hit result versus an id, a kind and a position, and the extra client-side firing, differ. No key. |

The Fabric points above come from Fabric's event declarations, mixins and javadoc,
and the Horizon points from Horizon's own call order; no decompiled 26.3 sources are
present in `.local/` for an independent vanilla check of the order of `destroyBlock`'s
guards or of the client's `useItemOn` prediction. The change is metadata only: no
dispatch, tick-time work, adapter, alias or gameplay change.

## Event counterparts: screen input posts (#1014)

Checked against the same Fabric API 26.3 revision,
`2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Fabric line numbers below are at that
revision; Horizon code is cited by function name (under `crates/horizon-client/src/`
unless the file says otherwise) and never by line number.

Scope: the seven `Post` hooks `screen_input_post.rs` declares, after the client
lifecycle, effects/interaction/damage/chat, server tick/level/chunk, player connection,
item/tooltip, loot, entity lifecycle, command and block/interaction parts in the earlier
sections. Fabric splits each of these moments into an `Allow*`, a `Before*` and an
`After*` event per screen instance, so the Horizon `_post` hook is compared against the
`After*` event only. All seven record a gap; no key is added.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `screen_mouse_button_pressed_post` | **Gap:** `ScreenMouseEvents.afterMouseClick` | [Screen mouse events][screen-mouse-events] are per screen instance (lines 32-38), with the `afterMouseClick` accessor at lines 70-74 and the `AfterMouseClick` listener at lines 215-225 returning a `boolean` `consumed`. [Screen mouse mixin][screen-mouse-mixin] lines 32-53 wrap the `Screen.mouseClicked(MouseButtonEvent, boolean)` call from `MouseHandler.onButton`: `allowMouseClick` at 38-41, `beforeMouseClick` at 43, the vanilla call at 46, then `afterMouseClick` at 48-50 whose answer is OR'd into the handled result at 49; the comment at lines 34-36 states that the screen passed to the events is the one the handler was called on, even if the handler changes screens. Horizon's `Game::native_screen_mouse_button_pressed_post` is fired by `Game::screen_mouse_post` from `Game::handle_window_event_inner` after the window event's screen arms ran and only when the mouse Pre did not cancel, with a `ScreenView` (id, menu, title, rect, slots, mouse, partial tick), the GLFW button and modifiers. It is a notice: not cancellable, no `wasClickHandled`, and no answer. It re-reads the screen open at that moment, so a press that opened another screen reports the new one and one that closed the screen reports nothing, and it is skipped when the same event ended in an error. Arguments (live `Screen` with its widget list versus a read-only view), cancel behaviour (a boolean claim versus a notice), subscription (per screen instance versus every screen the gate covers) and firing point (inside the wrapped `mouseClicked` call versus the end of the window event) all differ. No key. |
| `screen_mouse_button_released_post` | **Gap:** `ScreenMouseEvents.afterMouseRelease` | [Screen mouse events][screen-mouse-events] lines 103-107 declare the `afterMouseRelease` accessor and lines 252-262 the `AfterMouseRelease` listener, which returns the `boolean` `consumed`. [Screen mouse mixin][screen-mouse-mixin] lines 55-76 wrap `Screen.mouseReleased(MouseButtonEvent)` from `MouseHandler.onButton`, firing `afterMouseRelease` at 71-73 and OR-ing its answer into the handled result at 72. Horizon's `Game::native_screen_mouse_button_released_post` is fired by `Game::screen_mouse_post` from `Game::handle_window_event_inner` after the release arms ran and when the Pre did not cancel, with the same view, button and modifiers. The same three differences hold as for the press: a live `Screen` versus a view, a boolean claim versus a non-cancellable notice without `wasReleaseHandled`, and per-screen subscriptions versus a global re-read of the screen open at the end of the event. No key. |
| `screen_mouse_dragged_post` | **Gap:** `ScreenMouseEvents.afterMouseDrag` | [Screen mouse events][screen-mouse-events] lines 142-146 declare `afterMouseDrag` and lines 295-307 its listener, with the `boolean` `consumed` and the `double` horizontal and vertical amounts. [Screen mouse mixin][screen-mouse-mixin] lines 78-99 wrap `Screen.mouseDragged(MouseButtonEvent, double, double)` from `MouseHandler.handleAccumulatedMovement`, firing `afterMouseDrag` at 94-96 and OR-ing its answer at 95. Horizon's `Game::screen_mouse_dragged` (the drag Pre) decides in the `CursorMoved` arm of `Game::handle_window_event_inner` and leaves a `ScreenMousePost::Drag` that `Game::screen_mouse_post` fires after the drag arms ran; the payload is the button, a `[f32; 2]` GUI-pixel delta and modifiers. Horizon fires only for a real pointer move with a tracked drag button, and the post is a notice; Fabric wraps every `mouseDragged` call and its listener can claim the drag. Arguments (`f32` pixel delta and a view versus `double` amounts and a live `Screen` with its event) and cancel behaviour differ. No key. |
| `screen_mouse_scrolled_post` | **Gap:** `ScreenMouseEvents.afterMouseScroll` | [Screen mouse events][screen-mouse-events] lines 181-185 declare `afterMouseScroll` and lines 339-351 its listener (`boolean` `consumed`, `double` mouse x and y, `double` horizontal and vertical amounts). [Screen mouse mixin][screen-mouse-mixin] lines 101-122 wrap `Screen.mouseScrolled(double, double, double, double)` from `MouseHandler.onScroll`, firing `afterMouseScroll` at 117-119 and OR-ing its answer at 118. Horizon's `Game::screen_mouse_post` fires the scroll Post from the wheel arm of `Game::handle_window_event_inner` after the scroll arms ran, when the Pre did not cancel, with the `[f64; 2]` scroll amount and the screen view; the pointer is `screen.mouse`. Fabric receives the mouse position as two event arguments rather than through a screen view, wraps every `mouseScrolled` call, and its answer can claim the scroll; Horizon's is a notice. No key. |
| `screen_key_pressed_post` | **Gap:** `ScreenKeyboardEvents.afterKeyPress` | [Screen keyboard events][screen-keyboard-events] are per screen instance (lines 30-39), with the `afterKeyPress` accessor at lines 71-75 and the `AfterKeyPress` listener at lines 172-181, which returns `void` (line 180) and is documented as "Called after a key press is handled" (line 174). [Screen keyboard mixin][screen-keyboard-mixin] lines 33-54 wrap `Screen.keyPressed(KeyEvent)` in `KeyboardHandler.keyPress`: `allowKeyPress` at 38-42, `beforeKeyPress` at 44, the vanilla call at 47, then `afterKeyPress` at 49-51 on every call with a non-null screen, whatever the screen answered, and the answer is returned unchanged at 53. Horizon's `Game::screen_key_post`, called by `Game::handle_window_event_inner` just before `mapped_key_event` and by `Game::control_command_inner` for the control socket's synthetic `key`, fires only when no screen arm took the key (`Screen.keyPressed` returning false), hands the key's vanilla name, the repeat flag and modifiers, and a `canceled` makes the game's own key handling skip the press entirely. Firing point (only the unhandled branch, plus a synthetic key Fabric has no counterpart for), arguments (`KeyEvent` scancode/keycode/modifiers versus a key name), cancel behaviour (a claim that skips the game's handling versus a `void` notice) and subscription differ. No key. |
| `screen_key_released_post` | **Gap:** `ScreenKeyboardEvents.afterKeyRelease` | [Screen keyboard events][screen-keyboard-events] lines 104-108 declare `afterKeyRelease` and lines 209-218 its `void` listener (line 217). [Screen keyboard mixin][screen-keyboard-mixin] lines 56-77 wrap `Screen.keyReleased(KeyEvent)` in `KeyboardHandler.keyPress`, firing `afterKeyRelease` at 72-74 on every call with a non-null screen and returning the screen's answer unchanged. Horizon's `Game::screen_key_post` (the release branch) fires it from `Game::handle_window_event_inner` only when the release Pre did not cancel and no screen arm took the key, hands the key name and modifiers, and a `canceled` skips the game's own release handling, so a held mapping stays down. The same firing-point, argument and cancel-behaviour differences hold as for the press, and Horizon's cancel deliberately suppresses a key-mapping release that Fabric's notice cannot affect. No key. |
| `screen_char_typed_post` | **Gap:** `ScreenKeyboardEvents.afterCharType` | [Screen keyboard events][screen-keyboard-events] lines 137-141 declare `afterCharType` and lines 244-252 its `void` listener (line 251), whose `CharacterEvent` carries the codepoint and modifiers. [Screen keyboard mixin][screen-keyboard-mixin] lines 79-100 wrap `Screen.charTyped(CharacterEvent)` in `KeyboardHandler.charTyped`, firing `afterCharType` at 95-97 on every call with a non-null screen. Horizon's `Game::screen_chars_post` is called by `Game::screen_chars_typed` after the key's text was routed and only when no text box of the screen took the characters, once per surviving printable character, with the character and modifiers, and its `canceled` counts it as handled. Fabric fires per `Screen.charTyped` call, handled or not, and its `void` answer cannot claim the character; Horizon also fires several times for one key whose text holds several characters and does not fire on Options and the title's own screens, where the text is routed in place. Coverage, arguments, cancel behaviour and subscription differ. No key. |

The Fabric points above come from Fabric's event declarations, mixins and javadoc, and
the Horizon points from Horizon's own call order; no decompiled 26.3 sources are present
in `.local/` for an independent vanilla check of the call sites. The change is metadata
only: no dispatch, tick-time work, adapter, alias or gameplay change.

[screen-mouse-events]: https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/blob/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222/fabric-screen-api-v1/src/client/java/net/fabricmc/fabric/api/client/screen/v1/ScreenMouseEvents.java
[screen-mouse-mixin]: https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/blob/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222/fabric-screen-api-v1/src/client/java/net/fabricmc/fabric/mixin/screen/MouseHandlerMixin.java
[screen-keyboard-events]: https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/blob/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222/fabric-screen-api-v1/src/client/java/net/fabricmc/fabric/api/client/screen/v1/ScreenKeyboardEvents.java
[screen-keyboard-mixin]: https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/blob/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222/fabric-screen-api-v1/src/client/java/net/fabricmc/fabric/mixin/screen/KeyboardHandlerMixin.java

## Event counterparts: data and resource reload and save (#1014)

Checked against the same Fabric API 26.3 revision,
`2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Fabric line numbers below are at that
revision; Horizon code is cited by function name (under `crates/horizon-client/src/`
unless the file says otherwise) and never by line number.

Scope: `base.rs`'s five data/resource-notice hooks (`world_saved`,
`datapack_sync`, `recipes_received`, `data_pack_files`,
`client_resources_reloaded`), `tags_updated.rs` and the three whole-file registration hooks
`add_server_reload_listeners.rs`, `add_client_reload_listeners.rs` and
`add_pack_finders.rs`, after the client lifecycle, effects/interaction/damage/chat,
server tick/level/chunk, player connection, item/tooltip, loot, entity lifecycle,
command, block/interaction and screen input parts in the earlier sections. All nine
record a gap; no key is added.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `world_saved` | **Gap:** `ServerLifecycleEvents.AFTER_SAVE` | [Server lifecycle API][sl-api] lines 119-126 declare `AFTER_SAVE` after a server finishes saving, with a `(server, flush, force)` listener. [Server mixin][server-mixin] lines 119-122 inject at `MinecraftServer.saveAllChunks` TAIL and pass `flush` and `force`; the mixin method takes a `CallbackInfoReturnable<Boolean>` (line 120), but does not inspect the return value. This alone does not establish what the boolean means or which early returns reach TAIL. Horizon's `Game::save_game_with` calls `Game::native_world_saved(wait)` after `Game::save_level`, `Game::save_parked_levels`, `Game::save_game_metadata` and `Game::finish_player_autosaves`, for the autosave in `Game::tick` (`wait` false), `Game::save_game` (true) and the shutdown path; it returns early on errors propagated from the first three calls, ignores the result of `Game::finish_player_autosaves`, and never runs with `remote` set. Side (server), the non-cancelling contract and the server-host substitution match, and `wait` carries the same fact as `flush`, but the listener gets no `force` flag. Arguments differ; failure-path equivalence is not proven, so no key. |
| `tags_updated` | **Gap:** `ServerLifecycleEvents.END_DATA_PACK_RELOAD`; also considered `AdvancementEvents.ALL_LOADED` and `LootTableEvents.ALL_LOADED` | [Server lifecycle API][sl-api] lines 99-108 declare `END_DATA_PACK_RELOAD` for after a server reloaded data packs, with a `(server, resourceManager, success)` listener; [server mixin][server-mixin] lines 105-112 fire it once the reload's future completes, on success and on failure, and only from `MinecraftServer.reloadResources`. Horizon's `Game::open_world` calls `Game::native_tags_updated` with `ServerDataLoad { reload: false }` on a world's first open, before chunks and players, and `Game::reload_world_tables` calls it with `reload: true` after `/reload`, once the players were sent the new data. Fabric's event covers only the reload half, passes the resource manager and a success flag, and also fires when the reload failed; Horizon also covers the first open, passes a reload flag, and does not fire on failure. [Advancement API][adv-api] lines 90-97 and [loot API][lt-api] lines 96-103 are per-registry post-processing inside the load (`resourceManager`, the advancement registry and lookups for advancements; `resourceManager` and the loot registry for loot), not the whole-table-built notice. [Advancement load mixin][data-adv-load] lines 41-50 and [loot load mixin][data-loot-load] lines 47-59 invoke them in the registry-load future. Also considered `CommonLifecycleEvents.TAGS_LOADED`: [common lifecycle API][data-common-api] lines 28-44 pass `(registries, client)`, with `client` distinguishing client synchronization from server loading, not first load from reload. [Tag update mixin][data-tags-mixin] lines 46-49 invoke it at `updateComponentsAndStaticRegistryTags` TAIL, a tag-update boundary rather than this whole-table notice. Arguments and coverage differ. Gap. |
| `datapack_sync` | **Gap:** `ServerLifecycleEvents.SYNC_DATA_PACK_CONTENTS` | [Server lifecycle API][sl-api] lines 80-88 declare the event as "about to send tag and recipe data to a player", with a `(player, joined)` listener. [Sync mixin][sl-sync-mixin] lines 33-49 fire it per joining player before the recipes packet and, after a reload, for every player separately before the tags packet. Horizon asks `datapack_sync` once the player exists after admission, before their recipes are sent, and once per online player (at most once per player per reload) before their recipes are re-sent; with nobody online it is not asked, passing the required `PlayerRef` and collecting only that recipient's recipe types. Configuration tags have already been sent when the join hook runs. Fabric's timing before tags and its lack of a recipe-type argument still differ. Gap. |
| `recipes_received` | **Gap:** `ClientRecipeSynchronizedEvent.EVENT` | [Recipe sync API][recipe-sync-api] lines 25-38 declare a client-only event for "when client receives all synchronized recipes", with a `(Minecraft client, SynchronizedRecipes recipes)` listener, invoked only when the server supports Fabric's recipe sync packets. [Recipe sync impl][recipe-sync-impl] lines 37-56 receive the payload, install it into the client recipe manager (line 54) and then invoke the event. Horizon's `Game::recipes_received_now` calls `Game::native_recipes_received(&types)` only while the window has a player; `Game::remote_recipe_content` calls it immediately on installation when a player exists, otherwise the pending notice is retried by `Game::extras_frame` on a later frame. It and passes the recipe types, with an empty list when the server stopped sending them; the recipes themselves are read through the helpers. Fabric also invokes its event for an empty payload (implementation lines 50-55); emptiness alone is not a difference. The player-gated timing and arguments (type ids versus the synchronized recipes object) differ. Gap. |
| `data_pack_files` | **Gap:** none | Fabric has no event that asks for `(path, bytes)` pack files during a data load. [Resource loader API][res-loader-api] lines 54-61 is `registerReloadListener(id, listener)` and [data resource loader API][data-res-loader-api] lines 48-66 is its data counterpart; both register a listener at mod initialisation. [Data resource store API][data-store-api] lines 20-60 is a typed `Key`/value store a reload listener fills in its apply phase, not raw JSON joining the pack stack; [resource loader API][res-loader-api] lines 78-98 `registerBuiltinPack` contributes a whole pack from the mod jar with an activation type, not per-load files. Horizon's `Game::build_world_tables` calls `Game::native_data_pack_files` before the tables are read, on a world's first open and on `/reload`, and takes the pushed files as a collect answer. No Fabric event covers that moment; no key. |
| `add_server_reload_listeners` | **Gap:** none | Fabric's nearest is the listener registration method, [data resource loader API][data-res-loader-api] lines 48-66 (and [resource loader API][res-loader-api] lines 54-61), called from a mod initialiser once with a listener object. The 26.3 index lists no event for `fabric-resource-loader-v1`, and its API holds registration methods, a loader interface and stores rather than events. Horizon's `Game::prepare_server_reload` calls `Game::native_add_server_reload_listeners` at the start of every data load (a world opening and `/reload`), handing the listener a registry to fill with listener ids and folders. Registration time, per-load dispatch and the event object differ; no key. |
| `add_client_reload_listeners` | **Gap:** none | Same registration boundary: [resource loader API][res-loader-api] lines 50-61 obtains the loader and registers one listener at initialisation; the module declares no event. [Client lifecycle API][cl-api] lines 28-49 offer only `CLIENT_STARTED` (once, while the splash screen shows) and `CLIENT_STOPPING`, neither of which is a resource reload. Horizon's `Game::register_client_reload_listeners` and `register_startup_client_reload_listeners` fire on the first load and on every later reload (a pack change, F3+T, a server's packs), first on the loader thread and later on the render thread, and collect listener registrations. No Fabric event; no key. |
| `add_pack_finders` | **Gap:** none | [Resource loader API][res-loader-api] lines 78-98 `registerBuiltinPack` registers one pack from the mod jar at initialisation, with an activation type; it is a registration method, not an event, and does not ask a mod to add discoverable packs per repository at startup and again when the repository is rediscovered. Horizon's `Game::discover_mod_packs` calls `Game::native_add_pack_finders` per pack type (`ClientResources` and `ServerData`) whenever a repository discovers its packs, collecting `PackRegistration`s. Timing, per-repository dispatch and the answer shape differ; no key. |
| `client_resources_reloaded` | **Gap:** `InvalidateRenderStateCallback.EVENT` | [Render state API][invalidate-state-api] lines 22-38 say the event is "called when the level renderer reloads, usually as result of changing resource pack or video configuration, or when the player types F3+A", and its javadoc at lines 26-29 warns the render chunks and dependent objects are made invalid *afterwards*; [render state mixin][invalidate-state-mixin] lines 74-77 fire it at `allChanged` HEAD. Horizon's `Game::apply_staged_pack` calls `Game::native_client_resources_reloaded` after the new resources were applied (atlas, models, renderer, caches), only after a successful resource reload, and not for video configuration, F3+A or initial startup. The other client events, [Client lifecycle API][cl-api] lines 28-49, cover only client start and stop. Firing point and coverage differ; no key. |

The Fabric points above come from Fabric's event declarations, mixins and javadoc, and
the Horizon points from Horizon's own call order; no decompiled 26.3 sources are present
in `.local/` for an independent vanilla check of the call sites. For `world_saved`, neither the boolean result meaning nor failure-path equivalence is
proven by the mixin; the missing `force` argument independently establishes the gap.
The change is metadata only: no
dispatch, tick-time work, adapter, alias or gameplay change.

## Event counterparts: block events (#1014)

Checked against the same Fabric API 26.3 revision,
`2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Fabric line numbers below are at that
revision; Horizon code is cited by function name (under `crates/horizon-client/src/`
unless the file says otherwise) and never by line number.

Scope: `base.rs`'s block-change, neighbour-notify, random-tick, scheduled-tick,
shape-update, fluid/fire-spread, piston, explosion and bucket hooks, after the
client lifecycle, effects/interaction/damage/chat, server tick/level/chunk, player
connection, item/tooltip, loot, entity lifecycle, command, block/interaction,
screen input and data/reload parts in the earlier sections. All ten record a gap;
no key is added. The Fabric 26.3 index lists no event for a committed block change,
a neighbour notification, a random or scheduled block tick, a shape update, a piston
move or an explosion, and the events that exist near the other two hooks cover only
part of their moment.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `block_changed` | **Gap:** none | Fabric's 26.3 event index has no event for a committed `Level.setBlock`. The nearest event is [block transformer API][block-transformer-api] lines 52-59, whose `MODIFY` callback edits transformer definitions (`key`, `transforms`, `source`, registry lookup) while a data pack loads, not a block write; [Fabric block API][fabric-block-api] lines 42-58 is an interface a block implements for appearance, not an event. Horizon's `GameLevel::observe_block_changed` (`game.rs`) is called by `Sim::chunk_set_block_state` (`crates/horizon-world/src/level/update.rs`) immediately after `set_raw` accepted a different state, before neighbour updates and side effects, and passes the dimension, position, and old and new state ids. No Fabric event at that moment; no key. |
| `neighbor_changed` | **Gap:** none | Fabric has no neighbour-notification event: [block events][block-events-api] lines 40-57 declares only `USE_ITEM_ON` and `USE_WITHOUT_ITEM`, and `PlayerBlockBreakEvents` covers a player breaking a block. Horizon's `Sim::neighbor_changed_oriented` (`crates/horizon-world/src/level/hooks.rs`) calls `LevelAccess::observe_neighbor_changed` before the block's own `neighborChanged` chain, and `GameLevel::observe_neighbor_changed` (`game.rs`) fires it with the level's ticks, the changed block id and `moved`, so a part can read the inputs and schedule in this call. No Fabric event; no key. |
| `random_tick` | **Gap:** none | Fabric has no random-tick event. [Content registries block mixin][random-tick-cache-mixin] lines 31-45 only refresh a cached randomly-ticking flag when a mod's block behaviour changes; the 26.3 index lists no event around `ServerLevel.tickChunk` or `BlockState.randomTick`. Horizon's `Sim::random_tick_at` (`crates/horizon-world/src/level/tick.rs`) calls `before_random_tick` only for a randomly-ticking block, before `random_tick_block`, and a `handled` answer skips the block's own tick while lava's fluid random tick still runs. No Fabric event; no key. |
| `tick_block` | **Gap:** none | Fabric has no scheduled-block-tick event; the same index lists none around `ServerLevel.tickBlock`, and none of the interaction or lifecycle APIs above declares one. Horizon's `Sim::tick_due_block` (`crates/horizon-world/src/level/tick.rs`) calls `before_tick_block` after the due tick was matched to the block still at the position, before `tick_block`, and a `handled` answer skips the block's tick. No Fabric event; no key. |
| `update_shape` | **Gap:** none | Fabric has no shape-update event: the 26.3 index and the interaction, block and lifecycle APIs above declare no event around `BlockBehaviour.updateShape`. Horizon's `Sim::update_shape` (`crates/horizon-world/src/level/shapes.rs`) calls `before_update_shape` before `update_shape_at`, with the position, the state that may not be in the world yet, the direction, the neighbour position and the neighbour state, and takes a replacement state (`replace` contract, `new`) or lets vanilla run. No Fabric event; no key. |
| `block_spread` | **Gap:** `FluidFlowEvents.ALLOW` (partial) | [Fluid flow API][fluid-flow-api] lines 29-38 and 61-72 declare `ALLOW` with a `(FluidState, LevelAccessor, BlockPos)` listener, where any `false` vetoes. It fires at [flowing fluid mixin][fluid-flow-mixin] lines 41-48 (`FlowingFluid.spreadTo` HEAD, skipped when the target is lava), [lava fluid mixin][lava-fluid-mixin] lines 40-45 (lava's `spreadTo` HEAD) and [liquid block mixin][liquid-block-mixin] lines 39-44 (`LiquidBlock.shouldSpreadLiquid` HEAD, a neighbour-update check at the block's own position). Horizon's `Sim::spread_to` (`crates/horizon-world/src/level/fluids.rs`) calls `LevelAccess::allow_spread(Spread::Fluid, from, pos)` at the top of `spread_to`, before the water-to-stone override, and `Sim::fire_tick` (`crates/horizon-world/src/level/tick.rs`) calls the same hook for `Spread::Fire` before igniting. `GameLevel::allow_spread` (`game.rs`) passes the kind (`"fluid"` or `"fire"`), `from` and `to`. The fluid half shares the veto shape, but the hook also covers fire spread, which has no Fabric counterpart, and Fabric also fires on a liquid block's neighbour update, which Horizon's hook does not; the arguments (a source fluid state versus a kind string) differ too. A partial match is not a key; gap. |
| `piston_pre` | **Gap:** none | Fabric 26.3 has no piston event: searching every `api/**/*.java` file at the pinned revision finds `Piston` only in tag, loot-condition and networking examples, and the index lists none. Horizon's `Sim::piston_move_blocks` (`crates/horizon-world/src/level/pistons.rs`) calls `LevelAccess::allow_piston_move` after `piston_resolve` returned its push and destroy lists, before moving those lists (a retracting piston may already have removed its head); `GameLevel::allow_piston_move` (`game.rs`) passes the piston position, push direction, `extending`, and the push and destroy positions, and a cancel returns `None`, skipping the remaining push/destroy work without undoing that earlier head removal. No Fabric event; no key. |
| `piston_post` | **Gap:** none | Same absence: no Fabric event fires after a piston move. `Sim::piston_move_blocks` (`crates/horizon-world/src/level/pistons.rs`) calls `LevelAccess::observe_piston_moved` at its end, after the neighbour updates and only on the moving return; `GameLevel::observe_piston_moved` (`game.rs`) passes the same positions without a veto. No Fabric event; no key. |
| `explosion_detonate` | **Gap:** none | Fabric 26.3 has no explosion event: searching every `api/**/*.java` file at the pinned revision finds `Explosion` only in a loot-condition example and a networking example, and the index lists none. Horizon's `Game::server_explode_as` (`game/entities/explosion.rs`) computes the targets with `Game::exploded_positions`, then calls `Game::native_explosion_detonate` before any entity is hurt or block broken; the listener receives the dimension, the centre and the mutable position list, and removing positions spares those blocks. No Fabric event; no key. |
| `fill_bucket` | **Gap:** `UseItemCallback` / `ItemEvents.USE` (partial) | [Use item callback][use-item-callback] lines 27-35 and 36-52 fire at [server interaction mixin][interaction-server] lines 96-105 (`ServerPlayerGameMode.useItem` HEAD, before the spectator and cooldown checks) for every item use, with `(player, level, hand)` and an `InteractionResult` that can force success or failure. [Item events][item-events-api] lines 53-68 and 82-94 fire from [item stack mixin][item-stack-mixin] lines 36-45, wrapping the `Item.use` call itself and able to replace its result, again with no stack, target position or item filter. Horizon's `Game::use_tool` (`game.rs`) fires `Game::native_fill_bucket(stack, pos, &mut canceled)` only for a bucket, after a raycast chose a position and Horizon's `may_build`/`can_place_on_in_adventure` permission guard, once for the target and possibly again for the adjacent position a fluid bucket falls back to; it passes the stack and the target position, and a cancel answers `Fail`. Firing point, arguments, coverage and contract differ; gap. |

The Fabric points above come from Fabric's event declarations, mixins and javadoc,
and the Horizon points from Horizon's own call order; no decompiled 26.3 sources are
present in `.local/` for an independent vanilla check of the call sites. The change
is metadata only: no dispatch, tick-time work, adapter, alias or gameplay change.

## Event counterparts: block entities (#1014)

Fabric evidence is at `fabric-26.3/` pinned `2065040b…`. Horizon call sites are named
by function. Fabric's only block entity events are the two load/unload pairs below;
it has no event for a block entity tick, update tag, container, tanks, energy
buffer, renderer, render bounds or view distance. No decompiled 26.3 sources are
present in `.local/`; ordering claims rest on Horizon's own call order and on
Fabric's mixins and javadoc.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `block_entity` | **Gap:** `ServerBlockEntityEvents.BLOCK_ENTITY_LOAD` / `ClientBlockEntityEvents.BLOCK_ENTITY_LOAD` (partial) | [server block entity events][sbe-api] lines 35 and 53-59 declare a notify with `(BlockEntity, ServerLevel)`; [client block entity events][cbe-api] lines 35 and 54 do the same with `ClientLevel`. They fire at [server chunk mixin][sbe-mixin] lines 50-59 (`LevelChunk.setBlockEntity`, a `ModifyExpressionValue` on the map `put`) and [client chunk mixin][cbe-mixin] lines 47-57: after the instance exists in the chunk's map, when its data may not be loaded yet. Horizon's `GameLevel::new_block_entity` (`game/native_hooks/shared.rs`) calls `GameLevel::native_block_entity` before constructing the entity; `GameLevel::native_set_block` then inserts it after the block write, to get its initial saved compound only. No level is passed, and the answer creates the entity instead of hearing about one. Different firing point, arguments and contract (create versus notify): gap. |
| `block_entity_ticks` | **Gap:** none | Vanilla asks `EntityBlock.getTicker`. Fabric 26.3 declares no ticker event: no `Ticker` or `BlockEntityTicker` in any `api/**/*.java` at the pin, and the index lists none. Horizon's `Game::ticking_states` (`game/blocks/block_entity_ticks.rs`) calls `Game::native_ticks` for each non-`minecraft:` state when `Game::tick_block_entities` builds the ticking table once (guarded by `world.block_entities.has_states()`); the listener only sets `ticks`. No Fabric event; gap. |
| `block_entity_tick` | **Gap:** none | No block entity tick event in Fabric 26.3 (same search as above). Horizon's `Game::tick_block_entities` (`game/blocks/block_entity_ticks.rs`) walks `world.block_entity_tickers()` in vanilla's list order and calls `Game::tick_mod_block_entity` (`game/native_hooks/shared.rs`), which takes the entity out of the world and calls `Game::api_block_entity_tick` with mutable saved `nbt`, item slots and data slots. No Fabric event at that moment; gap. |
| `block_entity_update_tag` | **Gap:** none | Fabric declares no event around `BlockEntity.getUpdateTag` or `sendBlockUpdated` (no such name in any `api/**/*.java` at the pin apart from an attachment javadoc, and the index lists none). Horizon's `Game::lan_host` (`game/network/lan/mod.rs`) installs `ChunkEncoder::mod_update_tag`, whose closure calls `Game::api_block_entity_update_tag` while a chunk is encoded (the hook doc also names block changes and far chunk sends); the hook returns the compound clients merge into their copy. No Fabric event; gap. |
| `Registrar::block_container` | **Gap:** `ItemStorage.SIDED` (partial) | [item storage][item-storage] lines 85-86 declare a `BlockApiLookup<Storage<ItemVariant>, Direction>`, not an event: a provider is queried per `(level, pos, state, blockEntity, direction)` at use time and returns storage for that side. Horizon registers one `ModContainer` descriptor for a `BlockKey` at startup; game container tables and registration-time `ITEMS` coverage consume that same declaration. The descriptor defines slots, faces, input/output lists and menu, not a live per-position provider. No event key. |
| `Registrar::block_fluids` | **Gap:** `FluidStorage.SIDED` (partial) | [fluid storage][fluid-storage] lines 67-68 declare a `BlockApiLookup<Storage<FluidVariant>, Direction>` queried per position and side. Horizon registers one `ModFluidTanks` descriptor for a `BlockKey` at startup; game tank tables and registration-time `FLUIDS` coverage consume that same declaration. Tank capacities, faces and fill/drain lists are configuration, not a live lookup callback. No event key. |
| `Registrar::block_energy` | **Gap:** none | Fabric 26.3 has no energy API at the pin (the transfer module covers items and fluids only). Horizon registers one `ModEnergyBuffer` for a `BlockKey` at startup, defining capacity, per-call limits and faces; game energy tables and registration-time `ENERGY` coverage consume that same declaration. No Fabric counterpart or event key. |
| `block_entity_submit` | **Gap:** `BlockEntityRendererRegistry.register` (registration only) | [block entity renderer registry][be-renderer-registry] lines 29-47 register a `BlockEntityRendererProvider` per `BlockEntityType` (the class is deprecated in favour of the access widener), and the provider builds a renderer when the dispatcher is initialised. That is registration, not an event, and it carries no per-draw call. Horizon's `Game::native_draw_scene` (`game/modding/native_scene.rs`) calls `Game::native_block_entity_submit` for scene entities; `Game::mod_block_entity_geometry` (`game/modding/native_block_entity_draw.rs`) calls `Game::api_block_entity_submit` for eligible visible mod block entities after distance and frustum checks, unless retained geometry is reused. The hook receives position, block, state, the entity snapshot and a draw context. Gap. |
| `block_entity_render_bounding_box` | **Gap:** none | Fabric declares no render-bounds event in its API packages, and the index lists no event. Horizon's `Game::mod_block_entity_geometry` (`game/modding/native_block_entity_draw.rs`) calls `Game::api_block_entity_render_bounding_box` after the view-distance check and before the frustum test, collecting six floats; retained geometry reuses its cached bounds and bypasses the hook. No Fabric event; gap. |
| `block_entity_view_distance` | **Gap:** none | Fabric 26.3 has no `getViewDistance` hook in its api packages (search over `api/**/*.java` at the pin) and no event in the index. Horizon's `Game::view_distances` (`game/modding/native_block_entity_draw.rs`) asks `Game::native_block_entity_view_distance` once for each mod block the first time the distances are needed and keeps the answer. Gap. |

The change is docs only: no dispatch, tick-time work, adapter, alias or
gameplay change.

## Event counterparts: screen and HUD rendering (#1014)

Fabric evidence is at `fabric-26.3/` pinned `2065040b…`. Horizon call sites are named
by function. Fabric's screen render events are per-screen events registered on one
`Screen` instance, and its HUD extension points are registrations of `HudElement`s,
not per-frame events. No decompiled 26.3 sources are present in `.local/`; ordering
claims rest on Horizon's own call order and on Fabric's mixins and javadoc.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `screen_render_pre` | **Gap:** `ScreenEvents.beforeExtract` (partial) | [screen events][screen-events] lines 123 and 200-203 declare a per-screen notify with `(Screen, GuiGraphicsExtractor, mouseX, mouseY, tickDelta)` and no cancellation; [the GUI mixin][screen-gui-mixin] lines 88-89 invokes it just before the whole screen extraction (`Gui.extractRenderState` -> `Screen.extractRenderStateWithTooltipAndSubtitles`). Horizon's `Game::native_screen_render_pre` (`game/native_hooks/screen_render_stages.rs`) is reached through `Game::screen_render_begin` (`game/native_hooks/shared.rs`) from `Game::draw_screen_with_pre_hook` and `Game::draw_title_screen_with_pre_hook`; it is cancellable and a cancel skips the screen's drawing and its background hook. It covers the game screens admitted by `Game::screen_render_view` and the title screens admitted by `Game::title_screen_render_view`, excluding gameplay overlays such as server prompts and books. It passes `ScreenView` (the screen's id, menu type and title, slot count, panel rect, mouse and partial tick) and the draw batch, not the `Screen` instance or Fabric's `GuiGraphicsExtractor`. Cancel behaviour, arguments and coverage differ: gap. |
| `screen_render_background` | **Gap:** `ScreenEvents.afterBackground` (partial) | [screen events][screen-events] lines 134 and 205-208 declare the per-screen notify with the same arguments; [the screen mixin][screen-mixin] lines 121-124 invokes it immediately after vanilla's `Screen.extractBackground` and before `extractRenderState`, with no cancellation. Horizon's `Game::native_screen_render_background` is called by `Game::screen_render_begin` before the screen draws anything; for Horizon's own screens their background panel is drawn inside that same drawing, so the hook runs before the background rather than after it (its own doc says so). It is not cancellable, and its arguments are `ScreenView` and the draw batch. Firing point, arguments and coverage differ: gap. |
| `screen_render_foreground` | **Gap:** `ScreenEvents.afterForeground` (partial) | [screen events][screen-events] lines 145 and 210-213 declare the per-screen notify; [the screen mixin][screen-mixin] lines 126-131 fires it right after `Screen.extractRenderState` for screens that are not container screens, and [the container-screen mixin][container-screen-mixin] lines 40-43 fires it after `AbstractContainerScreen.extractContents` for container screens. Both points are before the deferred tooltips and subtitles, and the event cannot cancel. Horizon's `Game::native_screen_render_foreground` is fired by `Game::screen_render_end` after the screen has drawn its tooltips and dragged stack, immediately before `Game::native_screen_render_post` (its own doc says so), and receives `ScreenView` plus the draw batch. Firing point and arguments differ: gap. |
| `screen_render_post` | **Gap:** `ScreenEvents.afterExtract` (partial) | [screen events][screen-events] lines 156 and 215-218 declare the per-screen notify with `(Screen, GuiGraphicsExtractor, mouseX, mouseY, tickDelta)`; [the GUI mixin][screen-gui-mixin] lines 88-91 invokes it after the screen extraction operation returns, for every extracted screen, and it cannot cancel. Horizon's `Game::native_screen_render_post` (`game/native_hooks/base.rs`) is fired by `Game::screen_render_end` for the game screens admitted by `Game::screen_render_view` and the title screens admitted by `Game::title_screen_render_view`: the game-screen path skips `Screen::Playing` (the server dialogs, server pack, link and code-of-conduct prompts, a book and a sign editor drawn over gameplay) as well as the server pack prompt and an open book, while Fabric's event still fires for those screens. It passes `ScreenView` and the draw batch. Coverage and arguments differ: gap. |
| `screen_preedit_pre` | **Gap:** none | Fabric 26.3 has no preedit or IME event: no `preedit` in any `api/**/*.java` at the pin and no such entry in the index. `ScreenKeyboardEvents` (allow, before and after key press and char type) are key events, not vanilla's `KeyboardHandler.textEditing`. Horizon's `Game::ime_preedit` (`game/interface/ime.rs`) calls `Game::native_screen_preedit_pre` before the focused field's handler with the composed text, caret byte and blocks, and a cancel keeps the composition from every field. No Fabric event at that moment; gap. |
| `screen_preedit_post` | **Gap:** none | The same absence as above. Horizon's `Game::ime_preedit` calls `Game::native_screen_preedit_post` only when no field took the composition and the pre hook did not cancel, with the same text, caret and blocks. No Fabric preedit event and no Fabric event that reports an unclaimed composition; gap. |
| `screen_render_inventory_mob_effects` | **Gap:** none | Fabric 26.3 declares no event around `EffectsInInventory.extractRenderState`: no `EffectsInInventory` or `RenderInventoryMobEffects` in any `api/**/*.java` at the pin, and the index lists none. Horizon's `Game::inventory_effects_layout` (`game/items/potions.rs`) calls `Game::native_screen_render_inventory_mob_effects` when effects are shown and at least 32 GUI pixels are free, with `available_space`, mutable `compact` and `horizontal_offset`, and a cancel that hides the panels. No Fabric event; gap. |
| `gui_layer_pre` | **Gap:** `HudElementRegistry.attachElementBefore` / `replaceElement` (registration only) | [the HUD element registry][hud-element-registry] lines 107 and 152 register a `HudElement` before a vanilla element, or replace that element once; the element's [single callback][hud-element] line 35 gets only `(GuiGraphicsExtractor, DeltaTracker)` each frame. [The HUD mixin][hud-mixin] lines 47 onward wrap each vanilla HUD method so attached elements draw inside it. An attached element cannot veto the vanilla layer; a replacement can wrap the original and choose each frame whether to invoke it, but exposes no shared veto event or layer-id argument. Both inherit the vanilla element's render condition (registry lines 101 and 143). Horizon's `Game::gui_layer` and `Game::draw_mod_gui_layers` (`game/native_hooks/shared.rs`) call `Game::native_gui_layer_pre` for every vanilla and mod layer with its id, and a cancel hides that layer for the frame. Contract, arguments and firing units differ: gap. |
| `gui_layer_post` | **Gap:** `HudElementRegistry.attachElementAfter` (registration only) | [the HUD element registry][hud-element-registry] lines 123-127 register an element after a vanilla element; its [callback][hud-element] line 35 runs every frame with only the extractor and delta tracker, bound at registration to one element and inheriting that element's render condition. Horizon's `Game::gui_layer` and `Game::draw_mod_gui_layers` call `Game::native_gui_layer_post` after every vanilla and mod layer, even when the pre hook hid it, with the layer id and the draw batch. This callback is bound to one element rather than dispatched for every layer id, and the arguments differ: gap. |
| `gui_layer_draw` | **Gap:** `HudElement` registration (partial) | [the HUD element registry][hud-element-registry] lines 107 and 123 attach a `HudElement` that draws itself through its [callback][hud-element] line 35; there is no event that asks a mod to draw one of the layer ids the game owns. Horizon's `Game::draw_mod_gui_layers` (`game/native_hooks/shared.rs`) calls `Game::native_gui_layer_draw` for each registered mod layer id, between that layer's pre and post hooks, and only when the pre hook did not hide it. Fabric's element is the layer and is registered once; Horizon's hook is asked per mod layer per frame with a layer id and a draw batch. Contract and arguments differ: gap. |
| `gui_render_post` | **Gap:** none | Fabric declares no event after the complete GUI batch. A nearby slot is a `HudElement` attached after `VanillaHudElements.SUBTITLES`, documented as "render after everything" ([the HUD element registry][hud-element-registry] lines 70-71 and 123): still a `HudElement` inside the HUD element tree, registered once, drawing only with its `(extractor, delta)` callback and inheriting the subtitles' render condition. The registry also offers `addLast` (lines 35-36 and 87-95), which inherits no render condition but still draws inside the HUD pass, not after screens and IME. Horizon's `Game::native_gui_render_post` (`game/native_hooks/gui_render_post.rs`) is called from `Game::render` after `Game::hud` and after `Game::flush_ime`, with the whole batch and the `gui_hidden` and `focused` flags, even with F1 or a menu open; screens, prompts, toasts and IME are outside the HUD element tree. No Fabric event at that moment; gap. |

The change is docs only: no dispatch, tick-time work, adapter, alias or
gameplay change.

## Event counterparts: item drawing, tooltips and default components (#1014)

Fabric evidence is at `fabric-26.3/` pinned `2065040b…`. Horizon call sites are named
by function. Scope: the remaining item hooks in `hooks/base.rs` that concern how an
item is drawn, its tooltip and its default components: `item_bar`,
`item_decorations`, `tooltip`, `tooltip_component`, `tooltip_component_draw`,
`component_types` and `modify_default_components`. All seven record a gap; no keys
are added.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `item_bar` | **Gap:** none; nearest `ExtractItemDecorationsCallback.EVENT` (partial) | Fabric 26.3 declares no event for the item bar, the value vanilla's `Item.isBarVisible`/`getBarWidth`/`getBarColor` produce. [The item decorations callback][extract-item-decorations-api] line 34 runs at the end of `GuiGraphicsExtractor.itemDecorations`, after that bar has been chosen and drawn, and its listener gets the graphics, font, stack and x/y only: it cannot replace or hide the bar. Horizon's `Game::draw_item_decorations` (`game.rs`) chooses the bar (the damage bar, or a bundle's fullness), passes it to `Game::native_item_bar` as a mutable `Option<(i32, u32)>` and paints the answer. Different moment and contract, and no event at the bar query itself: gap. |
| `item_decorations` | **Gap:** `ExtractItemDecorationsCallback.EVENT` (partial) | [The item decorations callback][extract-item-decorations-api] lines 34-49 declare an array-backed notify with `(GuiGraphicsExtractor, Font, ItemStack, int x, int y)` and no cancellation; [its mixin][extract-item-decorations-mixin] lines 33-41 inject at `GuiGraphicsExtractor.itemDecorations` `RETURN`, guarded by a non-empty stack, so it fires after the durability bar, cooldown shade and stack count. That is the point of Horizon's `Game::native_item_decorations`, which `Game::draw_item_decorations` (`game.rs`) calls after it paints its port of those three decorations; both are client-side and non-cancelling. The arguments differ: the Fabric listener gets the `Font` and the live `GuiGraphicsExtractor`, while the Horizon listener gets the stack, `f32` GUI-unit x/y and a deferred draw context (`HudBatch`), with no font argument (text is drawn through the context's text and text_width helpers). Firing coverage is not proven equal either: Fabric fires for every qualifying `itemDecorations` call, while Horizon fires only where `Game::draw_item_decorations` is used, which the `Game::native_item_decorations` doc lists as the hotbar, menus, tooltips and the advancement, beacon, stats and trading screens. Arguments and coverage differ: gap. |
| `tooltip` | **Gap:** `ItemTooltipCallback.EVENT` (partial) | [The item tooltip API][item-tooltip-api] lines 29-46 declare the only item tooltip event, whose javadoc says it fires "after the game has appended all base tooltip lines to the list"; [its mixin][item-tooltip-mixin] line 38 injects at `ItemStack.getTooltipLines` `RETURN` ordinal 1, the return that has lines. Horizon's `tooltip` hook is the item's own `Item.appendHoverText`: `Game::stack_tooltip_lines` (`game/blocks/stations.rs`) calls `Game::native_tooltip` after the name and the item's own hover lines but before the components' lines (enchantments, lore, attributes), only when the stack is not a crossbow projectile and the line list is non-empty. Fabric's event fires after every line is appended, so the point is later; its listener gets the stack, `Item.TooltipContext`, `TooltipFlag` and the complete line list, against Horizon's stack and an empty list of lines to add. Firing point, arguments and coverage (the `item_tooltip` row above records Fabric's extra search-index calls) differ: gap. |
| `tooltip_component` | **Gap:** `ClientTooltipComponentCallback.EVENT` | [The client tooltip component callback][client-tooltip-component-api] line 37 declares an array-backed event whose listener gets the `TooltipComponent` data and returns a `ClientTooltipComponent` or null; [its mixin][client-tooltip-component-mixin] lines 31-41 inject at `ClientTooltipComponent.create` `HEAD` and set the returned component. Its key is the component data object (what `Item.getTooltipImage` returned), and the returned object supplies both its measured size and its drawing. Horizon's `tooltip_component` hook is keyed by the item stack, is asked per drawn stack for a size alone, and draws through the separate `tooltip_component_draw` hook; `Game::draw_stack_tooltip_with` (`game/blocks/stations.rs`) asks `Game::native_tooltip_component` before laying the tooltip out. Different key, arguments and unit of work (one Fabric object against two Horizon hooks): gap. |
| `tooltip_component_draw` | **Gap:** `ClientTooltipComponentCallback.EVENT` (partial) | The same [callback][client-tooltip-component-api] returns a `ClientTooltipComponent` that draws itself through the tooltip renderer; Fabric declares no separate event for drawing a custom tooltip component. Horizon's `Game::draw_stack_tooltip_with` (`game/blocks/stations.rs`) calls `Game::native_tooltip_component_draw` once per measured image, at the origin the tooltip layout computed, with the stack, the draw context and the size. The Fabric callback runs once at component creation, before layout, with no draw surface; the Horizon hook runs at draw time with a surface. Moment, arguments and split differ: gap. |
| `component_types` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no event for registering a data component type: `fabric-item-api-v1`'s item API has `DefaultItemComponentEvents`, `EnchantmentEvents`, `BlockTransformerEvents`, `ItemClickBehaviorCallback` and `ItemTooltipCallback`, and its nearest component helpers are the static `ItemComponentTooltipProviderRegistry.addFirst` and `FabricComponentMapBuilder`, neither an event that registers a component type; the index lists no component type event. Horizon's `register_components` (`game/modding/mods/mod.rs`) collects ids through `Game::native_component_types` with a `RegistrationContext` before content loads, then installs them as namespaced NBT types. No Fabric event at that moment: gap. |
| `modify_default_components` | **Gap:** `DefaultItemComponentEvents.MODIFY` (partial) | [The default item component events][default-item-component-events] line 38 declare a single event invocation. Its [implementation][default-item-component-impl] lines 31-52 calls every listener once with a `ModifyContext` over `BuiltInRegistries.ITEM`, and each `modify` call walks the whole item registry, matches a predicate and hands the listener a `DataComponentMap.Builder`, a `HolderLookup.Provider` and the `Item`. [Its mixin][default-item-component-mixin] lines 36-53 target `DataComponentInitializers$1` (the pending-components implementation) and fire it at that object's `apply` `RETURN` for the item registry. Horizon's hook is per item: `ItemData::load` (`crates/horizon-world/src/item_defaults.rs`) calls `ItemDefaults::apply_native`, which calls `native_modify_default_components` once for each entry in the generated `items.json` report, with the item's id string, a JSON component map and the load `LoadContext`. Both are non-cancelling and run on both sides; exact firing-point equality between Fabric's pending-components application and Horizon's report load is not proven. The arguments and call shape do not match: one listener call with a registry-wide context and `Item` objects against one call per report entry with an id string and JSON. The hook also cannot modify an item that is missing from `items.json`, a coverage difference that is not proven equal. Gap. |

The change is docs only: no dispatch, tick-time work, adapter, alias or gameplay
change.
