## Event counterparts: taming, anvils, armor wear and other misc hooks (#1014)

Fabric evidence is at `fabric-26.3/` pinned `2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Horizon call
sites are named by function. Scope: the eight `misc-add` hooks `add_attribute_tooltips`,
`affect_neighbors_after_removal`, `animal_tame`, `anvil_update`, `armor_hurt`, `arrow_nock`,
`baby_entity_spawn` and `block_jump_factor`. All eight record a gap; no keys are added.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `add_attribute_tooltips` | **Gap:** `ItemTooltipCallback.EVENT` (partial) | [The item tooltip API][item-tooltip-api] lines 29-46 declare the only item tooltip event, whose javadoc says it fires "after the game has appended all base tooltip lines"; [its mixin][item-tooltip-mixin] line 38 injects at `ItemStack.getTooltipLines` `RETURN` ordinal 1, so the listener sees the finished list, stack, `Item.TooltipContext` and `TooltipFlag`, with no player argument. Horizon's `Game::native_add_attribute_tooltips` is asked from the `extra` call at the stack's `ItemStack.addAttributeTooltips` point, mid-`getTooltipLines`, with the player UUID and entity id instead of the object, `shown`, `advanced` and `creative`, and a fresh vector whose lines land beside the attribute block. Firing point and arguments differ: gap. |
| `affect_neighbors_after_removal` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no event at `Block.affectNeighborsAfterRemoval`: the block API has no neighbour-update event and the pinned index lists none. Horizon's `GameLevel::native_affect_neighbors_after_removal` is a per-block collect hook that `GameLevel::affect_neighbors_after_removal` fires inside the authoritative level write, letting the block answer with ordered `OutputSignal`, `Neighbours` and `NeighboursExcept` updates. No Fabric event at that moment or with that collect contract: gap. |
| `animal_tame` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 has no taming event: the pinned index lists no `Tame` event, only the life-cycle, sleep, damage and conversion events in `fabric-entity-events-v1`, none of which runs between a successful tame roll and the ownership assignment. Horizon's `Game::animal_tame_cancelled` asks `Game::native_animal_tame` with the tamer UUID and entity id, the mob entity id and kind, and returns whether the attempt fails. No Fabric event carries those arguments or that cancel contract: gap. |
| `anvil_update` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no anvil event; the pinned index has no `Anvil` entry, and `ScreenEvents` concerns client screen initialization, ticking and rendering, not at `AnvilMenu.createResult`. Horizon's `Game::api_anvil_update` asks `Game::native_anvil_update` after vanilla's result exists, on both sides, with the player, position and a mutable `AnvilUpdate` (inputs, rename text, output, xp and material cost) plus cancel. No Fabric event at that moment with those arguments: gap. |
| `armor_hurt` | **Gap:** none; nearest `ServerLivingEntityEvents.ALLOW_DAMAGE` (different contract) | [The living entity events][sliving-api] line 36 declare `ALLOW_DAMAGE`, fired by [the living mixin][sliving-mixin] line 82 at `LivingEntity.hurtServer` before damage is applied, with the entity, source and amount; cancelling vetoes the whole hit. Horizon's `Game::hurt_armor` (and `Game::hurt_mob_armor` for mobs) builds one `ArmorHurt` entry per worn piece, with slot, stack, vanilla damage and a mutable `new_damage`, then asks `Game::native_armor_hurt`; cancel suppresses armor wear only. Per-piece granularity, the mutable wear values and the moment after vanilla computed the damage have no Fabric counterpart: gap. |
| `arrow_nock` | **Gap:** `UseItemCallback.EVENT` (partial) | [The use item callback][use-item-callback] lines 28-52 is fired by [the server game mode mixin][spgm-mixin] lines 96-105 at `ServerPlayerGameMode.useItem` `HEAD`, before the item's own `use` runs, with the player, level and hand, and does not know whether ammunition was found. Horizon's `Game::native_arrow_nock` is asked from the bow branch of `Game::use_hand` after checking `getProjectile` and creative mode, even when both checks are false, with the player UUID and name, bow stack, hand and `has_ammo`, and cancel prevents starting bow use. Fabric runs on both sides and can return `SUCCESS`, `PASS` or `FAIL`; Horizon is server-side with a boolean veto of bow use. Firing point, side, arguments and return contract differ: gap. |
| `baby_entity_spawn` | **Gap:** `ServerEntityEvents.ALLOW_LOAD` / `ENTITY_LOAD` (partial) | Fabric declares no breeding-specific event: [the lifecycle API][sentity-api] lines 43-60 offers non-cancelling `ENTITY_LOAD` after insertion and veto-only `ALLOW_LOAD` before insertion, with an already-created entity, level, spawn reason and loaded-from-disk flag. [The callbacks mixin][sentity-callbacks-mixin] lines 38-40 fires `ENTITY_LOAD` at tracking-start `TAIL`; [the section manager mixin][misc-add-entity-manager] lines 34-40 fires `ALLOW_LOAD` at `addEntity` `HEAD`. Horizon's `Game::breed` asks `Game::native_baby_entity_spawn` before creating the child, with both parent entity ids, optional causing-player UUID and entity id, child kind and a cancel that skips the baby and breeding rewards. Even the cancellable Fabric event runs after entity creation, covers all entity insertions and lacks the breeding arguments: firing point, arguments and coverage differ. |
| `block_jump_factor` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no event for `Block.getJumpFactor`; the pinned index lists no block-factor event, and `DimensionEvents.MODIFY_ATTRIBUTES` changes a dimension's environment attributes, not a block state's jump factor. Horizon's `native_block_jump_factor` (`motion/surface.rs`, called through `ModBlockFactors::jump`) is a non-cancelling listen hook on both sides that hands the loop context, state and a mutable factor. No Fabric event at that query: gap. |

The change is docs only: no dispatch, tick-time work, adapter, alias or gameplay
change.

[misc-add-entity-manager]: https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/blob/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222/fabric-lifecycle-events-v1/src/main/java/net/fabricmc/fabric/mixin/event/lifecycle/PersistentEntitySectionManagerMixin.java

## Event counterparts: base visuals, zoom, minimap and Horizon's own screens (#1014)

Fabric evidence is at `fabric-26.3/` pinned `2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Horizon call
sites are named by function. Scope: the eleven `base` hooks `visuals`, `emissive`, `zoom`, `minimap`,
`setting_changed`, `panel_event`, `screen`, `screen_draw`, `screen_click`, `screen_tooltip`
and `menu_button`. All eleven record a gap; no keys are added.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `visuals` | **Gap:** none; nearest `LevelRenderEvents.COLLECT_SUBMITS` | [The level render events][level-render-events] line 71 declare `COLLECT_SUBMITS`, fired by [the level renderer mixin][level-renderer-mixin] line 92 at `LevelRenderer.submitFeatures` `RETURN`, with the frame's `LevelRenderContext` whose `submitNodeCollector()` a listener may add to. Horizon's `Game::native_visuals` is a non-cancelling listen hook asked by `native_frame_visuals` only while a world is visible, handing two empty vectors that native mods fill with block rims and colored-light sources for Horizon to validate and draw. No Fabric event has that collect contract or those arguments: gap. |
| `emissive` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no event for per-block emissive sprite layers; the pinned index has no per-sprite or emissive-texture entry. Horizon's `Game::native_emissive` is a client collect hook asked by `Game::render_with` for up to 32 pairs of already-loaded base and transparent overlay sprite ids, which Horizon's `native_emissive::table` turns into full-bright quads. Firing point and arguments have no Fabric counterpart: gap. |
| `zoom` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 has no FOV or zoom event: the pinned index lists none, and `ClientTickEvents` only ticks. Horizon's `Game::native_zoom` is a client replace hook asked by `native_zoom_now` only while playing and not scoping, where a native mod may set `Zoom { factor, smooth, turn }` that Horizon clamps and applies to the view. No Fabric event carries that data or lets a listener replace the FOV: gap. |
| `minimap` | **Gap:** `HudElementRegistry` (partial) | [The HUD element registry][hud-element-registry] registers a named [HUD element][hud-element] drawn by [the HUD mixin][hud-mixin], giving a draw context and returning nothing. Horizon's `Game::native_minimap` is a client replace hook asked by `native_minimap_now` where the first native mod may set a `Minimap` (size, blocks per pixel and the rest), which Horizon clamps and draws itself. A Fabric mod would draw its own HUD layer, and no event returns a minimap description: gap. |
| `setting_changed` | **Gap:** none; nearest NeoForge `ModConfigEvent.Reloading` | Fabric API 26.3 has no config or settings-screen event: the pinned index lists none. Horizon's `Game::native_setting_changed` is a client listen hook fired by `native_setting_event` with the mod id and the key of a setting (`Registrar::register_setting`) the player changed on the mod's page in Options > Mods, after the new value is kept. |
| `panel_event` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 has no event for a mod's own in-game browser or HUD panel: `ScreenEvents` only covers `Screen` instances and `HudElementRegistry` only draws HUD layers, neither delivering browser events. Horizon's `Game::native_panel_event` is a client listen hook fired by `panel_event` with the owning mod id and a `PanelEvent` from Horizon's panel. Nothing in Fabric dispatches that: gap. |
| `screen` | **Gap:** `ScreenEvents.AFTER_INIT` (partial) | [The screen events][screen-events] line 99 and [their mixin][screen-mixin] line 195 only notify after a vanilla `Screen` exists; Fabric has no event that supplies a screen for a block's menu. Horizon's `Game::native_screen` is a both-sided replace hook keyed on the block, asked by `open_native_screen` and `native_hidden_slots` to return a `Screen` layout whose hidden slots the server also honours. Firing point, side, arguments and out all differ: gap. |
| `screen_draw` | **Gap:** `ScreenEvents.afterExtract` (partial) | [The screen events][screen-events] line 156 declare `afterExtract`, a per-screen event taking the screen, `GuiGraphicsExtractor`, mouse coordinates and tick progress (listener at line 216); [the GUI mixin][screen-gui-mixin] lines 87-91 fires it after `Screen.extractRenderStateWithTooltipAndSubtitles` returns in `Gui.extractRenderState`. Horizon's `Game::native_screen_draw` is a client listen hook asked by `draw_native_screen` with the block id, the menu's data slots, a `HudBatch` and the origin in GUI pixels, to draw a mod container's screen. No Fabric event supplies block, data, batch or origin: gap. |
| `screen_click` | **Gap:** `ScreenMouseEvents.allowMouseClick` / `afterMouseClick` (partial) | [The screen mouse events][screen-mouse-events] lines 43-70 declare per-screen click events taking a `Screen` and a `MouseButtonEvent`, `allowMouseClick` returning a veto and `afterMouseClick` also receiving the consumed flag and returning whether to consume the click; [the mouse handler mixin][screen-mouse-mixin] lines 32-49 invokes the veto before `Screen.mouseClicked` and ORs the after-callback result with vanilla's result. Horizon's `Game::native_screen_click` is a client replace hook asked by `native_screen_press` with the block id, the data slots, the cursor in GUI pixels and left/right, where a mod may set a menu button id that Horizon sends as `ContainerButtonClick`. No screen object, and the out is button data rather than a veto: gap. |
| `screen_tooltip` | **Gap:** `ItemTooltipCallback.EVENT` (partial) | [The item tooltip API][item-tooltip-api] line 33 declares the item-stack tooltip event, fired by [its mixin][item-tooltip-mixin] line 40 at `ItemStack.getTooltipLines` `RETURN` ordinal 1; [the client tooltip component callback][client-tooltip-component-api] line 37 only maps a `TooltipComponent` to a client component. Horizon's `Game::native_screen_tooltip` is a client collect hook asked by `draw_native_screen_tooltip` with the block id, data slots and cursor position when no item is under the cursor, appending plain lines that Horizon draws. The stack argument and the firing point differ: gap. |
| `menu_button` | **Gap:** none; nearest `ItemClickBehaviorCallback.EVENT` (different moment) | [The item click callback][item-click-api] is fired by [its container menu mixin][item-click-mixin] at `AbstractContainerMenu.tryItemClickBehaviourOverride` `HEAD` for a slot click, with the hovered stack, slot, carried stack, slot access, click action and player. Horizon's `Game::native_menu_button` is a server listen hook asked by `api_menu_button` from the container's `clickMenuButton` path with a `MenuButtonContext`, a mutable block entity (out of the world) and the clicking player's carried stack, then writing the block entity snapshot back. No Fabric event fires at that moment or allows those mutations: gap. |

## Event counterparts: base menus, mobs and player gear (#1014)

Fabric evidence is at `fabric-26.3/` pinned `2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Horizon call
sites are named by function. Scope: the ten `base` hooks `menu_clicked`, `menu_closed`,
`player_died`, `mob_spawned`, `mob_killed`, `item_picked_up`, `hurt_enemy`, `inventory_tick`,
`on_equip` and `block_signal`. All ten record a gap; no keys are
added.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `menu_clicked` | **Gap:** `ItemClickBehaviorCallback.EVENT` (different menu and arguments) | [The item click callback][item-click-api] line 45 is fired by [the container menu mixin][item-click-mixin] line 39 at `AbstractContainerMenu.tryItemClickBehaviourOverride` `HEAD` for a slot click in a vanilla menu, handing the hovered stack, slot, carried stack, slot access, click action and player. Horizon's `Game::native_menu_clicked` is fired by `run_native_menu_events` for a click in a menu a mod opened through `native_open_menu`, with the slot, button, `ClickType` id and player UUID, and no stack or carried stack. The moment is Horizon's native menu rather than a vanilla `AbstractContainerMenu`, and the arguments differ: gap. |
| `menu_closed` | **Gap:** `ScreenEvents.remove` (client screens) | [The screen events][screen-events] lines 106-115 expose a per-screen callback after client `Screen.removed()`; Fabric declares no server-side container-close event and the pinned index lists none. Horizon's `Game::native_menu_closed` is fired by `run_native_menu_events` when a menu a mod opened closes (the player, the server, another menu, or leaving), with the player UUID and window id. Side and arguments differ: gap. |
| `player_died` | **Gap:** `ServerLivingEntityEvents.AFTER_DEATH` (different moment and arguments) | [The living entity events][living-api] lines 85-88 declare `AFTER_DEATH`, fired by [the living mixin][living-mixin] lines 66-74 at the `broadcastEntityEvent` call inside `LivingEntity.die` and by [the server player mixin][loot-player-death-mixin] lines 72-80 at `ServerPlayer.die` `TAIL`. Horizon's `Game::native_player_died` is fired by `guest_die` during death handling, after the death game event and kill-credit lookup but before later death processing, with the player UUID and the death message key (or none). Firing point and arguments (`DamageSource` and living entity versus UUID and message key) differ, and `ALLOW_DEATH` is a fatal-damage veto at `hurtServer`: gap. |
| `mob_spawned` | **Gap:** `ServerEntityEvents.ENTITY_LOAD` / `ALLOW_LOAD` (different coverage) | [The lifecycle API][sentity-api] lines 43-60 offers non-cancelling `ENTITY_LOAD` after insertion, fired by [the entity callbacks mixin][sentity-callbacks-mixin] lines 38-40 at tracking start, and veto-only `ALLOW_LOAD` fired by [the entity manager mixin][misc-add-entity-manager] lines 34-40 at `addEntity` `HEAD`; both also run for entities loaded with a chunk. Horizon's `Game::native_mob_spawned` is fired by `native_mobs_joined` once per mob the world actually spawned, with the dimension, kind, position and the `natural` flag, and stays silent for chunk loads, hive exits and level changes. Coverage and arguments differ: gap. |
| `mob_killed` | **Gap:** `ServerEntityCombatEvents.AFTER_KILLED_OTHER_ENTITY` (different coverage and arguments) | [The combat events][scombat-api] lines 31-36 declare `AFTER_KILLED_OTHER_ENTITY`, fired after `Entity.killedEntity` in [the living mixin][living-mixin] lines 60-68 and, for a player victim, in [the server player mixin][loot-player-death-mixin] lines 66-74, with the level, killer entity, killed entity and damage source. Horizon's `Game::native_mob_killed` is fired by `native_kill_event` only for a player's melee blow that killed a mob, with the player UUID, entity id and kind, and never for other deaths. The arguments are ids instead of objects, and Horizon's coverage is narrower: gap. |
| `item_picked_up` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no item pickup or `ItemEntity.playerTouch` event: the pinned index has no pickup entry, and NeoForge's `ItemEntityPickupEvent.Pre` is not Fabric. Horizon's `Game::native_item_picked_up` is asked by `native_pickup_answer` while a player touches a ground stack, before its pickup delay and target are checked, with the player UUID, the item entity's handle and a mutable `TriState` that leaves the stack in place or forces the pickup. No Fabric event at that moment or with that contract: gap. |
| `hurt_enemy` | **Gap:** `ServerLivingEntityEvents.AFTER_DAMAGE` / `ALLOW_DAMAGE` (different moment and arguments) | [The living entity events][living-api] lines 46-56 declare `AFTER_DAMAGE`, fired at `LivingEntity.hurtServer` `TAIL` by [the living mixin][living-mixin] lines 84-92 only when the entity survives, with the entity, source and damage amounts; `ALLOW_DAMAGE` (lines 31-36) is a veto before mitigation. Horizon's `Game::native_hurt_enemy` is asked by `native_hurt_enemy_event` where `Item.hurtEnemy` is called, before post-attack enchantments and weapon wear, with the acting player's stack, the mob entity id and kind. The stack argument, the attack-side `Item.hurtEnemy` point and the killing-blow case have no Fabric counterpart: gap. |
| `inventory_tick` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no per-stack inventory tick event: `ServerTickEvents` ticks the server or level, not each carried stack, and the pinned index has no `inventoryTick` entry. Horizon's `Game::native_inventory_tick` is fired by `native_slot_tick` for every non-empty carried stack each tick, to the `ItemPart`s of the stack's item, with the saved slot index and whether it is selected, and lets the part read and change the stack in place. Firing point, per-stack arguments and mutation contract have no Fabric counterpart: gap. |
| `on_equip` | **Gap:** `ServerEntityEvents.EQUIPMENT_CHANGE` (different moment and arguments) | [The lifecycle API][sentity-api] lines 73-79 declares `EQUIPMENT_CHANGE`, fired by [the lifecycle living mixin][equipment-mixin] lines 32-40 while `LivingEntity.collectEquipmentChanges` runs in `tick`, comparing `ItemStack.matches`, and also on join; it reports previous and current stacks per `EquipmentSlot` for any living entity. Horizon's `Game::native_on_equip` is fired by `player_equipped` at `LivingEntity.onEquipItem`, before the equip sound, for players only and never a spectator, with a slot name and the new stack (or none). Firing point, guard and arguments differ: gap. |
| `block_signal` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no event for `BlockBehaviour.getSignal` or `getDirectSignal`; the pinned index has no block-signal entry. Horizon's `GameLevel::native_block_signal` is asked through `GameLevel::adjust_signal` inside the authoritative level update, with the dimension, position, state, face, the `direct` flag and a mutable `signal` that may override vanilla's answer. No Fabric event at that query or with that override contract: gap. |

The change is docs only: no dispatch, tick-time work, adapter, alias or gameplay
change.

## Event counterparts: base-is block signals, entity interaction, effects and rendering (#1014)

Fabric evidence is at `fabric-26.3/` pinned `2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Horizon call
sites are named by function. Scope: the twelve `base` hooks `is_signal_source`, `health_hud`,
`entity_interact`, `player_tick_pre`, `player_tick_post`, `living_incoming_damage`,
`should_apply_effect_tick_this_tick`, `apply_effect_tick`, `on_effect_added`, `mob_effect_added`,
`world_drawn` and `render_needs`. All twelve record a gap; no keys are added.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `is_signal_source` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no event for `BlockBehaviour.isSignalSource`; the pinned index has no signal-source or block-query entry. Horizon's `GameLevel::native_is_signal_source` is asked through `adjust_signal_source` only on the authoritative side, with the block state id and a mutable `source` that may override vanilla's answer. No Fabric event fires at that query or offers that override: gap. |
| `health_hud` | **Gap:** `HudElementRegistry.replaceElement` (partial) | [The HUD element registry][hud-element-registry] line 152 replaces one identified HUD element with a listener-supplied [HUD element][hud-element] that draws into the HUD context, and lines 145-147 note that the vanilla status bars (`HEALTH_BAR`, `ARMOR_BAR`, `FOOD_BAR`) are separate elements. Horizon's `Game::native_health_hud` is asked once by `hud_layers` for every health row together (armour, hearts, food and air), handing `HealthInputs` (health, food, saturation, air, vehicle and effect state) and the GUI size, and replacing them all when it sets `draws`. One Horizon call covers several Fabric elements and takes data Fabric does not pass: gap. |
| `entity_interact` | **Gap:** `UseEntityCallback.EVENT` (different guards and arguments) | [The use entity callback][use-entity-api] lines 29-45 is hooked in before the spectator check and returns an `InteractionResult` (on the server any non-`PASS` result cancels further processing); [the server packet mixin][use-entity-server-mixin] lines 83-92 fires it in `handleInteract` before `getItemInHand`, handing the player, level, hand, `Entity` and `EntityHitResult`. Horizon's `Game::native_entity_interact` is fired by `interact_entity` where `Player.interactOn` starts, past the spectator guard, once per hand, with the entity id, type id, held stack and a `claimed` flag that also swings the hand. The guard differs and the entity arrives as ids instead of an object: gap. |
| `player_tick_pre`, `player_tick_post` | **Gap:** none; nearest `ServerTickEvents.END_SERVER_TICK` and `ClientTickEvents.END_CLIENT_TICK` | Fabric API 26.3 ticks the server, the client and each level, never an individual player, and the pinned index has no player-tick entry. Horizon's `Game::native_player_tick_pre` and `Game::native_player_tick_post` are fired by `tick_guests` and `tick_remote_player` around the player's body tick on both logical sides, including death-screen ticks, with the ticked `PlayerRef`. No Fabric event fires at either moment or carries that argument: gap. |
| `living_incoming_damage` | **Gap:** `ServerLivingEntityEvents.ALLOW_DAMAGE` / `AFTER_DAMAGE` (veto-only and read-only) | [The living entity events][living-api] lines 31-36 declare `ALLOW_DAMAGE`, a veto fired by [the living mixin][living-mixin] lines 76-84 inside `hurtServer` before mitigation with only the entity, source and a `float` amount; `AFTER_DAMAGE` (lines 46-56) fires at `TAIL` only when the entity survives and reports the amounts read-only. Horizon's `HitLevel::native_living_incoming_damage` is fired by `incoming_damage` before the hit is applied with the victim's id and kind, the source, the direct entity's weapon, a mutable damage container (new damage and invulnerability ticks) and `canceled`. Fabric offers no mutable damage, weapon or invulnerability ticks: gap. |
| `should_apply_effect_tick_this_tick` | **Gap:** none | Fabric's [effect events][effect-api] cover add and remove only; the pinned index has no `shouldApplyEffectTickThisTick` entry. Horizon's `Game::native_should_apply_effect_tick_this_tick` is asked by `mod_effect_tick` inside `MobEffectInstance.tickServer` before the countdown, with the effect key, ticks left and amplifier, and its `apply` decides whether `native_apply_effect_tick` follows. No Fabric event fires at that moment: gap. |
| `apply_effect_tick` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no event for `MobEffect.applyEffectTick`; the [effect events][effect-api] have add and remove only. Horizon's `Game::native_apply_effect_tick` is fired by `mod_effect_tick` when the effect's tick applies, with the effect key, dimension, entity id, amplifier, holder and health/food, and clearing `keep` ends the effect before the countdown. No Fabric event carries that moment or those arguments: gap. |
| `on_effect_added` | **Gap:** `ServerMobEffectEvents.AFTER_ADD` (different arguments) | [The effect events][effect-api] line 86 declare `AFTER_ADD`, fired by [the effect mixin][effect-mixin] lines 98-106 at `LivingEntity.onEffectAdded` `RETURN` on the server only, handing the `MobEffectInstance`, the `LivingEntity` and an `EffectEventContext`. Horizon's `Game::native_on_effect_added` is fired by `mod_effect_added_resolved` when a content mod's effect is newly added, with the effect key, the entity id (none for a player) and the amplifier. Horizon passes ids instead of the instance and entity objects, and its content-effect callback does not establish the same post-`LivingEntity.onEffectAdded` return point: gap. |
| `mob_effect_added` | **Gap:** `ServerMobEffectEvents.AFTER_ADD` (new effects only) | `AFTER_ADD` at `LivingEntity.onEffectAdded` `RETURN` ([the effect mixin][effect-mixin] lines 98-106) runs only when vanilla adds an effect the entity did not have. Horizon's `Game::native_mob_effect_added` is fired by the same `mod_effect_added_resolved` for every `addEffect` of a content mod's effect, new or not and changed or not, after `native_on_effect_added`, with the effect key, entity id and amplifier. Fabric has no after-update moment, so coverage differs: gap. |
| `world_drawn` | **Gap:** `LevelRenderEvents.END_MAIN` (different firing point) | [The level render events][level-render-events] lines 160-169 declare `END_MAIN`, fired by [the level renderer mixin][level-renderer-mixin] line 131 at the end of the main pass after terrain, entities, block entities and particles but before clouds, weather, late debug and the fabulous combine. Horizon's `mod_render::render::world_drawn` is called from the client renderer after the whole world pass, weather and the world post pass included, with a `WorldDrawn` giving the drawn world's depth and camera. Weather and post-processing are already drawn at Horizon's moment: gap. |
| `render_needs` | **Gap:** none; nearest `LevelRenderEvents.COLLECT_SUBMITS` | [The level render events][level-render-events] line 71 declare `COLLECT_SUBMITS`, fired by [the level renderer mixin][level-renderer-mixin] line 92 at `LevelRenderer.submitFeatures` `RETURN`, with a `LevelRenderContext` for submitting render work rather than collecting render requirements. Horizon's `mod_render::render::render_needs` is asked by `needs` as a frame is built, with a mutable `RenderNeeds` (entity spans and world depth) that several mods may set. No Fabric event fires at that moment or offers that out: gap. |

## Event counterparts: base block queries, dispense, survival, placement, bone meal, step-on, consume and enchant effects (#1014)

Fabric evidence is at `fabric-26.3/` pinned `2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Horizon call
sites are named by function. Scope: the twelve `base` hooks `has_analog_output`, `analog_output`,
`dispense_item`, `can_survive`, `may_place_on`, `is_valid_bonemeal_target`, `is_bonemeal_success`,
`perform_bonemeal`, `block_step_on`, `consume_effect`, `state_for_placement` and `enchant_effect`. All
twelve record a gap; no keys are added.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `has_analog_output` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no event for `BlockBehaviour.hasAnalogOutputSignal`, and the pinned index has no analog-output or block-query entry. Horizon's `GameLevel::native_has_analog_output` is asked by `adjust_has_analog_output` on the authoritative server with the block state id and a mutable `output` seeded from vanilla's answer. No Fabric event fires at that query or offers that out. No key. |
| `analog_output` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no event for `BlockBehaviour.getAnalogOutputSignal`, and the pinned index has no analog-output or block-query entry. Horizon's `GameLevel::native_analog_output` is fired by `adjust_analog_output` on the authoritative server with the dimension, position, state, the face touching the comparator and a mutable `i32`, which `adjust_analog_output` clamps to 0-15. No Fabric event fires at that query or carries those arguments. No key. |
| `dispense_item` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no event for `DispenserBlock.getDispenseMethod`, and the pinned index has no dispense entry. Horizon's `GameLevel::native_dispense_item` is fired by `dispense_item` in the dispenser block code on the authoritative server with the dimension, position, facing, the held stack and a mutable answer holding the remainder stack and the level event. No Fabric event fires at that choice or lets a listener replace the behaviour. No key. |
| `can_survive` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no event for `BlockBehaviour.canSurvive`, and the pinned index has no survival or block-query entry. Horizon's `GameLevel::native_can_survive` is fired by `before_can_survive` on the authoritative server with the state, position and a mutable `Option<bool>` that may force the answer before the block's own rule runs. No Fabric event fires at that query or offers that override. No key. |
| `may_place_on` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no event for `VegetationBlock.mayPlaceOn`, and the pinned index has no may-place-on entry. Horizon's `GameLevel::native_may_place_on` is fired by `before_may_place_on` on the authoritative server with the plant state, the ground state, the ground position and a mutable `Option<bool>`. NeoForge's `IBlockExtension.canSustainPlant` likewise has no Fabric counterpart. No key. |
| `is_valid_bonemeal_target` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no event for `BonemealableBlock.isValidBonemealTarget`, and the pinned index has no bone-meal entry. Horizon's `GameLevel::native_is_valid_bonemeal_target` is asked by `is_valid_bonemeal_target` on both sides with the state, position and a mutable `Option<bool>` whose `Some(false)` refuses the meal. No Fabric event fires at that question or answers it. No key. |
| `is_bonemeal_success` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no event for `BonemealableBlock.isBonemealSuccess`, and the pinned index has no bone-meal entry. Horizon's `GameLevel::native_is_bonemeal_success` is fired by `is_bonemeal_success` on the authoritative server with the state, position and a mutable `bool`, after the target question. No Fabric event fires at that draw or sets the answer. No key. |
| `perform_bonemeal` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no event for `BonemealableBlock.performBonemeal`, and the pinned index has no bone-meal entry. Horizon's `GameLevel::native_perform_bonemeal` is fired by `perform_bonemeal` on the authoritative server with the ticks, dimension, position and state, as a listen hook that changes the level itself. No Fabric event fires at that growth. No key. |
| `block_step_on` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no event for `Block.stepOn`, and the pinned index has no step entry. Horizon's `Game::native_block_step_on` is called from the block-contact `step_on` callback in `BLOCK_CONTACT_HOOKS` as the simulation reports a contact, with the dimension, the entity id, its kind id, the state and the position. Those are ids rather than an entity object, and no Fabric event fires at that contact. No key. |
| `consume_effect` | **Gap:** `ServerMobEffectEvents.AFTER_ADD` (different moment and data) | [The effect events][effect-api] lines 79-86 and 136-143 declare `AFTER_ADD` and `AFTER_REMOVE`, fired by [their mixin][effect-mixin] lines 95-106 and 209-221 at `LivingEntity.onEffectAdded` and `onEffectsRemoved` `RETURN` on the server with a `MobEffectInstance` and a `LivingEntity`. Horizon's `Game::native_consume_effect` is fired by `apply_consume_effect` when a consumable names a `ConsumeEffect` of a mod's own kind, handing the kind and its JSON while the acting player is the one consuming. No Fabric event fires at `ConsumeEffect.apply` or passes that data. No key. |
| `state_for_placement` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no event for `Block.getStateForPlacement`, and the nearest [use-block event][use-block-api] lines 28-55 receives the player, level, hand and hit result, not a `BlockPlaceContext`; [its server mixin][spgm-mixin] lines 85-87 fires at `useItemOn` `HEAD` before placement. Horizon's `GameLevel::native_state_for_placement` is asked by `before_state_for_placement` on the authoritative server with the block's default state, the placement context, the fluid at the target position and a mutable `Option<Option<BlockStateId>>` that chooses a state or refuses the placement. No Fabric event fires at that query or returns a state. No key. |
| `enchant_effect` | **Gap:** none | The pinned index has no hit-effect or post-attack entry: [the enchantment events][base-has-enchantment-api] lines 54, 84 and 107 cover enchantment eligibility and definition modification (`ALLOW_ENCHANTING`, `MODIFY`, `MODIFY_WITH_LOOKUP`), not the effects a carried enchantment applies on a hit, and [the effect events][effect-api] lines 79-86 and 136-143 report status-effect addition and removal. Horizon's `Game::native_enchant_effect` is fired by `post_attack` in the enchantment-effect code once for each `minecraft:post_attack` entry of a mod's own kind, with the enchantment id, the kind, the effect JSON, the level and the victim's id. No Fabric event fires at that moment or carries those arguments. No key. |

## Event counterparts: screen mouse input, camera shake, shader parameters, block outline, hand render and item-use queries (#1014)

Fabric evidence is at `fabric-26.3/` pinned `2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Horizon call
sites are named by function. Scope: the twelve `base` hooks `screen_mouse_button_pressed_pre`,
`screen_mouse_button_released_pre`, `camera_shake`, `shader_params`, `block_highlight`, `render_hand`,
`use_duration`, `use_animation`, `can_eat`, `living_entity_use_item_start`, `living_entity_use_item_finish`
and `screen_mouse_scrolled_pre`. All twelve record a gap; no keys are added.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `screen_mouse_button_pressed_pre` | **Gap:** `ScreenMouseEvents.allowMouseClick` / `beforeMouseClick` (one moment split in two) | [The screen mouse events][screen-mouse-events] lines 48-63 declare a boolean `allowMouseClick` veto and a separate void `beforeMouseClick` notification; [the mouse handler mixin][screen-mouse-mixin] lines 32-53 invokes both around `Screen.mouseClicked` inside `MouseHandler.onButton`, after the handler has routed the press to a real `Screen`. Horizon's `native_screen_mouse_button_pressed_pre` is fired by `handle_window_event_inner` straight from `WindowEvent::MouseInput`, before that routing, with the screen, the GLFW button, the modifier bits and a mutable `canceled`, and it deliberately skips connect, loading, dialog and overlay screens that Fabric still sees as `Screen`s. Fabric's allow callback can veto on its own, but the different routing and screen coverage prevent a match; the `MouseButtonEvent` signature does not prove that modifier data is absent: gap. No key. |
| `screen_mouse_button_released_pre` | **Gap:** `ScreenMouseEvents.allowMouseRelease` / `beforeMouseRelease` (one moment split in two) | [The screen mouse events][screen-mouse-events] lines 81-96 declare a boolean `allowMouseRelease` veto and a separate void `beforeMouseRelease`; [the mouse handler mixin][screen-mouse-mixin] lines 55-73 invokes them around `Screen.mouseReleased`, again only after the release has been routed to a `Screen`. Horizon's `native_screen_mouse_button_released_pre` is fired by `handle_window_event_inner` from the same window-event point as the press, with the screen, button, modifier bits and a mutable `canceled` that stops the release before any slot, drag or slider sees it. Fabric's allow callback can veto on its own; the different routing point and screen coverage are the proven mismatch: gap. No key. |
| `camera_shake` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 has no camera-angle or viewport event: the pinned index lists none, and NeoForge's `ViewportEvent.ComputeCameraAngles` has no counterpart. Horizon's `native_camera_shake` is called by `native_shake_matrix` once a frame while playing, with the partial tick and a mutable `Shake` whose pitch, yaw, roll and offset Horizon bounds and applies to the camera pose. No Fabric event fires at that pose computation or carries that mutable shake: gap. No key. |
| `shader_params` | **Gap:** none | Horizon's shader packs and their `frame.mods` slots are its own; the pinned index has no shader, pipeline or parameter event. Horizon's `native_shader_params` is asked by `native_shader_params_now` once a frame while a pack is on, with the partial tick and eight mutable `vec4` slots that Horizon sanitises. Fabric offers no event at that moment or with that data: gap. No key. |
| `block_highlight` | **Gap:** `LevelExtractionEvents.AFTER_BLOCK_OUTLINE_EXTRACTION` / `LevelRenderEvents.BEFORE_BLOCK_OUTLINE` (split and after the extraction moment) | [The extraction events][level-extraction-events] lines 46-57 declare a void callback that runs at `LevelExtractor.extractBlockOutline` `RETURN` ([the extractor mixin][level-extractor-mixin] lines 64-66) and can suppress default rendering by nulling the already-extracted state; [the render events][level-render-events] lines 97-114 declare a separate boolean veto that [the level renderer mixin][level-renderer-mixin] lines 110-113 invokes inside `submitBlockOutline`. Horizon's `native_block_highlight` is fired by `render_with` from the picked hit before the outline geometry is built, after the HUD and game-mode gates, with the position, block state and partial tick, and `canceled` skips only the default outline. Both Fabric events run later in the pipeline, take a `LevelExtractionContext`/`LevelRenderContext` and hit or outline state rather than the block state and partial tick, and split extraction from rendering: gap. No key. |
| `render_hand` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no first-person hand-render event; the pinned index lists no hand entry, and `HudElementRegistry` only adds HUD layers rather than replacing hand geometry. Horizon's `native_render_hand` is called by `hand_geometry` through `render_hand_cancelled` before one hand's vertices are built, with a `HandRender` (player, hand, stack, pose data, light), a draw context in the hands' view space and a mutable `canceled` that skips the default geometry. No Fabric event fires at that moment, exposes that render data or takes geometry for the hand: gap. No key. |
| `use_duration` | **Gap:** `ItemEvents.USE` / `UseItemCallback.EVENT` (nearest, different moment and data) | [The item events][item-events] line 56 declare `USE`, fired by [the item stack mixin][item-stack-mixin] line 38 as a wrap of `Item.use`, with the level, player and hand (listener signature at lines 85-98); [the use item callback][use-item-api] lines 37-52 declares a use attempt with the player, level and hand. Neither listener receives a stack argument. Horizon's `native_use_duration` is asked by `use_duration` on the result of vanilla's `Item.getUseDuration` on both sides, with the stack and a mutable tick count, and has no player because it is asked repeatedly. Neither Fabric event runs at that query or offers a mutable duration: gap. No key. |
| `use_animation` | **Gap:** `ItemEvents.USE` / `UseItemCallback.EVENT` (nearest, different moment and data) | [The item events][item-events] line 56 and [the use item callback][use-item-api] line 37 cover starting a use with a player, level and hand (neither listener has a stack argument), not the item's animation choice. Horizon's `native_use_animation` is asked by `item_use_state` on the client with the stack and a mutable `UseAnimation`, after vanilla's `Item.getUseAnimation` answer, and Horizon refuses a spear pose without kinetic data. No Fabric event fires at that query or carries the animation: gap. No key. |
| `can_eat` | **Gap:** `ItemEvents.USE` / `UseItemCallback.EVENT` (nearest, different moment and data) | [The item events][item-events] line 56 and [the use item callback][use-item-api] line 37 concern beginning a use with player, level and hand arguments, and neither asks whether the player may eat or receives the food stack directly. Horizon's `native_can_eat` is asked by `can_eat` on both sides from `Player.canEat`'s answer (creative, `can_always_eat` or hunger), with the food stack and a mutable boolean. No Fabric event fires at that question or exposes the answer: gap. No key. |
| `living_entity_use_item_start` | **Gap:** `UseItemCallback.EVENT` / `ItemEvents.USE` (nearest, different moment and data) | [The use item callback][use-item-api] lines 28-52 and [the item events][item-events] line 56 cover an attempted `use` by a player with the level, player and hand; neither listener receives a stack argument. Horizon's `native_living_entity_use_item_start` is fired by `start_using_item` and `start_mob_using_item` at `LivingEntity.startUsingItem`, on both sides, with the stack, an entity id (or `None` for a player), the hand, a mutable duration and a mutable `canceled`; a mob fires it through `LivingRef::start_using_item`. The Fabric events precede `Item.use`, carry no duration and no entity id, and cannot change the duration: gap. No key. |
| `living_entity_use_item_finish` | **Gap:** `ItemEvents.USE` / `UseItemCallback.EVENT` (nearest, different moment and data) | [The item events][item-events] line 56 and [the use item callback][use-item-api] line 37 receive level, player and hand rather than a stack or mutable result, and fire when a use is attempted, not when `LivingEntity.completeUsingItem` runs its time out. Horizon's `native_living_entity_use_item_finish` is fired by `use_item_finished` on the server for every player of the world and by `remote_complete_using` on a server's client, with the stack, entity id, hand, the ticks left and a mutable result stack that may replace what stays in the hand. No Fabric event fires at completion or offers that result: gap. No key. |
| `screen_mouse_scrolled_pre` | **Gap:** `ScreenMouseEvents.allowMouseScroll` / `beforeMouseScroll` (one moment split in two) | [The screen mouse events][screen-mouse-events] lines 155-171 declare a boolean `allowMouseScroll` veto and a separate void `beforeMouseScroll`; [the mouse handler mixin][screen-mouse-mixin] lines 101-122 invokes them around `Screen.mouseScrolled` with `mouseX`, `mouseY`, the horizontal and vertical amounts, but no modifier bits. Horizon's `native_screen_mouse_scrolled_pre` is fired by `handle_window_event_inner` on the raw window wheel before dispatch, with the screen, the scaled scroll amounts, the modifier bits and a mutable `canceled`, and it skips the same non-game screens as the button hooks. The allow callback alone provides a veto, but no modifier argument and a different routing point and screen coverage prevent a match: gap. No key. |

The change is docs only: no dispatch, tick-time work, adapter, alias or gameplay
change.

## Event counterparts: base screen input, camera, fog and respawn hooks (#1014)

Fabric evidence is at `fabric-26.3/` pinned `2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Horizon call
sites are named by function. Scope: the twelve `base` hooks `screen_key_pressed_pre`,
`screen_char_typed_pre`, `biome_colors`, `compute_fov`, `render_living_pre`, `camera_distance`,
`compute_fog_color`, `mouse_button_pre`, `mouse_button_post`, `player_respawn_position`,
`player_respawned` and `render_fog`. All twelve record a gap; no keys are added.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `screen_key_pressed_pre` | **Gap:** `ScreenKeyboardEvents.allowKeyPress` (per-screen call site, not the window event) | [The screen keyboard events][screen-keyboard-events] lines 44-49 declare one per-screen boolean allow event; [the keyboard handler mixin][screen-keyboard-mixin] lines 33-54 wrap `Screen.keyPressed(KeyEvent)` inside `KeyboardHandler.keyPress` and veto at lines 38-42. Horizon's `native_screen_key_pressed_pre` is fired by `screen_key_pressed` from the window event and the control socket's `key`, before dispatch, with a `ScreenView`, the key's name, the repeat flag and modifiers, excluding connect, loading, dialog and overlay screens. Fabric sees every `Screen`, has no repeat argument, and receives a `KeyEvent` keycode/scancode/modifiers rather than a name: gap. No key. |
| `screen_char_typed_pre` | **Gap:** `ScreenKeyboardEvents.allowCharType` (screen coverage and unproven argument parity) | [The screen keyboard events][screen-keyboard-events] lines 110-115 declare the per-screen boolean allow event; [the keyboard handler mixin][screen-keyboard-mixin] lines 79-100 wrap `Screen.charTyped(CharacterEvent)` and veto at lines 84-88. Horizon's `native_screen_char_typed_pre` is fired per printable character by `screen_text_typed` and `screen_chars_typed` and by the control socket's `type`, with a screen view, the character, modifiers and `canceled`, excluding connect, loading, dialog and overlay screens as documented for the key hook. Fabric fires wherever `Screen.charTyped` is called; its listener takes a `CharacterEvent` (lines 221-229), documented here only as containing a codepoint, so parity with Horizon's modifier payload is not proven. Screen coverage differs and argument parity is unproven: gap. No key. |
| `biome_colors` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 has no biome-colour or tint event: the pinned index lists none, and `ColorProviderRegistry` only registers block and item colours, not a biome's grass, foliage, dry foliage and water tints. Horizon's `native_biome_colors` is asked by `refresh_biome_colors` for each biome with a mutable `BiomeColors`. No Fabric event fires at that query or carries those values: gap. No key. |
| `compute_fov` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no field-of-view, viewport or projection event; the pinned index lists none. Horizon's `native_compute_fov` is fired by `fov_degrees` after vanilla's calculation and zoom and before projection and nausea, with the partial tick, camera and mutable degrees. No Fabric event fires at that moment or offers the angle: gap. No key. |
| `render_living_pre` | **Gap:** `LevelRenderEvents.COLLECT_SUBMITS` (later, level-wide, cannot replace one entity) | [The level render events][level-render-events] lines 64-71 declare a level-wide submit collection after entity submits are gathered; [the level renderer mixin][level-renderer-mixin] lines 86-94 fire it at `submitFeatures` RETURN with a `LevelRenderContext`. It can add submits, not receive a per-entity `LivingEntityRenderState`, pose stack or light, and cannot leave out one mob's vanilla model; `LivingEntityFeatureRenderEvents.ALLOW_CAPE_RENDER` only vetoes a cape layer. Horizon's `native_render_living_pre` is fired by `mod_render_pre` per mob and player in view with a `MobRender` and a draw context, and `canceled` skips all of the entity's drawing and its `render_living_post` (a player's `render_player_post` still runs when only this hook cancels): gap. No key. |
| `camera_distance` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 has no camera event: the pinned index lists none, and NeoForge's detached-camera distance event has no counterpart. Horizon's `native_camera_distance` is asked by `camera_view` through `align_with_entity_distance` with the partial tick, mirrored flag, camera entity and a mutable distance, before visual collision clipping. No Fabric event fires at that calculation or carries the distance: gap. No key. |
| `compute_fog_color` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no fog event; the pinned index lists no fog colour or distance entry. Horizon's `native_compute_fog_color` is fired by `native_fog_color_now` after ordinary effects and before upload, with the partial tick, camera, fog type and mutable RGB, and title panoramas skip it. No Fabric event fires there or carries the colour: gap. No key. |
| `mouse_button_pre` | **Gap:** `ScreenMouseEvents.allowMouseClick` / `allowMouseRelease` (press and release split, per screen, after routing) | [The screen mouse events][screen-mouse-events] lines 48-63 and 81-96 declare separate per-screen vetoes for a click and a release; [the mouse handler mixin][screen-mouse-mixin] lines 32-53 and 55-73 invoke them around `Screen.mouseClicked` and `Screen.mouseReleased` inside `MouseHandler.onButton`, after the press has been routed to a `Screen`. Horizon's `native_mouse_button_pre` is fired by `handle_window_event_as` for every non-synthetic `WindowEvent::MouseInput`, press or release, with no screen required and before dispatch, with the GLFW button, pressed flag, modifiers and `canceled`. Horizon covers both moments in one global hook and for screens Fabric never sees: gap. No key. |
| `mouse_button_post` | **Gap:** `ScreenMouseEvents.afterMouseClick` / `afterMouseRelease` (per screen, and a consumed claim) | [The screen mouse events][screen-mouse-events] lines 65-70 and 98-103 declare per-screen after-events whose listeners receive the current consumed flag and can additionally consume the event (listener signatures at lines 215-224 and 252-261); [the mouse handler mixin][screen-mouse-mixin] lines 32-53 and 55-73 fire them inside `MouseHandler.onButton` after a non-vetoed `Screen.mouseClicked` or `Screen.mouseReleased` call, even when that call returned false; the callback result is ORed with vanilla's result. Horizon's `native_mouse_button_post` is fired by `handle_window_event_as` after `handle_window_event_inner` for every accepted non-synthetic raw button event, screen or not, as a notice with the button, pressed flag and modifiers. Firing point, subscription, arguments (no screen view, no consumed flag) and contract differ: gap. No key. |
| `player_respawn_position` | **Gap:** `EntitySleepEvents.MODIFY_WAKE_UP_POSITION` / `ServerEntityLevelChangeEvents.AFTER_PLAYER_CHANGE_LEVEL` (nearest, different moments and data) | [The sleep events][sleep-api] lines 201-208 declare a bed-only wake-up redirect taking the sleeping entity, bed position, bed state and a nullable `Vec3` (listener at line 353), fired at `LivingEntity.stopSleeping`'s `findStandUpPosition` ([the living mixin][living-mixin] lines 166-174); [the level change events][level-change-api] line 59 declare a notification fired at `PlayerList.respawn` TAIL ([the player list mixin][sp-mixin] line 42). Neither provides Horizon's mutable respawn level, position, facing and copy decision or its missing-block flag; the level-change notification fires only when the old and new levels differ (mixin lines 41-43). Horizon's `native_player_respawn_position` is called by `respawn_position` with the player handle, original level and position, `missing_respawn_block` and mutable level, position, yaw, pitch and copy flag. Horizon initially places the player and resolves this point after the required chunks load, so it is not a before-placement hook: gap. No key. |
| `player_respawned` | **Gap:** `ServerPlayerEvents.AFTER_RESPAWN` (same tail, different payload and coverage) | [Server player events][sp-api] lines 40-45 declare `AFTER_RESPAWN` with a callback over the old player, the new player and the `alive` flag; [the player list mixin][sp-mixin] lines 33-41 invoke it at `PlayerList.respawn` TAIL with those three values. Horizon's `native_player_respawned` is fired by `guest_respawn` after the player is placed and their state swapped in, with the respawned player's handle and name only, and deliberately not for the End exit. The old player object and the `alive` flag have no counterpart, and that excluded case is one Fabric's event sees: gap. No key. |
| `render_fog` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no fog event; the pinned index lists none. Horizon's `native_render_fog` is fired by `native_fog_now` after the computed colour and ordinary fog calculations, with the partial tick, camera, fog type and a mutable `FogData`, and title panoramas skip it. No Fabric event fires at that moment or carries the distances: gap. No key. |

The change is docs only: no dispatch, tick-time work, adapter, alias or gameplay
change.

## Event counterparts: base screen release, music, containers and item use (#1014)

Fabric evidence is at `fabric-26.3/` pinned `2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Horizon call
sites are named by function. Scope: the twelve `base` hooks `screen_key_released_pre`, `music`,
`music_layers`, `input_key`, `mouse_scrolling`, `screen_mouse_dragged_pre`, `mod_container_viewers`,
`living_knockback`, `experience_orb_can_merge`, `experience_orb_merge`, `menu_slot_rules` and
`living_entity_use_item_tick`. All twelve record a gap; no keys are added.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `screen_key_released_pre` | **Gap:** `ScreenKeyboardEvents.allowKeyRelease` / `beforeKeyRelease` (window release before dispatch, not the per-screen call site) | [The screen keyboard events][screen-keyboard-events] lines 82-93 declare the per-screen veto and notification for a release; [the keyboard handler mixin][screen-keyboard-mixin] lines 56-77 wrap `Screen.keyReleased(KeyEvent)` inside `KeyboardHandler.keyPress` and veto at lines 61-65. Horizon's `native_screen_key_released_pre` is fired by `screen_key_released` for the window's real key release before dispatch, with a `ScreenView`, the key's name and modifiers, and cancels screen handling, excluding connect, loading, dialog and overlay screens. Fabric sees every `Screen`, after the release has been routed, and receives a `KeyEvent` keycode/scancode/modifiers rather than Horizon's name: gap. No key. |
| `music` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 has no music-selection event: the pinned index lists no music, soundtrack or situational-music entry, and `SelectMusicEvent` is NeoForge's. Horizon's `native_music` is fired by `tick_music` once a client tick with mutable `Music` and volume, after `situational_music` chose vanilla's track. No Fabric event fires there or carries the track: gap. No key. |
| `music_layers` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 has no layered-music event or layer collection; the pinned index lists no music entry at all. Horizon's `native_music_layers` is fired by `tick_music` once a client tick to collect `MusicLayer`s separately from vanilla's one track. No Fabric event fires at that collection or carries the layers: gap. No key. |
| `input_key` | **Gap:** `ScreenKeyboardEvents.afterKeyPress` / `afterKeyRelease` (screen-only after-events, not the raw window event) | [The screen keyboard events][screen-keyboard-events] lines 60-104 declare per-screen before/allow/after keyboard events; [the keyboard handler mixin][screen-keyboard-mixin] lines 33-77 invoke them only around `Screen.keyPressed` and `Screen.keyReleased`. Horizon's `native_input_key` is fired by `window_event` for every non-synthetic `WindowEvent::KeyboardInput`, after handling, including repeats and consumed shortcuts, with the physical key's name and a press/repeat/release action. Fabric has no window-level keyboard event, only per-screen notifications with a `KeyEvent`, so it neither fires without a screen nor carries Horizon's action or name: gap. No key. |
| `mouse_scrolling` | **Gap:** `ClientHotbarScrollEvents.ALLOW` (hotbar slot change only) and `ScreenMouseEvents.allowMouseScroll` (per screen) | [The hotbar scroll events][hotbar-scroll-api] line 34 declare a veto over the hotbar slot change, fired at [the interaction mouse handler][hotbar-scroll-mixin] line 45 around `Inventory.setSelectedSlot`, with the inventory, old and new slots and scroll offsets. [The screen mouse events][screen-mouse-events] line 155 declare a per-screen scroll veto fired around `Screen.mouseScrolled` at [the mouse handler mixin][screen-mouse-mixin] lines 101-122. Horizon's `native_mouse_scrolling` is fired by `hotbar_scroll_canceled` after accumulation and before hotbar and spectator handling, with scroll, accumulated steps, the three mouse buttons, the cursor, modifiers and a cancel that skips all gameplay scrolling. Neither Fabric event is at that moment or carries those values: gap. No key. |
| `screen_mouse_dragged_pre` | **Gap:** `ScreenMouseEvents.allowMouseDrag` (per screen, modifier payload not proven) | [The screen mouse events][screen-mouse-events] lines 116 and 277 declare a per-screen drag veto taking a `MouseButtonEvent`, horizontal and vertical drag amounts; [the mouse handler mixin][screen-mouse-mixin] lines 78-99 invoke it around `Screen.mouseDragged` inside `MouseHandler.handleAccumulatedMovement`. Horizon's `native_screen_mouse_dragged_pre` is fired by `screen_mouse_dragged` only for a real held button after pad-synthetic and same-position filtering, with the screen view, GLFW button, the cursor's movement since the previous position, modifiers and a cancel that skips every screen drag handler; it also excludes connect, loading, dialog and overlay screens. Fabric has no explicit modifier argument, the cited event documentation does not establish modifier parity through `MouseButtonEvent`, and its screen coverage differs: gap. No key. |
| `mod_container_viewers` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 has no container-opener counter event: the pinned index lists nothing for `ContainerOpenersCounter`. Horizon's `native_mod_container_viewers` is fired by `tick_mod_container_viewers`, which recounts open menus and container-opening golems every server tick and reports each change of viewer count with the dimension, position, previous and current counts, including players lost without closing. No Fabric event fires at that recount or carries the counts: gap. No key. |
| `living_knockback` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no knockback event; the pinned index lists none, and `LivingKnockBackEvent` is NeoForge's. Horizon's `native_living_knockback` is fired by `knockback` before the victim's knockback resistance and motion change, with the victim, its type, damage source, attacker id and held stacks, and mutable strength and direction. No Fabric event fires at `LivingEntity.knockback` or carries that data: gap. No key. |
| `experience_orb_can_merge` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no experience-orb event; the pinned index lists none. Horizon's `native_experience_orb_can_merge` is fired by `orb_merges` for each candidate pair from `ExperienceOrb.tryMergeToExisting` and the periodic scan, with the dimension, both orb ids and values, and vanilla's merge answer. No Fabric event fires at that query: gap. No key. |
| `experience_orb_merge` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no experience-orb merge event; the pinned index lists none. Horizon's `native_experience_orb_merge` is fired by `orb_merges` after the merge was allowed and before one orb absorbs the other, with the dimension, both orb ids, the incoming value and a cancel. No Fabric event fires at that absorption: gap. No key. |
| `menu_slot_rules` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no slot-placement or stack-limit event; the pinned index lists nothing for `Slot.mayPlace` or `mayPickup`. Horizon's `native_menu_slot_rules` is called by `apply_menu_slot_rules` with the loop context, menu name, backing slot index, stack, and mutable placement and maximum, on both the server and client prediction. No Fabric event fires at that query or carries the menu context: gap. No key. |
| `living_entity_use_item_tick` | **Gap:** none (nearest `UseItemCallback`, a different moment) | [Fabric's item-use callback][use-item-api] lines 27-53 handles an attempted use with player, level and hand and an `InteractionResult`; [the server interaction mixin][interaction-server] lines 96-104 fires it at `ServerPlayerGameMode.useItem` HEAD, not on each tick of `LivingEntity.updatingUsingItem`. The pinned index lists no item-use tick event. Horizon's `native_living_entity_use_item_tick` is fired by `use_item_ticked` every tick of a use with the stack, hand, remaining duration and a cancel. No Fabric event fires at that tick or carries the duration: gap. No key. |
## Event counterparts: base item use, client levels, projectiles, mounts, interaction, maps and data (#1014)

Fabric evidence is at `fabric-26.3/` pinned `2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Horizon call
sites are named by function. Scope: the twelve `base` hooks `on_use_tick`,
`living_entity_use_item_stop`, `release_using`, `client_level_load`, `client_level_unload`,
`hears_projectile_impacts`, `projectile_impact`, `entity_mount`, `interaction_key_mapping_triggered`,
`map_decorations`, `data_map_types` and `living_breathe`. All twelve record a gap; no keys are added.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `on_use_tick` | **Gap:** none (nearest `UseItemCallback`, a different moment) | Fabric API 26.3 has no event for a tick of an item in use: the pinned index lists no `Item.onUseTick` or living-entity use-tick entry. The nearest, [the item-use callback][use-item-api] lines 27-53, is an attempted use resolved by [the server interaction mixin][interaction-server] at `ServerPlayerGameMode.useItem` HEAD (lines 96-104), not on each tick of `LivingEntity.updatingUsingItem`, and it carries a player, level, hand and `InteractionResult` instead of the stack and ticks left. Horizon's `native_on_use_tick` is fired by `item_on_use_tick` every tick of a use with the stack, entity id and remaining ticks. No key. |
| `living_entity_use_item_stop` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 has no living-entity use-stop event: the pinned index lists no use-stop entry, and the same nearest `UseItemCallback` is a one-shot attempted use, not the end of an ongoing use. Horizon's `native_living_entity_use_item_stop` is called by `use_item_stopped` and `release_mob_using_item` when a use is released before its time, with the stack, entity, hand, ticks left and a cancel that skips the item's own release. No Fabric event fires at that point or offers the veto. No key. |
| `release_using` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no item-release event; the pinned index lists none, and `UseItemCallback` covers the attempt, not vanilla's `Item.releaseUsing` return. Horizon's `native_release_using` is fired by `mod_release_using` and `release_mob_using_item` after the use-stop veto, with the stack, entity, ticks left and a mutable `used` that starts the use cooldown. No Fabric event fires after the vanilla release or carries that answer. No key. |
| `client_level_load` | **Gap:** `ClientLevelEvents.AFTER_CLIENT_LEVEL_CHANGE` (after the swap, not when the level is made) | [The client level events][client-level-api] lines 29-46 declare a single after-change notification taking `(Minecraft, ClientLevel)`; [the lifecycle mixin][cl-mixin] lines 55-61 fire it at `updateLevelInEngines` TAIL, once the new level has already been installed in the engines. Horizon's `native_client_level_load` is fired by `enter_remote_dimension` when the new client level is made for a dimension, before `native_client_level_unload` of the old level and before its chunks, and is told the dimension id. The swap is announced after rather than before the old level goes, and Fabric supplies a `ClientLevel` object rather than Horizon's dimension id: both firing point and arguments differ. No key. |
| `client_level_unload` | **Gap:** none (nearest `ClientLevelEvents.AFTER_CLIENT_LEVEL_CHANGE`, a different moment) | Fabric API 26.3 declares no client-level unload or clear notification: `ClientLevelEvents` ends with the after-change event, and the pinned index lists no unload entry. Horizon's `native_client_level_unload` is fired by `leave_server`, `poll_remote` and `enter_remote_dimension` for each level before it is dropped, with the dimension id, once per load. No Fabric event fires at that teardown or carries the level. No key. |
| `hears_projectile_impacts` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 has no projectile event at all, and this hook is Horizon-only: a collect gate asked once before a server tick's projectiles fly, so the tick knows whether to keep a copy of each flying projectile. Horizon's `native_hears_projectile_impacts` is called by `native_hears_impacts`, including the check in `native_trident_impact`, with a mutable `hears`. No Fabric event fires there. No key. |
| `projectile_impact` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no projectile-impact event; the pinned index lists no projectile or impact entry, and NeoForge's `ProjectileImpactEvent` has no counterpart. Horizon's `native_projectile_impact` is fired by `native_projectile_impacts` and `native_trident_impact` once a hit is found and before it acts, with the projectile id and type, owner, target entity or block face and point, dimension, and a cancel that suppresses damage, sound and stick. No Fabric event fires at that decision or carries the hit. No key. |
| `entity_mount` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no mount or dismount event; the pinned index lists none, and `EntityMountEvent` is NeoForge's. Horizon's `native_entity_mount` is called by `native_mount_allowed` before a rider boards or leaves a vehicle, with the entity and vehicle ids and kinds, the dimension, a mounting flag and a cancel, and is gated by `native_hears_mounts`. No Fabric event fires at that point or carries the pair. No key. |
| `interaction_key_mapping_triggered` | **Gap:** `ClientPreAttackCallback.EVENT` (attack-only, before use and cooldown guards) | [The pre-attack callback][base-on-pre-attack-api] lines 25-65 is client-only, takes the client, player and attack click count, and cancels attack handling including the swing. [The interaction client mixin][base-on-pre-attack-mixin] lines 97-136 invokes it in `handleKeybinds` before `LocalPlayer.isUsingItem` and applies its answer to `startAttack` and `continueAttack`. Horizon's `native_interaction_key_mapping_triggered` is called by `native_interaction` for Attack after its guards, Use per hand and PickBlock, with the interaction key, hand, mutable `swing_hand` and a cancel that skips the other use hand while retaining the swing unless separately cleared. Fabric has no matching combined moment, arguments or independent swing choice: no key. |
| `map_decorations` | **Gap:** `ExtractItemDecorationsCallback.EVENT` (item-slot overlay, not map decorations) | [The decoration callback][extract-item-decorations-api] lines 28-34 fires at the end of `GuiGraphicsExtractor.itemDecorations` for an item stack's durability bar, cooldown and count; [the extractor mixin][extract-item-decorations-mixin] lines 33-41 draws it over the stack in a slot. Horizon's `native_map_decorations` is asked by `mod_map_decorations` each time a map is drawn, with a `MapInfo`, collects `MapDecoration`s and draws them in the map's pose, filtered by frame. Fabric's event has no map, no decoration list and no map pose. No key. |
| `data_map_types` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 has no data map type registry or collection event; the pinned index lists none, and `RegisterDataMapTypesEvent` is NeoForge's. Horizon's `native_data_map_types` is called by `build_world_tables` on each table assembly and `/reload` to collect `DataMapType`s that its own loader then reads from the world's data packs. No Fabric event fires at that collection. No key. |
| `living_breathe` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no breathing event; the pinned index lists none, and `LivingBreatheEvent` is NeoForge's. Horizon's `native_living_breathe` is called by `breathe` for each living entity's air update on the server, with the loop context, entity id and type, and mutable `can_breathe`, `consume` and `refill` that change this tick's air. No Fabric event fires there or carries those values. No key. |

The change is docs only: no keys, dispatch, tick-time work, adapter, alias or gameplay
change.

[hotbar-scroll-api]: https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/blob/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222/fabric-events-interaction-v0/src/client/java/net/fabricmc/fabric/api/event/client/player/ClientHotbarScrollEvents.java
[hotbar-scroll-mixin]: https://github.com/FabricMC/fabric/blob/2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222/fabric-events-interaction-v0/src/client/java/net/fabricmc/fabric/mixin/event/interaction/client/MouseHandlerMixin.java

## Event counterparts: block and item declaration, entity ticks, player and mob rendering, signs and menus (#1014)

Fabric evidence is at `fabric-26.3/` pinned `2065040b236a0d5ac011bd6a45625dc49579b222`. Horizon call
sites are named by function. Scope: the twelve `base` hooks `blocks`, `items`, `entity_tick_pre`,
`entity_tick_post`, `entity_tick_listened`, `render_player_pre`, `render_living_post`, `render_player_post`,
`sign_changed`, `standalone_models`, `player_container_open` and `player_container_close`. All twelve
record a gap; no keys are added.

| Horizon hook | Fabric event considered / status | Evidence and porting boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `blocks`, `items` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 has no block or item registration event: a Fabric mod registers into `Registries.BLOCK` and `Registries.ITEM` directly during initialisation, so there is no dispatch moment like NeoForge's `RegisterEvent`, and the pinned index lists no such entry. Horizon fires `native_blocks` and `native_items` once from `native_declared` at startup, handing each a `RegistrationContext` and a `Vec` of its own `Block`/`Item` descriptors, which Fabric never carries. No key. |
| `entity_tick_pre`, `entity_tick_post` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no entity-tick event; the pinned index has no per-entity tick entry (only effect ticking), and NeoForge's `EntityTickEvent` has no counterpart. Horizon's `native_entity_tick_pre` is fired by `native_tick_pre_batch` and `native_tick_pre_one` before an entity ticks on the server with its id, kind and a cancel, and `native_entity_tick_post` by `native_tick_post_batch` and `native_tick_post_one` afterwards; neither moment nor its arguments exist in Fabric. No key. |
| `entity_tick_listened` | **Gap:** none (Horizon-only opt-in gate) | No Fabric event is involved: the hook is asked by `native_tick_round` each tick, once per entity family, so the server can skip keeping hook notification lists when no mod listens, and its `listened` answer gates the two tick hooks above. Fabric API 26.3 has no counterpart to that gate. No key. |
| `render_player_pre` | **Gap:** `LevelRenderEvents.COLLECT_SUBMITS` (whole-level submit collection, not a per-player model draw) | [LevelRenderEvents.java:64-76,194-196][level-render-events] declares `COLLECT_SUBMITS` taking a `LevelRenderContext`, and [LevelRendererMixin.java:90-93][level-renderer-mixin] fires it at `submitFeatures` RETURN after entity, block-entity and particle submits are collected, to add extra submits. It carries no player or entity, no per-player frame, and cannot omit a vanilla player model. Horizon's `native_render_player_pre` is called by `mod_render_pre` per visible player per frame with the player's frame, `who` and a `canceled` that skips all of the player's drawing. No key. |
| `render_living_post`, `render_player_post` | **Gap:** `LevelRenderEvents.COLLECT_SUBMITS` (whole-level submit collection, not a per-entity post-render moment) | The same [level render events][level-render-events] and [level renderer mixin][level-renderer-mixin] as above: one level-wide submit collection with no entity, render state or per-entity pose. [LivingEntityRenderLayerRegistrationCallback.java:52-65][living-render-layer-api] adds `RenderLayer`s while renderers are built, the counterpart of `register_entity_layers`, not of this frame moment. Horizon's `native_render_living_post` and `native_render_player_post` are called by `mod_render_post` for every mob and player in view whose pre hooks weren't canceled (and `render_player_post` also by `mod_render_pre` when only a player's `render_living_pre` cancels), after the vanilla model and layers, with the entity's frame, its posed parts and the mod's own geometry buffer. No key. |
| `sign_changed` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 has no sign-editing event; the pinned index lists none, and NeoForge has no counterpart either. Horizon's `native_sign_changed` is called by `lan_sign_update` on the server once a player's `SignUpdate` has passed vanilla's editing, reach and wax checks, with the dimension, position, side, mutable lines and a cancel. No Fabric event fires at that point or carries the text. No key. |
| `standalone_models` | **Gap:** `BuiltInBlockModelsCallback.EVENT` (built-in block models, not file-backed standalone ids/forms) | [BuiltInBlockModelsCallback.java:26-46][builtin-block-models-api] gives listeners a `BuiltInBlockModels.Builder` and is fired by [BuiltInBlockModelsMixin.java:35-40][builtin-block-models-mixin] before `BuiltInBlockModels.createBlockModels` calls `build`, so mods add constructed `BlockModel.Unbaked` values for block states. Horizon's `native_standalone_models` contract version 2 is asked once by `mods/standalone.rs::collect_standalone_models` for plain model ids and bake-time rotated/UV-locked or combined file dependencies, rebaked beside block models on every resource load, with no block state and no arbitrary mod-typed model object; NeoForge's `ModelEvent.RegisterStandalone` is the event it mirrors. The registration point and data handed over differ. No key. |
| `player_container_open`, `player_container_close` | **Gap:** none | Fabric API 26.3 declares no server-side menu open or close event; the pinned index lists no event at `ServerPlayer.openMenu` or `doCloseContainer`, and NeoForge's `PlayerContainerEvent` has no counterpart. Horizon's `native_player_container_open` and `native_player_container_close` are fired by `player_menu_opened` and `player_menu_closed` with the player's id and name, dimension, menu type name and optional block position. No Fabric event fires at either moment or carries those arguments. No key. |
